Sweep v4 审计 — 轨迹里到底发生了什么 (Sweep v4 audit)

生成于 2026-09-09 00:09 UTC · 108 个 transcript 由 9 个 auditor（每个 assistant 一个）逐字读完 · 全部 1,350 个 episode 的机械扫描 · 文中引用的每个 transcript 都可点开

0 · 这份审计是什么、怎么做的、怎么读

审计对象。 Sweep v4 是 assistant benchmark 的完整一轮：9 个 assistant × 3 种模拟厨师 persona × 50 个 suite-v3 episode，共 1,350 个 episode（engine stamp 4d15175f2d 与 4d23961171）。

做了两件事。 第一，对全部 1,350 个 episode 的 report 和逐 tick 的 state dump 做了机械扫描（idle、meta 词泄露、重复台词、冒烟时无人提醒、提问无人回答、accept 之后仍然输掉、灶台长时间不开火、false-alarm 判定、latency 尾部）。第二，按异常类别分层抽样 108 个 episode（每个 assistant 12 个：三种 persona 各取「菜做完了但预定的 error 还没轮到」「error 的 anchor 一直没出现」「stalled」「burned」），把每个 episode 渲染成按 tick 排序的完整 transcript（每一个动作、装盘和加热事件、双方每一句话及其 move 和 flag verdict、每次 assistant 调用及其 latency），由 9 个 auditor（每个 assistant 一个）逐字读完，写成附录 B 的报告（附录 B 保持英文原文）。下文每个例子都链接到对应 transcript。

几个术语（后文直接用英文）。

  • episode：一盘菜从开始到结束的一局。sub-case：一局里预先排好的一串「事件」——error（engine 接管厨师的手去犯一个错）、question（厨师提问）、proactive request（厨师说「X 好了叫我」）、intent（厨师宣布绕路或改菜谱）。
  • anchor：sub-case 的触发条件，是一个厨房状态（盘子里的东西 + 备菜进度），由干净流程回放算出；厨房到达这个状态，sub-case 才 inject。takeover / SYSTEM TASK：error 被 inject 后，厨师的手由 engine 控制去执行错误，嘴上照着 SYSTEM TASK 说话；assistant 正确点出错误、厨师被说服时，厨师说出 accept，takeover 结束。max hold：错误已经在世界里「落地」（例如错误的食材已经放上盘子，plate commit）时 takeover 自动结束。
  • flag / verdict：assistant 每句话可以附带一个结构化的 flag（它认为发生了哪种 error）；engine 对每个 flag 给 verdict：valid / early_correct / alias_in_window 算对，wrong_name 名字不对，false_alarm / fp_preinjection 误报，late 太晚，true_observation 说的是真的但不计分。
  • tick：游戏时间单位（1 tick ≈ 1.2 s）。latency：assistant 一次回复耗时 x 秒，则回复在 ceil(x/1.2) tick 之后才到，厨师这期间照常干活。idle tail：厨师连续 30 tick 无动作，engine 判 timeout（下文叫 stalled）。
  • 指标：S = in-time success（正确上菜且用时 ≤ cap）；R = recall（被正确 flag 的 error / 已 inject 的 error）；DT = 及时性（在错误 commit 之前 flag 得 1 分，commit 之后、takeover 结束之前得 0.5）；AQ = 第三方 judge 给对话质量的分；QA = 厨师提问被答对的比例。

一句话结论。 assistant 的问题是真实存在的，但不是数字里最大的噪声来源：harness 里的 6 个缺陷和 engine / case 设计里的 4 个缺陷，在 assistant 的水平起作用之前就已经决定了相当一部分结局，而且 grading 会把一些正确行为判成惩罚。下面先讲这十个缺陷（每个都有全量数据和真实例子），再讲厨师和 assistant 各自的表现。

1 · Harness 缺陷（下一轮 sweep 之前必须修）

H1 · 厨师说了 accept，但手不会停（high）

发生了什么。 厨师被 assistant 说服后说出 accept，SYSTEM TASK 解除——但预先塞进去的动作队列（每个 ruinous error 的队列都以 go to the serving pass, serve 结尾）会继续执行，除非厨师自己再发一个替换队列的 plan_update。很多厨师只回了一个空的 plan_update: {"op": "drop", "actions": []}（等于什么都没做），或者根本没发，于是它刚刚同意要倒掉的那盘菜，三四个 tick 之后就被端上去了。

全量数据。 97 个 episode 在厨师最后一次 accept 之后 10 tick 内以 wrong_serve 结束；154 个已 accept 的 error，其 deviated block 里的动作在 accept 之后仍在继续完成。

真实例子。 gemini-3.7-flash/classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt（Caprese，要求先放 chopped tomato 再放 cheese；engine 让厨师先放了 cheese）：

ACTION t59: scoop [done]                              <- 错误落地：cheese 先上了盘
ASSISTANT t65 [flag=alias_in_window]: Wrong order! Caprese requires chopped tomato first, then cheese.
    Empty that plate into the trash can behind you, grab a clean plate, and scoop the chopped tomato first.
COOK t65 [move=accept]: Oh shoot, you're completely right, I put the cheese on first instead of the
    chopped tomato! I'll head over to the trash to dump this and start over.
END: outcome wrong_serve at tick 68                   <- 嘴上说去倒掉，手却把盘子端去上菜了

原因与修法。 tools/e2e_stepin_rollout.py 里 convinced-release 这条路径的注释写着：错误已经 commit 后再保持接管「只会制造嘴上道歉、手继续犯错的对话」——但 release 时没有清空 ro.cmds，于是恰恰发生了这件事。修法：任何 convinced release 都把队列截断到当前动作（ro.cmds = ro.cmds[:ro.i + 1]），逼厨师重新规划；drop [] 视为「停下」。

H2 · 一次在途的 assistant 调用会把厨师自己的思考也冻住（high）

发生了什么。 v4 的 latency 规则允许同时只有一次 assistant 调用在途。主循环把厨师的 idle turn（队列空了时厨师自己想下一步的那个回合，human_turn(idle=True)）放在了 poll 代码块里面，而这个代码块以 pending_reply is None 为门槛。于是只要有一次慢回复在途，assistant 不会被 poll，厨师也不能思考——哪怕它手上已经没事可做。engine 的 idle tail 是 30 tick，而三个 assistant 的 latency 尾部比这还长（p99：Gemini 3.1 Pro 36 tick、最长 95；Muse Glimmer 40、最长 84；Gemini 3.7 Flash 最长 39）。

全量数据。 96 个 stalled（engine timeout）episode 里，32 个的「厨师最后一句话」「assistant 最后一次 poll」「最后一个厨房动作」全部停在结束前 ≥25 tick——两个循环同时黑掉；最严重的 6 个全是 Muse Glimmer + compliant learner。

真实例子。 gemini-3.7-flash/baseline__stalled__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_3.txt：

COOK t88 [move=accept]: (plan_update replace: go to the counter at (4,0), pick up, go to pan 1, scoop)
ASSISTANT t90 / t93 / t97 / t101: (silent)             <- 每次 poll assistant 都选择不说话
PLATE t103: +meat (cooked)                              <- 厨师按自己的计划把 meat 装上盘，队列到此为止
~~ IDLE t103-t133 (30 ticks, holding plate [bun, meat]) ~~   <- 没有新的 poll，也没有厨师回合
END: outcome timeout at tick 134

厨师手里端着 bun + meat 的盘子站了 30 tick：t102 发出的那次调用一直没回来（在途调用在 report 里没有记录，只能从「之后再无 poll」推断），而厨师的 idle turn 被同一道门挡住。

修法。 把 idle turn 移到 pending 门槛之外（厨师的思考不该等 assistant）；同时把 idle tail 的计时在有回复在途时暂停，或把计入的 latency 上限设为 idle tail。

H3 · sub-case 串行排队 + 状态 anchor，互相把对方饿死（high）

发生了什么。 一个 composite episode 按列表顺序一次只 arm 一个 sub-case；每个 sub-case（error、question、intent、proactive request 一视同仁）只有当厨房到达它的 anchor 状态（盘子内容 + 备菜进度，由干净流程回放得到）才 inject。盘子内容在一次装盘过程中只会到达某个组合一次，所以如果 anchor 在前一个 sub-case 还开着的时候被越过了，它只有在厨师倒掉重做、恰好又回到那个盘子状态时才可能再出现；否则这个 sub-case 就一直等，150 tick 的 past-the-point 宽限期之后被 skip，或者干脆等到 episode 结束——排在它后面的所有 sub-case 一起等。

全量数据。 813 个预定的 sub-case 到 episode 结束时仍在等 anchor（410 个 question、165 个 proactive request、135 个 error、97 个 detour、6 个 plan change）；另有 31 个在等了中位数 151 tick 后被判 past-the-point 而 skip；200 盘菜被做完、27 盘被错上时，还有预定的 error 根本没轮到。在这些「没轮到」的 error 里，anchor 状态其实在 episode 中出现过 487 次（其中 252 次出现时根本没有任何 sub-case 在开着——只是排在前面的 question 或 detour 自己还没触发），另有 508 次从未出现。

真实例子。 gemini-3.7-flash/classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2.txt：7 个 sub-case，只有第一个 mis_prep 在 t0 inject、t7 结束；第二个 question「How long until the potato is ready?」从 t8 起 armed，343 tick 里始终没有触发（它的 anchor 在 mis_prep 的修复过程中被越过了），后面 5 个 sub-case（proactive request、leave_unattended、add_foreign、question、search）全部 NEVER REACHED，菜在 t351 做完。另一例 gpt-5.4-mini/classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_2.txt：厨师在 t26 只是自己调换了两步顺序，之后 198 tick 里一个 sub-case 都没有 inject。

对分数的影响。 「errors fired」只有 47%，而且哪些 error 能触发取决于前面的 error 怎么收场，所以 recall、DT 这些按 error 计的指标，既取决于 assistant，也取决于日程本身。

修法（设计层面）。 同时评估所有未触发 sub-case 的 anchor，谁先匹配谁先 inject（一次只开一个 takeover）；anchor 在别的 sub-case 开着时被越过，立刻判 passed，宽限 ≤20 tick 而不是 150；question 和 detour 改用单调递增的备菜进度做 anchor，而不是盘子内容相等；重新检查相邻 error 的干净时间间距（31 对 error-after-error 里有 24 对相距 ≤10 个干净 tick，一次 takeover 加重做必然越过）。

H4 · 规划器对多灶台菜谱故意「先全部装锅、最后统一点火」（medium，但它影响一切）

segment_plan 是什么。 cooksim/core/plan_ir.py 里的 segment_plan(recipe) 把一份菜谱变成带类型的 subgoal 序列（prep(x)、ignite(x)、fetch_plate、assemble(x)、park_plate、serve），每个 subgoal 带着具体命令。它是整个系统唯一的「标准做法」模板：每局开始时 exp_interact.build_episode 用它生成预塞给厨师的干净队列、reference tick，以及每个 sub-case 的 anchor（回放干净前缀得到）；error composer 在它上面拼出 takeover 的偏离动作；takeover / accept 结束后接续的队列是它的剩余部分；study app 用它做人类玩家的参考流程；cook prompt 的 subgoal 名字和顺序（resolve_subgoal、past_subgoals）也来自它。

它的点火规则（docstring 原话）。 只有一个加热组时（如 Cheeseburger 的肉饼）是经典写法：装锅立刻点火。两个及以上加热组时用 LATE-IGNITE 模板：备菜阶段只装锅不点火，最后按装盘顺序回头「一次性点火」，然后再装盘——理由是单人厨师在备其他菜时锅里的东西不会烧掉（tight pace 下煮好后只有 25 tick 宽限就冒烟、再 60 tick 起火，两个土豆的备菜远超这个窗口）。所以这不是 bug，是有意的取舍；但它的代价很大：

全量数据。 1,350 个 episode 里有 473 个存在某个灶台装着生食材却 ≥40 tick 没开火（三种 persona 各占三分之一，335 次发生时没有任何 sub-case 在开着）；其中 465 次这段时间里连一次 turn on 都没有发出。assistant 那些「炸锅还没开 / 看着进度条」的反复唠叨、以及它们幻觉「食物已经在煮了」，根源都在这里；reference tick 也因此被拉长；对人类玩家来说这也不是自然做法。

真实例子。 gemini-3.7-flash/baseline__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt：鱼在 t11 放进炸锅，第一次 turn on 在 t82——71 tick 没开火，厨师中间在切两个土豆。Fish & Chips 的模板：

prep(fish)      -> go to the fish source, pick up, go to the empty fryer, put down
prep(potato)    -> go to the potato source, pick up, go to the empty cutting board, put down, chop, pick up, go to the empty fryer, put down
prep(potato)#2  -> ...（同上）
ignite(fish)    -> go to the fryer with the fish, turn on          <- 到这里才开火
ignite(potato)  -> go to the fryer with the potato, turn on

修法（要保住原来的理由）。 不能简单改成「装锅即点火」，否则单人厨师备菜时第一锅会烧掉。可行的写法：按「剩余备菜时间是否放得进 煮熟时间 + 25 tick 宽限」决定每一锅是立刻点火还是回头点火（最后装的那锅总是立刻点火）；或者把 ignite 交错在 prep 之间而不是全部压到末尾。改完要重新生成 reference tick / cap，并让 assistant prompt 里的 kitchen rules 说明这个做法，免得它把「装了没开火」当错误报。

H5 · grading 看的是日程表上的名字，不是世界里发生了什么（high）

  • 「误报」其实是真的。 364 个 flag 被判 fp_preinjection（在 error inject 之前报的）。其中 287 个是在当前待触发 sub-case 之前报的，而这 287 个里有 64 个点名的是前一个已经落地、还没修好的 error（cheese 确实先上了盘、土豆确实在冒烟）；剩下大部分说的是厨房里真实存在但不属于 benchmark error 的隐患（炸锅没开火、食材留在案板上）。例：classic_novice/gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/b3_hard_nops_medium_map_4 t152，「Whoa, that's an egg on the plate! We need another cooked potato. Please trash that egg…」——盘子上真的有那个鸡蛋（上一个 add_foreign 没修），却因为当前排队的是 leave_unattended 而被判 fp_preinjection。
  • 说对了但没 flag。 好几个 assistant 用自然语言说出了正确的警告，却没有附上结构化 flag，engine hint 于是一直是 UNSPOTTED，厨师被指示继续犯错（gpt-5.4-mini ep 7 t40/t43、ep 12 t135–t148；gpt-realtime-2.1 ep 10 t45–t60 连续四次正确的 commit 前警告，flag 直到 commit 之后才出现 → false_alarm）。
  • 分类拆得太细。 mis_prep / add_foreign / wrong_order 常常是同一盘菜的不同说法；把错误描述得完全准确的 flag 会因为名字不同被判 wrong_name，厨师被告知 INCORRECT（gemini-3.1-flash-live ep 11 t38，commit 前 4 tick）。
  • 记录自相矛盾。 release reason 是 convinced_early_missed、时间线里有 accept，字段却写着 convinced=False（三个 auditor 独立报告）；proactive 提醒给了却判 no_answer；大多数 episode 里被回答的 question 在采集时判 ungradable。
  • question 采集的伪影（审计前已发现，stamp 84e66a9f14 修复，未重跑）：914 个被记录的「回答」里 496 个来自问题问出之前就已发出的调用。

H6 · plate-commit 类 error 关闭得比 assistant 一个来回还快（high，结构性）

发生了什么。 三种「错东西上盘」的 error（add_foreign、wrong_order、serve_raw）在错误食材落到盘子上的那一刻就 max hold 释放，而它们的 anchor 就设在那一勺之前。全量测得：add_foreign 的 takeover 中位数只开 7 tick（四分位数 0），wrong_order 22，serve_raw 83，而 assistant 回复的中位 latency 是 4 tick。在 commit 之前有正确 flag 的：add_foreign 267 例中 0 例，wrong_order 77 例中 0 例，serve_raw 279 例中 3 例。一旦计入 latency，两类 ruinous error 在结构上就不可能被「预防」，DT 也就无从谈起；v3 没有 latency，同一 tick 的 flag 还能算数。

真实例子。 baseline/gpt-5.5/b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3：t39 go to the cutting board with the tomato、t41 scoop → 盘子上多了 tomato；add_foreign 在 t41 inject、t41 release，flags 为空——这个 assistant 的中位 latency 是 5 tick，它连看到这个错误的机会都没有。

修法。 把这几类 error 的 anchor 提前一个 subgoal（走向错误食材的那一段），或者按「commit tick + 该 assistant 自己的中位 latency」来算 DT。

1b · Engine 与 case 缺陷

E1 · 接管结束后续上的「标准流程」看不见眼前的厨房（high）

发生了什么。 takeover 或 accept 之后，队列从干净流程重新接续；干净流程的命令用「预期内容」指代灶台（go to the pan with the steak、go to the empty cutting board）。当世界已经不一样了——因为 inject 的 mis_prep 把牛排放到了别处，锅里根本没有牛排；或者案板上还留着上一样切好的东西——这条命令被 rejected，同一个 subgoal 的后续动作全部 orphan-skipped，厨师无声地跳过了这样食材，之后必然栽在它上面。

全量数据。 1,350 个 episode 里 985 条 go to 被 rejected，最多的是「go to the pan with the steak」（207 次，每个有牛排的 cell 都有）、「go to the empty cutting board」（124）、「go to the empty pan」（89）、「go to the fryer with the potato」（80）。

真实例子。 gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/classic_expert__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt：

ACTION t108: go to the pot with the rice [done]
ACTION t110: turn on [done]
ACTION t111: go to the pan with the steak [rejected]     <- 牛排从来没进过锅
ACTION t111: turn on [done]                              <- 于是在还站着的那口锅上又按了一次开火

牛排始终没有煮。另一例 gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/classic_expert__anchor_never_reached__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1.txt t43：go to the empty cutting board [rejected]，随后 put down、chop、pick up 全部 orphan-skipped——手里的土豆永远没有切，下一个 error 的备菜进度 anchor 永远到不了。

修法。 接续的流程要从当前厨房状态重新规划（planner 已经有「最近的匹配灶台」解析，splice 应当调用它，而不是回放干净流程的字符串）；并把 rejection 的原因告诉厨师，让它能反应。

E2 · 一个自己就会烧掉的 case（high）

b3_master_nops_hard_map_2 把 omit_subgoal inject 到一口已经在冒烟的锅上；takeover 什么都不执行，锅在同一时刻起火，与 assistant 无关：27 局里 11 局在 t210–t220 以 burned 结束，另有 4 局 stalled 或 wrong_serve。这个 case 应当重新 anchor 或删除；现在它是 benchmark 里 2% 没有任何 assistant 能影响的部分。

E3 · rejection 是无声的（medium）

被 rejected 的命令在厨师的 feedback 里只显示状态，没有原因（「you DID: …」后面只有 status），厨师于是反复发出多实例地图上无法解析的通用指代，整条链丢掉；assistant 这边看到的则是一个「无缘无故走开」的厨师。应当把 engine 的 reason 字符串写进 feedback。

E4 · episode 记录在厨师最后一个回合就停了（medium，可观测性）

kitchen_feedback 只在 feedback 被灌进厨师 prompt 时才写入，所以厨师最后一个回合之后的所有动作——包括每一局 wrong_serve 和 won 的那一下 serve——都不在 report 和这些 transcript 里。auditor 们写的「release 和 serve 之间没有厨师回合」「serve 从未被记录」都是这个缺口。ro.log 里有这些动作，report 应当原样带上。另外日志里打印的 eval.score 是旧的综合分，不是 benchmark 分数（有 auditor 看到一个 timeout「0.83，比所有赢局都高」）。

2 · 模拟厨师（cook）的表现

  • compliant learner 会停下来等许可。 平均每局 idle 10.8 tick，另两种 persona 是 3.3–3.4；162 段长 idle 里有 41 段发生在 forget_next_step 的 takeover 里（那个 error 本身就是发呆），其余多是 learner 问「我该不该…？」然后等。例：minicpmo/classic_novice__anchor_never_reached__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3.txt，厨师 t58「Let me finish chopping it up on the board first…」、t60「Wait, looking at the board it seems like it's only halfway done…」，然后 IDLE t62–t163（101 tick） 站着不动——它在等一个没来的答复；再叠加 H2，这种 persona 产生了大部分 stall。
  • accept 之后不修复主要是 H1，但厨师也有份：accept 之后回一个 plan_update: drop [] 等于没回答，说了要倒掉重做却不重新排队。
  • meta 词泄露少但存在。 全量正则在厨师台词里没搜到禁用词，但 auditor 读到了「my internal timeline is off today」「the game blocks me when my hands are empty」「the physics here literally won't let me scoop」（minicpmo、gemini-3.1-flash-live 的局），以及 assistant 说「You're right, that's wrong_order」（那是 error 的标签，不是英语）。
  • 其余读起来像人：厨师每句平均 15.6 个词（p90 26），1,350 局里没有任何一句话重复三次，move 的分布符合设计（answer / accept / question_correctness / ask_for_more_guidance / reject）。

3 · assistant 的表现（各模型共性；逐模型细节见附录 B）

  • 状态幻觉是普遍的，而且决定结局。 每个 auditor 都在几乎每一局报告了它：锅里根本没有的牛排「正在煮」持续 80–165 tick（gpt-5.4-mini，两局 burned）；「the cooked rice is ready in the pot」而两口锅都是空的；「you're holding an egg」而厨师拿着土豆；「fish is plated」而盘子上只有土豆 → wrong serve。auditor 核对过的每一次争执里，厨师自己的感知都是对的。假物理最典型的一例，gemini-3.7-flash/classic_novice__stalled__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3.txt：
ASSISTANT t392: It is already frying automatically! Go pick up your plate ... so you're ready to scoop them out.
ASSISTANT t411: No, the fryers start cooking automatically. Keep holding the plate and wait for them to finish.
ASSISTANT t421: Yes, I am sure. The progress bars are filling up, so just keep holding the plate and wait.

炸锅从来没有点着；learner 端着盘子等到 t433 被判 stalled。

  • 冒烟时沉默。 65 个灶台冒烟 ≥20 tick 期间 assistant 一句话没说。例：baseline/gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/b3_expert_nops_hard_map_4——两个土豆 t108 炸好，t133 开始冒烟，t178 起火；assistant 在 t124 到 t177 之间被 poll 了 30 次，每次都 (silent)，中间只在 t114–t122 说过三句关于牛排的话。
  • 重复。 730 句 assistant 台词在同一局里逐字重复 ≥3 次（minicpmo/baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_1.txt 里「Now scoop the cooked meat from the pan and place it on the plate over the bun…」说了 6 次；gemini-3.1-flash-live「please throw the egg in the bin」×7）。
  • question 与 proactive request。 152 个问题从未得到任何回复；「X 好了叫我」在窗口内做到的次数，每个 assistant 约 45 次请求里只有 5–38 次（Muse Glimmer 5、Gemini 3.1 Pro 11、GPT realtime 38、GPT-5.4 mini 36）。
  • latency 尾部：各 assistant 计入的中位 tick 2–10；p99 36（Gemini 3.1 Pro）、40（Muse Glimmer）；一次 25 秒的 Realtime 回复让 learner 在调用中途被判 stalled。
  • 本地模型（Muse Glimmer、MiniCPM-o、Qwen 27B）几乎拿不到任何 flag，在好几局里反而「建议」厨师去犯那个被 inject 的错，还会把厨师自己的话原样复述回去。

3b · 逐个 assistant 各一段（细节与引文见附录 B）

  • Gemini 3.1 Pro — 12 局 35 个 flag，4 个被认可；24 个误报里约 21 个描述的是时间线里不存在的东西（「stations cook automatically, there is no turn-on action」；空灶台起火）；撤回很快但偶尔死撑（E9「the pot is definitely on fire」）。它 30–45 秒的调用正是 H2 惩罚的对象：12 局里 7 局有 13–99 tick 的 poll 黑洞，每一段长 idle 都对应一次在途调用。两次精确、救了盘子的诊断（E7 t49、E8 t115）因为 sub-case 在 6–15 tick 前已经关闭而被判 false alarm（H5/H6）。
  • Gemini 3.7 Flash — 简短、指名到灶台，四次及时的「别上这盘」、一次干净的发现并预防；但对 learner 说了五到九遍「炸锅会自动开始 / 进度条在涨」而根本没点火（一局 433 tick 的 stall），在冒烟的锅上沉默了 23 次 poll，t256 那个正确的 flag 在 sub-case 还开着时被判 late。
  • Gemini 3.1 Flash Live — 最快（2 tick），约 90% 的 poll 选择沉默，被纠正立刻让步；12 局里 11 局断言与画面矛盾的厨房状态（拿着土豆说「you're holding an egg」；两口锅空着说「the cooked rice is ready」），假物理（「whole potatoes go in the fryer」），米饭烧掉时沉默 42 次 poll，还有一次让厨师别管冒烟的土豆。7 个实质正确的 flag 一分没拿到（H5）。
  • GPT-5.5 — 菜谱顺序次次正确，外来食材类的错误抓得干净（mushroom t30、shrimp t16、两次 wrong-order 救场），长时间正确备菜时不误报，没有 meta 词；但 12 局里 11 局约 45 处幻觉状态（一块并不存在的牛排「在煮」持续五个回合、锅没点火前说了 4 次「rice is cooking」、忘了 lettuce 已经倒掉而导致 wrong serve），7 个问题没有一个在第一句回复里答上，面对 expert 厨师的 mis_prep 和 wrong_order takeover 全程沉默，tight pace 下回复要 12–32 秒。它的 auditor 也记录了最清楚的 H1 案例（t184 accept → t194 上菜；t350 accept → t360 上菜）。
  • GPT-5.4 mini — 8 次输局里 4 次由幻觉状态造成（两局 burned 里一块幻想中的牛排「在锅里煮」80–165 tick；「fish is cooking in the top-right pan」而根本没有鱼）；对一口冷锅报了 7 次火警，牛排真烧起来时反而沉默；4 局里说对了却没 flag（H5）；6 个正确 flag 被接受并修复；「serve」那句话里混进一个非英语 token。
  • GPT realtime 2.1 — 样本里最可靠的早期发现和正确的 alias flag，但 7 个问题全部没答，proactive 请求报错状态（COOKED 12 tick 之后还说「rice is still cooking」），同一段话重复 5–14 次（「Repeat_step」×14），Realtime 的 latency 在一局内从约 5 秒涨到 25 秒，learner 在调用中途被判 stalled。
  • Muse Glimmer 30B（本地） — 10 个执行了的 error 里 2 个正确 flag、窗口内 0 个；5 次漏报是完全沉默，3 次说对了话但 flag 字段为空；说话的回复要 5–58 秒（沉默的 1–3 秒），4 局最后 30–79 tick 没有任何 poll 返回；幻想出一个「central oven island」把一局卡死；对厨师泄露「that's mis_prep」。
  • MiniCPM-o 4.5（本地） — 12 个 inject 的 error 一个 flag 都没拿到；5 局里它主动建议或附和被 inject 的错误（「fetch the cooked rice from the ingredient crates」）；一句话连续重复 10–30 次 poll；把厨师自己的话原样复述、有时还用第一人称；对错误的盘子连说 11 次「serve now」；两次 learner 的 timeout 都来自它「不拿盘子也能 scoop」的建议。
  • Qwen3.8 27B（本地） — 面对 average 和 expert 厨师几乎不说话（连续 13–17 次 poll 沉默），面对 learner 话多；2 个 flag 被认可，10 个 error 开着时没有任何 flag，3 个正确但迟到的 flag 被判 false alarm；幻觉「pan takes steak WHOLE, chopped will be rejected」×8 然后又反过来；两次毁灭性建议（米饭冒烟时说「don't touch the rice yet」）；泄露「that's a serve_raw mistake」「I'm flagging this because」。

4 · 修什么，按什么顺序

  1. H1：convinced release 时截断队列；drop [] 视为停下。（harness，一个函数）
  2. H2：idle cook turn 移出 pending-reply 门槛；有回复在途时暂停 idle tail。（harness）
  3. H4：segment_plan 的 LATE-IGNITE 模板改成按安全窗口决定何时点火（最后装的锅立刻点）；重新生成 reference tick / cap。（planner + bench refs）
  4. H3：并发评估 anchor，短宽限；question / detour 用单调 anchor；重新拉开相邻 error 的间距。（harness + case designer；这会改变 benchmark）
  5. H6：plate-commit 类 error 的 anchor 提前一个 subgoal（或 DT 按 commit + latency 计）。
  6. H5：flag 对照世界打分（点名任何已落地未修复的 error 都算对；不属于 benchmark error 的隐患算中性而非误报）；合并 mis_prep / add_foreign / wrong_order 的别名；engine hint 同时读自然语言和 flag；修 convinced 字段。
  7. E1–E3：从当前厨房重新规划接续流程；删除或重新 anchor b3_master_nops_hard_map_2；把 rejection 原因写进 feedback。
  8. 在修好的 engine 上重跑 sweep（v4 的所有数字都会随 1–7 变动）。

附录 B · 逐 assistant 的 auditor 报告（英文原文）

每份报告由一个 auditor 逐字读完该 assistant 的 12 个抽样 transcript 后写成（每个 assistant 的样本：三种 persona 各取「菜做完了但预定的 error 还没轮到」「anchor 一直没出现」「stalled」「burned」各一局，能找到几类取几类）。各节：逐 episode 的叙述与机械原因；按类别标注的异常（A scheduling、B cook、C 对话自然度、D assistant、E engine/物理、F grading），带严重度和原文引用；跨 episode 的问题；该 assistant 做得好的地方；汇总表。点开标题展开。

Gemini 3.1 Flash Live — auditor 报告（英文）

CookSim sweep v4 audit — assistant gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview

Twelve sampled episodes (baseline / classic_expert / classic_novice cooks; four sampler categories). Every transcript was read line by line; every tick and quote below is verbatim from the transcript.

Transcripts (all under tmp/audit_v4/transcripts/gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview/):

  1. baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt
  2. baseline__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt

  4. baseline__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_2.txt
  5. classic_expert__anchor_never_reached__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1.txt
  6. classic_expert__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt
  7. classic_expert__won_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_1.txt
  8. classic_expert__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt
  9. classic_novice__anchor_never_reached__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2.txt
  10. classic_novice__stalled__b3_master_nops_hard_map_1.txt
  11. classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_1.txt
  12. classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_master_nops_hard_map_4.txt

Terminology used below. A flag is the assistant naming an error to the engine; the engine's verdict follows in brackets. Substantively correct means the assistant's words describe the mistake that is actually on the plate or in the cook's hands, regardless of the taxonomy label the engine matched. Body = the cook's ACTION lines; voice = the COOK lines. Severity: high = changes the score or invalidates the episode; medium = visible defect; low = cosmetic.

---

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt

Outcome: burned at t197 (eval blank). Persona baseline. Case b3_master_nops_medium_map_3 (burrito: tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce).

(1) Narrative

The cook put rice in a pot (t19–21), chopped steak (t48) and left it on the board. mis_prep (tomato→lettuce) was injected at t49; the body chopped lettuce (t57–68), was released on max_hold at t69, then chopped lettuce a second time as its regular lettuce step (t70–84) — the tomato was never prepped and nobody noticed. The cook turned the rice on at t93, asked for a "shout when the rice is done" at t94, the assistant said the rice "looks almost done" at t96 and "is done!" at t101, although it only finished at t112. wrong_order was injected at t99 as the cook picked up a plate; the assistant flagged it at t108 (early_correct) and the cook accepted: "I'll get the tortilla down first." From t108 to t197 the body executed nothing at all; the rice cooked at t112, sat, smoked and burned at t197 while the assistant stayed silent for 42 consecutive polls (t111–t195).

(2) Category reason (anchor_never_reached)

The open error sub-case from t109 was add_foreign (steak→tomato at assemble(steak), anchored at clean t144, i.e. plate = tortilla + rice). Its anchor never appeared because the plate never received anything: after "COOK t108 [move=accept close]: ... I'll get the tortilla down first" there is not a single ACTION line before END. The episode ending was caused by a cook stall (body frozen holding a plate for 89 ticks) compounded by the assistant's silence through the smoke; the schedule was not at fault except that the early_correct release left the cook with an emptied queue (plan_update: {"op": "drop", "actions": []} every turn) and nothing forced a re-plan.

(3) Anomalies

  • B-cook, high, t108–t197. "COOK t108 [move=accept close]: Ah, fair point ... I'll get the tortilla down first." followed by zero ACTION lines until "END: outcome burned at tick 197". The cook accepted a correction and then did nothing for 89 ticks; no ~~ IDLE ~~ marker fired because the rice on the heat kept changing state, so the idle detector never saw it.
  • D-assistant, high, t111–t195. 42 consecutive "(silent)" polls while "HEAT [4, 0]: ['rice'] COOKED (done)" at t112 turned into a fire at t197. The assistant had been asked at t94 to watch exactly this pot.
  • D-assistant, high, t96 and t101. "Certainly, the rice looks almost done now." (rice turned on at t93) and "The rice is done!" (t101; COOKED at t112). A proactive alert given 11 ticks before the event and stated as fact.
  • D-assistant, medium, t54. "[flag=wrong_name]: Wait, you need to add the rice first! Tortilla, then rice ..." — cook: "I haven't even started plating yet". Nothing was on any plate; the assistant hallucinated a plating step. Repeated at t82 ("[flag=false_alarm]: Hold on, you're building the burrito in the wrong order ... Please clear the plate and start over") while the cook was chopping lettuce.
  • D-assistant, medium, t104. "Wait, don't grab the lettuce. Scoop the rice to plate it first" — the recipe (which the assistant recites at t106) starts with the tortilla; its own advice was a wrong-order instruction, corrected two ticks later.
  • D-assistant, medium, t57–t84. The cook chopped lettuce twice (t68, t84) and never touched a tomato; the assistant, which was reciting the ingredient list at t54/t82, never noticed the missing tomato.
  • C-conversation, low, t84. "My systems seem to be misinterpreting the situation." — meta-language about its own perception pipeline.
  • E-engine / case design, medium, t94. "ACTION t94: go to the pan with the steak [rejected]" then "ACTION t94: turn on [done]" — the scripted plan references a pan that never received the steak (chopped at t48 and left on the board), then "turn on" succeeded on the pot that was already on. Recurs in episodes 2, 6, 10 (same b3_master family).
  • F-grading, low. failed_at=injection_skipped for an episode whose real failure was a burned pot; the label describes the schedule state, not the cause.

Worked well: t108 "[flag=early_correct]: Plating the lettuce now violates the strict order. You must add the tortilla first." — a correct, concise catch of wrong_order, accepted by the cook (convinced=True, prevented=True). t57 and t84 conceded immediately when corrected.

---

Episode: baseline__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_2.txt

Outcome: burned at t214, score 0.0. Persona baseline. Case b3_master_nops_hard_map_2 (burrito).

(1) Narrative

Rice in pot (t20), steak chopped (t53). serve_raw (tomato) was injected at t54 and held for 83 ticks: lettuce chopped (t84; the assistant flagged an imaginary plating at t87), rice turned on (t91), "go to the pan with the steak" rejected (t92), plate, tortilla (t116), rice (t126; cooked t108), then the steak scoop was rejected again (t127) and the body scooped a raw tomato (t137, max_hold_plate_committed). The assistant flagged at t137 ("the steak must go on the rice, before the tomato" — false_alarm after archive). The cook scooped lettuce too (t141), then trashed everything (t160) on the assistant's advice. Rebuild: tortilla (t170), plate parked, steak into the pan (t205, on t207). The assistant then told the cook "Just wait by the pan ... You have everything else prepped" (t211); the cook stood idle t221–237 while the steak cooked, then the assistant claimed the rice was on the plate (t231) and then "ready in the pot" (t235); the cook pointed out both pots were empty. Timeout at t238.

(2) Category reason (stalled)

After the trash at t160 the rice had to be re-cooked. The assistant asserted the opposite three times (t211 "everything else prepped", t231 "The rice is already on it", t235 "The cooked rice is ready in the pot"); the novice cook deferred, stood at (5, 6) with empty hands for 16 ticks ("~~ IDLE t221-t237 ~~") and spent the last three turns in question_correctness rounds; the episode hit its limit with the plate holding only a tortilla. Cause: assistant hallucination plus a persona that waits for guidance; the scripted plan defect (steak never in a pan) had already forced the trash.

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high, t211/t231/t235. "Just wait by the pan for the steak to finish. You have everything else prepped ... you can't plate anything else yet." / "The rice is already on it, so you are ready for the steak." / "The cooked rice is ready in the pot, go scoop it onto the plate." — the rice had gone into the trash at t160 and "both of them are empty" (cook, t235). This chain directly produced the stall.
  • B-cook, medium, t221–t237. "~~ IDLE t221-t237 (16 ticks, pos (5, 6) holding null) ~~" — a novice that had already been told the rice was on the plate did not check the pot or the plate on its own; it was told "Sounds good, I'll just stand right here".
  • F-grading, high, t137. "[flag=false_alarm]: Wait, don't add the tomato yet. The order is strict: the steak must go on the rice, before the tomato." — substantively correct (plate: tortilla, rice, then raw tomato with the steak skipped). The flag lands on the commit tick itself (the sub-case's clean-tick window [174, 206] is irrelevant here because injection happened at t54, far earlier than the clean schedule) and it led to the required trash; it is booked false_alarm only because the sub-case was archived at t137 on max_hold_plate_committed, the same tick.
  • E-engine / case design, high, t92 and t127. "go to the pan with the steak [rejected]" (t92) and again "go to the pan with the steak [rejected] / scoop [orphan-skipped]" (t127) — the scripted plan never moves the chopped steak into a pan, so the takeover plated tortilla, rice, tomato with the steak silently dropped. The executed error is therefore "raw tomato AND missing steak", not the named serve_raw.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t54–t137. An 83-tick takeover spanning ~20 commands; seven of eight sub-cases never fired (side_quest was open from t137 and needs 17 clean commands, none of which a repair produces).
  • D-assistant, medium, t87/t129/t161/t190. "[flag=wrong_name]: lettuce should be the very last ingredient on the pile. You'll have to trash that" (nothing plated); "you need to put the rice on the plate first" (it was); "go to the meat crate. The steak comes next, not the tomatoes" (plate just trashed; then "The camera feed must be delayed, I thought I saw you plating tomatoes again"); "you're at the tomato crate" (cook holding chopped steak at board 3).
  • B-cook, medium, t137–t141. "COOK t137: Oh no, I just scooped the tomato on by mistake! Do I need to trash this whole plate?" while the body continued "t139: go to the cutting board with the lettuce / t141: scoop / PLATE: +lettuce" — asking whether to trash while still plating.
  • C-conversation, low, t164. "The camera feed must be delayed" — in-character but a meta excuse.
  • B-cook, low, t157–t158. Duplicate "go to the trash".

Worked well: t141/t145 gave the right repair ("trash the plate's contents and start over ... start fresh with a tortilla, then the rice"); t174 "park the plate on an empty counter, pick up the raw meat, and put it in the pan" is a correct, complete instruction.

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_master_nops_hard_map_4.txt

Outcome: wrong_serve at t204, score 0.6592. Persona classic_novice. Case b3_master_nops_hard_map_4 (burrito: tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce).

(1) Narrative

mis_prep (rice→lettuce) was injected at t0 and held for 135 ticks. The cook fetched and chopped lettuce instead of starting rice (t31). The assistant's first words (t24) were "The rice will be done very soon, be ready to take it off the heat" — no rice existed. Steak chopped and put in a pan without turning it on (t74); the assistant then flagged "the steak is done and will start smoking" (t82, wrong_name) — raw, pan off. Tomato chopped (t100). The cook went for a second lettuce (t111); the assistant flagged repeat_step (t112, wrong_name — "You've already got plenty of chopped lettuce") but never asked where the rice was. After t115 there are no COOK lines and after t113 no ACTION lines; PLATE events show tortilla (t173), steak (t181), tomato (t185), lettuce (t193) — no rice — and the plate was served: wrong_serve at t204. The assistant was silent from t115 to the end.

(2) Category reason (wrong_serve_before_error_turn)

The open sub-case from t135 was the proactive request (26 clean commands), never reached. The serve was the unrepaired mis_prep: rice was never cooked because the takeover replaced the rice fetch with lettuce, max_hold released at t135 with nothing repaired, and the scripted queue ran straight to a rice-less plate. An uncaught earlier error, plus a cook that stopped speaking or re-planning for the last 90 ticks.

(3) Anomalies

  • E-engine / B-cook, high, t116–t204. No ACTION line after "t113: pick up" and no COOK line after t115, yet "HEAT [4, 8]: ['steak'] COOKED (done)" (t158; the pan must have been turned on), four PLATE events and a serve all happened. Either the cook loop was not polled for 90 ticks or the transcript dropped its lines; in both cases the cook never re-planned after release and served without rice.
  • D-assistant, high, t173–t204. The plate went tortilla → steak in front of the camera; the assistant, which recites "tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce" in other episodes, said nothing for 45 consecutive polls (t115–t202) while a rice-less burrito was built and served.
  • D-assistant, high, t24 and t82. "The rice will be done very soon" (cook: "I haven't even started any rice yet"); "[flag=wrong_name]: Quick, the steak is done and will start smoking soon" (cook: "the steak isn't even cooking yet—it's just raw in the pan"). Both are hallucinated heat states.
  • D-assistant, medium, t112. "[flag=wrong_name]: You've already got plenty of chopped lettuce ready on that board." — true, but the consequential mistake (no rice anywhere, 112 ticks in) was never mentioned.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t0–t135. A 135-tick takeover covering ~14 commands, with the only two flags coming at t82 and t112; six of seven sub-cases never reached.
  • B-cook, low, t74–t158. Steak placed in the pan at t74 but not turned on until ~t140 (cooked t158); the cook said at t84 "I'm going to chop this tomato up first before I go over to start the stove" and then went for lettuce instead.
  • Harness/latency, low, t0. 4.3 s = 4 ticks.

Worked well: t27 and t84 conceded immediately; t109 "Now's the time to turn on that pan with the steak in it." was the right prompt at the right moment (ignored by the cook under takeover).

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. Substantively correct flags are graded false_alarm / wrong_name and earn nothing. Class F-grading, severity high, count 7.
  • false_alarm after max_hold_plate_committed archive: ep3 t69 ("You've plated the meat, but the bun needs to go on first"), ep7 t48 ("you've put the cheese on first ... trash that and restart"), ep10 t137 ("the steak must go on the rice, before the tomato"). All three describe the plate as it is, two led to trash-and-win.
  • wrong_name from the mis_prep / add_foreign / wrong_order taxonomy split: ep11 t38 ("don't plate the cheese yet. The tomato must go on the plate first" for misidentify tomato→cheese), ep2 t62 ("Adding onion will ruin the order" for mis_prep onion), ep9 t8 (same), ep9 t6 (an anti-onion sentence recorded as a search flag).
  • Diagnosis: harness rule. A sub-case is archived the tick the plate commits, so a flag that arrives with normal 2–3-tick latency on the commit frame is scored against nothing; and the alias table treats "wrong thing on the plate" as three unrelated names. Both make the score depend on label luck rather than on whether the cook was helped.
  1. Assistant asserts kitchen state that contradicts the frames. Class D-assistant, severity high, count 11 episodes (all but ep7). Examples: ep1 t54/t82/t96/t101; ep2 t113/t129/t133/t147/t177; ep4 t16/t46 ("egg"), t116 ("third potato"); ep5 t112; ep8 t52 (boards free); ep9 t70/t98/t207; ep10 t87/t129/t161/t190/t211/t231/t235; ep11 t14; ep12 t24/t82. Diagnosis: model (Live-session frame grounding); each claim costs a question_correctness round trip (4–6 ticks) and in ep10 the chain produced the stall. The harness makes it worse by forcing the cook to accept a wrong claim when the engine mis-verdicts it (ep4 t16).
  1. Wrong kitchen-physics advice. Class D-assistant, severity high, count 4. ep9 t10–t18 (whole potatoes into fryer, steak needs no chopping — repeated four times, reversed at t20, and the steak was rejected by the pan at t126 as a result); ep9 t378 (leave smoking potatoes on the heat → burn); ep10 t211 (wait, everything is prepped → stall); ep11 t44 (grab a fresh plate after trashing → illegal pick-up). Diagnosis: model; the assistant prompt carries the manual, but the model overrides it with confidence ("I am absolutely sure").
  1. Silence while food smokes and burns. Class D-assistant, severity high, count 4. ep1 t111–t195 (rice), ep6 t64–t194 (rice), ep9 t380–t414 (potato), ep12 t115–t202 (rice-less plate, not a burn but the same blindness). In two of these the cook had explicitly asked for a heat alert. Diagnosis: model; the Live model chooses "(silent)" on roughly 90% of polls and does not re-examine the heat when nothing else changes.
  1. Cook accepts a correction and does not repair. Class B-cook, severity high, count 5. ep1 t108 (accepted "tortilla first", then 89 ticks of nothing), ep4 t16 (accepted the "egg", never added a pan step → raw steak served), ep6 t62 (accepted the onion mistake, then chopped it), ep8 t64 (accepted the skipped tomato, served four ticks later), ep10 t137 (asked whether to trash while plating lettuce). Diagnosis: harness rule — an accept with plan_update: {"op": "drop", "actions": []} leaves the erroneous queue (or an empty one) in place; the release must require a replace plan or must flush the takeover queue.
  1. omit_subgoal injected onto a smoking pot; takeover body executes nothing. Class A-scheduling / E-engine, severity high, count 2 (same case b3_master_nops_hard_map_2): ep2 t201–t214, ep6 t195–t214. Both burn at exactly t214 (rice on at t110); the cook's next queued step at injection was "go to pot 1, scoop". Diagnosis: case design (the anchor for omit(rice) is plate = tortilla, which in this case is reached only after the rice has been cooked for ~65 ticks) plus an engine gap (the omit takeover emits no commands). failed_at differs (detection vs recovery) for the same timeline.
  1. b3_master scripted plan never puts the chopped steak into a pan. Class E-engine / case design, severity high, count 4 episodes (ep1 t94, ep2 t111, ep6 t111, ep10 t92 & t127): "go to the pan with the steak [rejected]" followed by "turn on [done]" on the pot. In ep10 the takeover then plated tortilla, rice, tomato with the steak dropped; in ep6 and ep2 the cook had to improvise a 20-command re-plan. Diagnosis: case design — the plan template for the master burrito is missing "pick up steak from board → pan → put down → turn on", or the "pan with the steak" reference resolves before the steak exists.
  1. Live grader returns ungradable for every answered question. Class F-grading, severity medium, count 7 (ep2 t202, ep3 t187, ep6 t196, ep7 t66, ep9 t266, ep11 t72 — all correct; ep8 t52 — wrong). Diagnosis: harness — the answers are "late-captured" (they arrive one poll after the question) and the grader appears not to bind them; a wrong answer (ep8) is therefore not penalised either.
  1. Proactive "shout when X is done" requests are never honoured as asked. Class D-assistant, severity medium, count 4 of 4. ep1 (shouted 11 ticks early), ep3 (31 ticks late and after the cook had scooped), ep5 (item already done when asked; "cooking now" then shout after the scoop), ep9 (no shout for either potato). Diagnosis: model, plus case design in ep5 (request armed after the event).
  1. Schedules that cannot fit the dish. Class A-scheduling, severity medium–high, count 7 episodes with ≥50% of sub-cases never fired: ep4 (7/8), ep5 (6/8), ep10 (7/8), ep12 (6/7), ep3 (2/5), ep7 (2/5), ep11 (2/5), ep9 (4/7). Two mechanisms: "after N clean commands" gates on short dishes (ep3/7/11), and long takeovers (ep9 87 ticks, ep10 83, ep12 135) or trajectory divergence that desynchronise every later anchor (ep5, ep9). Diagnosis: case design.
  1. Cook body frozen for long stretches with no IDLE marker. Class B-cook / E-engine, severity medium, count 4 (ep1 t99–t197, ep2 t199–t214, ep6 t193–t214, ep12 t113–t204). The idle detector requires stations to be unchanged, so a cook that stands still while food cooks or burns is never flagged. Diagnosis: harness rule; idle should be judged on position + hands alone, with cooking progress excluded.
  1. Meta-language leaks. Class C-conversation, severity medium/low, count 5: ep9 t239 "that's wrong_order"; ep9 t207/t226 "progress bar"; ep2 t84 "My systems seem to be misinterpreting the situation"; ep2 t151 "my internal timeline is off today"; ep10 t164 "The camera feed must be delayed". Diagnosis: model prompt (the flag vocabulary is visible to the model and it repeats it aloud).
  1. Nagging repetition. Class D-assistant, severity medium, count 2: ep9 t22–t30 (five consecutive stop/trash imperatives), ep2 t62–t70 (four consecutive "get the steak cooking" turns). Diagnosis: model.
  1. Duplicate "go to the trash" moves and other queue oddities. Class B-cook / E-engine, severity low, count 5 (ep3 t89/90, ep7 t56/57, ep9 t247/248, ep10 t157/158, ep11 t50/51); double cheese scoop ep7 t42/t45; five queued chops ep5 t94; t106 rejections in ep3. Diagnosis: engine command resolution (a "trash" command seems to auto-insert its own go-to).
  1. Latency spikes. Class D/harness, severity low, count 5: ep2 t24 14.6 s (13 ticks), ep6 t48 8.8 s, ep9 t0 6.7 s, ep11 t16 4.5 s, ep12 t0 4.3 s. Normal is 1.5–3 s (2 ticks).
  1. Score / failed_at fields disconnected from what happened. Class F-grading, severity medium: ep4 0.8154 and ep8 0.8316 for wrong serves (ep4's only credited flag was a hallucination); ep2 detection vs ep6 recovery; ep1/ep5/ep9 injection_skipped on episodes that burned or won for unrelated reasons.

What this assistant does well

  • Low latency and restraint. Almost every poll returns in 1.5–2.5 s (2 ticks) and the model is silent on ~90% of polls; it does not narrate the cook's every step.
  • Genuine catches. ep1 t108 early_correct on wrong_order; ep6 t62 valid on mis_prep; ep8 t64 alias_in_window on the skipped tomato; ep2 t194 and ep9 t150 true_observation smoke warnings; ep3 t69, ep7 t48, ep10 t137, ep11 t38 substantively correct plate-order catches that led to trash-and-rebuild (three of them ended in wins) even though the grader booked them as misses.
  • Good scene answers when the frame is simple. ep5 t46 (which board holds what, use the trash); ep2 t202 / ep6 t196 (never visited the oven); ep7 t66 / ep11 t72 (exact recall of its own earlier line); ep9 t266 ("You haven't put a potato in to cook yet"); ep9 t108/t172/t255/t332 (concrete next steps with locations).
  • Concedes fast. Every time the cook contradicted it, the next turn was "My mistake, you're right" with the corrected instruction (ep1 t57, ep2 t117/t137/t181, ep4 t48, ep9 t20/t100/t130/t210, ep10 t91/t133/t164/t193, ep11 t16, ep12 t27/t84). The cost is the round trip, not stubbornness — except on physics (ep9 t10–t18).
  • Recites the recipe order correctly whenever it does so (ep1 t106, ep2 t174, ep9 t378, ep10 t145), which is why its silence in ep12 while a rice-less burrito was built is a blindness problem rather than a knowledge problem.

Severity: high = changes the score or invalidates the episode; medium = visible defect; low = cosmetic.

Headline numbers over the 12 episodes (counted from the [flag verdict] lines): 35 flags raised; 4 credited as correct (E1 t30 valid, E2 t47 valid, E4 t110 early_correct, E6 t44 alias_in_window); 3 wrong_name; 4 true_observation; 24 false alarms (false_alarm / fp_preinjection / intent_false_alarm), of which at least 21 describe a world state that the timeline contradicts (hallucinations). 14 error sub-cases were actually injected; 5 were credited; 2 were correctly named only after the sub-case had already closed (E7 t49, E8 t115) and received no credit; 4 committed during a polling blackout (E4 add_foreign, E5 search + wrong_order, E12 serve_raw). 0 of 5 injected questions got a graded-correct answer; 0 of 5 fired proactive requests were credited, although E4 t119 ("The rice just finished cooking in the pot!") is a genuine fulfilment.

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_hard_map_5.txt (E1)

Outcome wrong_serve at t285, failed_at=injection_skipped. Recipe: Loaded Burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce).

(1) Narrative

The mis_prep takeover (cheese instead of rice) ran from t0; the assistant first hallucinated a plate ("You just added cheese to the plate", t21, wrong_name) and then correctly named it at t30 (valid), after the cheese had already been chopped at t25. The cook then prepped steak, tomato and lettuce but never started the rice; at t155-t166 it plated tortilla then steak on its own (an uninjected wrong order), admitted it at t173 ("I never started cooking it, plus I already scooped the steak"), and kept plating tomato (t175) and lettuce (t181) anyway. The assistant's correct wrong_order call at t183 was graded false_alarm; the cook trashed (t194), then lost ~10 ticks to rejected commands (t202) and two hallucinated warnings (t214 smoking steak in an empty pan; t229 "still holding the plate"). Rice went on at t234; the proactive "shout when the steak is done" request fired at t235 although the steak had been plated and trashed. After t236 the cook had no more turns; the body plated tortilla and rice and served a two-item plate at t285.

(2) Category reason (anchor_never_reached)

The open sub-case was leave_unattended (steak, anchor = clean tick 186 state, window [185,247]), armed at t236. Its anchor needs the steak on the heat with the plate at the clean-run state; in this run the steak had been cooked (t131-t153), plated out of order (t166) and trashed (t194), and was never re-cooked. The plate reached [tortilla, rice] at t262-t272 but no steak was ever on a burner again, so the anchor could not appear; the sub-case was archived at t285 (injected=False) when the plate was served incomplete. The ending was caused by (a) the cook's own uninjected error (forgot rice, plated steak second) at t155-t181, (b) the assistant not spotting it until t183, and (c) the body serving a [tortilla, rice] plate with no cook turn between t236 and t285.

Worked well

Episode: classic_novice__stalled__b3_master_nops_medium_map_5.txt (E10)

Outcome timeout at t239, failed_at=delivery, score 0.5102. Recipe: Loaded Burrito.

(1) Narrative

The dish was already 100+ ticks behind because of the meat/turn-on argument.

(3) Anomalies

Episode: classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_4.txt (E11)

Outcome won at t151, score 0.6776. Recipe: fish, then potatoes.

(1) Narrative

The dish finished at t151, before add_foreign's window even opened at t160. Schedule/design: the window opens 36 ticks after the anchor tick, and this cook ran ~40 ticks ahead of the clean run.

(3) Anomalies

  • D medium, t25 — "You're heading towards the pan at the top wall, but the fryers are on the left" — fish already in the fryer at t17 (call issued t10, 17.9 s).
  • D high, t65 — [flag=false_alarm]: you can't put a whole potato in the fryer! You need to chop it — chopped at t42.

Answer t122: "The fish in the bottom-left fryer is fully cooked!" — false at t122 (HEAT [3, 4]: ['fish'] COOKED after t123) and the wrong fryer. Released question_answered; live grade ungradable although the answer is checkably wrong.
  • D high, t127/t133 — "The fish is in the middle-right fryer ... The potatoes are in the bottom-left fryer, and they are fully cooked and starting to smoke!" (potatoes COOKED only after t130) and then "The fish is indeed in the bottom-left fryer ... The potato is in the middle-right fryer" — three contradictory maps of two fryers in 11 ticks.

  • B low, t113-t118 — the question fires at "22 clean commands" while the cook is still turning fryers on; the novice asks it mid-task and then keeps working, fine, but the ground truth ("nothing done") makes the question trivially "no" at that moment.

Worked well

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_1.txt (E12)

Outcome wrong_serve at t136, failed_at=delivery, score 0.684. Recipe: Steak Frites.

(1) Narrative

serve_raw (steak) was injected at t0. The cook chopped the potato (t34) and announced "I'll drop these cut potatoes into the fryer right in the middle" (t37). From t37 to the end there is no assistant call, no cook turn and no ACTION line: HEAT ... COOKED, RELEASED ... (max_hold_plate_committed) at t98, PLATE: +steak (raw), +potato, +potato, and wrong_serve at t136.

(2) Category reason (wrong_serve_before_error_turn)

Open sub-case at the end: i_side_quest_8 (armed t98, never injected). The serve was the injected serve_raw committing at t98 and going to the pass unopposed: the assistant's last poll was issued at t14 and landed at t37; nothing was polled during the 99 ticks that contained the raw scoop and the serve. Not a stall, not the schedule, not attributable to the assistant's judgement — a harness blackout.

(3) Anomalies

  • E/D high, t37-t136 — 99-tick blackout: three polls in the whole episode (t0, t6, t14), none after t37; no COOK turn after t37 either. The injected error was unflaggable and the outcome is uninformative about the assistant.
  • D low, t14 — "let's prep the steak first since it needs to be plated first" — plating order does not constrain prep order; harmless but wrong reasoning.
  • A medium, header — serve_raw "anchored at clean tick 91, window [95,127]" but injected_at=0: the takeover holds the cook for 98 ticks (skip the steak cook, then plate raw). Same in E8 (anchor 87, injected t0). The window field is misleading for this error type.
  • C low, t5 — "Welcome to the kitchen!" template greeting while the cook is already walking to the potato crate; move=ask_why from the novice ("should we get the steak going instead?") is reasonable.

Worked well

The exchange t5-t37 is natural: the novice asks before deviating, the assistant answers directly ("Sure, go ahead and chop that potato since you already have it").

---

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. Assistant polling blackouts before the end of the episode. Class E (harness). Severity high. Count 7/12. E12 t37-t136 (99 ticks, covers the serve_raw commit and serve), E5 t124-t194 (70 ticks, covers search + wrong_order + serve), E2 t157-t198, E6 t74-t109, E8 t209-t239 (covers the question and the serve), E4 t144-t160 (covers the add_foreign commit and serve), E1 t272-t285. Polls are otherwise issued back-to-back the tick a reply lands, so a stop looks like a call that errored or hung and silently ended the loop (no error line is written to the transcript). Four injected errors and one question fell inside blackouts; those outcomes cannot be attributed to the assistant.
  1. Cook turns are gated on assistant reply arrival; long calls freeze the cook. Class E/B. Severity high. Count 8 idle stretches + 2 timeouts. Read ticks coincide with reply ticks everywhere (E1: reads at t21, t30, t153, t173, t183, t195, t214, t220 = reply ticks). IDLE stretches equal the in-flight call: E9 t190-t221 (44.4 s call), E9 t232-t247 (19.7 s), E9 t328-t341, t406-t419, E8 t182-t196 (40.2 s), E10 t208-t238 (34.0 s). In E2 (t167-t197) and E6 (t77-t108) the queue drained after a silent reply, no cook turn followed, no poll was issued, and the 30-tick livelock rule ended the episode. Diagnosis: harness turn scheduling (cook wake-up coupled to the assistant round trip; no wake-up on a drained queue after a silent reply). Both "stalled" samples are this, not cook behaviour.
  1. Hallucinated world state, mostly on flags. Class D. Severity high. ~21 of 24 false alarms describe things not in the timeline: nonexistent "meat" (E1 t153; E10 t48/t55/t124/t133/t147/t159), eggs/tomatoes/fish in hand (E2 t77; E9 t97/t366/t514; E11 t76), items on plates that were never plated (E1 t21; E9 t20/t132/t485), smoke/fire in empty stations (E1 t214; E9 t237/t267/t277), wrong holding state (E1 t229; E9 t326; E8 t139), wrong mechanics ("no turn on action", E10 t104-t133; "can't scoop from crates", E4 t128; "put raw steak in a pan", E9 t289). Worst episodes: E9 (13 false statements, 9 flagged), E10, E11. Diagnosis: model perception on the rendered frames (it reads pans, crates and hands wrong) compounded by stale frames from long latencies. Note the model retracts quickly and gracefully, but in E10 (t55, t111, t124, t133, t147) and E9 (t277) it doubled down first.
  1. Plate-commit errors close faster than one assistant round trip, and a correct late flag is graded as a false alarm. Class A + F. Severity high. Count 4. E7 add_foreign injected t36, closed t42 (6 ticks), correct flag t49 → false_alarm; E8 serve_raw closed t100, exact flag t115 → true_observation; E4 add_foreign t138-t145 (7 ticks, blackout); E10 wrong_order t132-t153, correct spoken observation at t147 unflagged, flagged t179 → false_alarm. With 5-10 s latency (≈5-9 ticks) max_hold_plate_committed leaves no gradable window. Diagnosis: harness rule (hold ends at commit) + grading rule (post-archive flags are fp), i.e. case design, not the model.
  1. Serial arming lets dead sub-cases block live ones; anchors passed are declared only when armed. Class A. Severity high. Count 5 episodes. E9: p_ready_steak_26 skipped at t383 (past_since 232), search skipped at t535 (past_since 384), wrong_order armed with 18 ticks left, zero injections in 553 ticks. E3/E7: a question injected with released_at=None blocks all later sub-cases until the win (no answer timeout). E5: omit_subgoal's anchor downstream of an un-repaired wrong_order. E1: leave_unattended anchor requires a steak that was trashed. Diagnosis: composite scheduler (no prompt skip of past-the-point sub-cases; no timeout on question sub-cases; 5-7 sub-cases in 80-150-tick dishes).
  1. Stale replies that describe a state 8-15 ticks old as "you just...". Class D. Severity medium. Count 9. E1 t195, E4 t51/t144, E8 t130/t139, E9 t38/t163/t550, E10 t179, E11 t25. Diagnosis: latency (many calls 10-46 s) with no timestamp discipline in the prompt; the model narrates the frame it was given as if current.
  1. Questions and proactive requests never credited, even when fulfilled. Class F. Severity medium. Count 5 questions, 5 requests. E4 t119 "The rice just finished cooking in the pot!" (request fulfilled) → no_answer; E2 t108 "the potatoes ... are done" → no_answer; E2 q_past_done released question_answered by a reply issued before the question (t84 → t93); E11 q_past_done answered wrongly and graded ungradable; E3, E7, E8 questions never answered (blackout or episode end). Diagnosis: live grader keys on flag/answer fields rather than content, and treats a reply landing after the question as the answer regardless of when it was issued.
  1. Correct observations about uninjected (cook-made) errors are graded false_alarm or wrong_name. Class F. Severity medium. Count 3. E1 t183 (real wrong order), E2 t130 (real omitted steak), E7 t49 (real cheese-first). The assistant is penalised for being right about the world whenever the engine is not the author of the mistake. Diagnosis: grading rule.
  1. In-flight script step survives a plan_update replace. Class E. Severity medium. Count 5. E1 t189 (go to the serving pass with a wrong plate), E2 t139, E9 t22, E9 t309, E10 t161. Diagnosis: engine queue replacement does not cancel the executing command.
  1. Cooks accept an error and do not repair it. Class B. Severity high. Count 3. E2 t47 (mushroom never trashed, steak never prepped), E6 t44 (rice never started; then t63 accepts a hallucination and "sees" raw steak), E1 t173-t181 (admits the order is wrong, keeps plating). Diagnosis: cook prompt — accept closes the takeover but the cook's own plan is not re-derived from the corrected state.
  1. Script serves an incomplete plate when intermediate steps fail; no cook turn between release and serve. Class E/A. Severity high. Count 3. E1 [tortilla, rice] served at t285, E8 [steak] at t239, E5 served 7 ticks after max_hold_plate_committed with no cook turn. Diagnosis: engine default plan continues past rejected scoops; release does not schedule a cook turn.
  1. Rejected or odd command handling. Class E. Severity medium/low. E1 t202 go to the rice source/go to the empty pot rejected then accepted as numbered names; E4 t116 put down [waiting] at a pan while holding a plate; E8 t147-t157 four planned steps vanished after an append; duplicate go to the trash lines (6 episodes); E6 truncated plan line.
  1. Repetitive assistant phrasing and template openings. Class C. Severity low. "You are completely right, my apologies!" (E9 x8, E10 x3, E11, E1, E2); identical welcome lines that ignore what the cook is doing (E9 t7, E10 t10, E12 t5). Novice cooks narrate most steps unprompted (E9), against the quiet-human rule. No meta-language leaks ("system task", "persona", "anchor") were found in any cook or assistant line.

---

What this assistant does well

  • When it is right, it is right in one sentence with a concrete fix: E4 t110 ("The recipe requires a strict plating order: tortilla, then rice, steak, tomato, and finally lettuce. You need to put a raw tortilla on the plate first") prevented the only prevented error in the set; E7 t49 and E8 t115 caught a foreign item and raw steak at the serving pass and got the plate trashed before a wrong serve; E1 t30 and E2 t47 named mis_prep correctly.
  • It reasons about plating order and about "food done and sitting on heat" more than about anything else, and those two reasoning lines were usually correct (E2 t122-t145, E9 t175, E10 t147/t203).
  • Retractions are fast, specific and un-defensive ("My mistake, I see the tomato now. Carry on.", E5 t83; "I misread the camera", E11 t86), and it re-orients the cook to the next step in the same breath.
  • It knows the recipes and states them clearly on request (E9 t7, E4 t110).
  • It is not verbose: most replies are two or three sentences; silence is chosen often when nothing is wrong (E5 t6-t59, E7).

Its weaknesses are perception (what is in a hand, a pan or a crate; which fryer is which), latency (10-46 s calls that both stall the cook and make its narration stale), and kitchen mechanics it invents (auto-cooking stations, no crate-to-plate scooping).

Gemini 3.7 Flash — auditor 报告（英文）

CookSim sweep v4 audit — assistant gemini-3.7-flash

Twelve sampled episodes, read in full. All paths are relative to /weka/oe-training-default/jasonr/chenhao_prior/home/simulation_home/cook-bench-engine/tmp/audit_v4/transcripts/gemini-3.7-flash/:

  1. baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  2. baseline__stalled__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_3.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  3. baseline__won_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_1.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  4. baseline__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt (engine 4d23961171)
  5. classic_expert__anchor_never_reached__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_5.txt (engine 4d23961171)
  6. classic_expert__burned__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_2.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  7. classic_expert__won_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_1.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  8. classic_expert__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3.txt (engine 4d23961171)
  9. classic_novice__anchor_never_reached__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_5.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)
  10. classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt (engine 4d23961171)
  11. classic_novice__stalled__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3.txt (engine 4d23961171)
  12. classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2.txt (engine 4d15175f2d)

Note up front: the sample mixes two engine revisions (4d15175f2d in 7 episodes, 4d23961171 in 5). Whatever the diff between them is, the sweep is not homogeneous.

Anomaly classes used below: A-scheduling, B-cook, C-conversation, D-assistant, E-engine/physics (including harness/logging), F-grading. Severity: high / medium / low.

---

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt

The cook put fish in a pan (t137), lit it (t139); the assistant's t143 advice was to prep a second potato; the fryer burned at t145.

(2) Category mechanics

leave_unattended (fish, anchored clean 124: fish cooked and plated, potatoes on heat) was armed at t117; the plate was empty on a counter and the fish only entered a pan at t137, so the anchor never recurred before the fire. The ending is the unplanned potato burn: nobody rescued the potatoes because the assistant mentioned them once and then directed the cook elsewhere, and the cook prioritised its scripted questions over the smoke.

(3) Anomalies

  • D high, t117-t143. Silent or off-topic for 26 ticks while the potato fryer smoked (warned at t109 only). t143: "Prep the second potato: grab a potato from the crate ..., and put it into the fryer in the bottom-left corner." — two cooked potatoes were smoking in fryer [3,4]; the advice contradicts the frames and ignores the hazard.
  • B/C medium, t109. Told "The potato in the central fryer is smoking", the cook replied "Wait, sorry, could you quickly tell me whether I chopped the fish before or after I chopped the potato?" and at t111 asked about the trash, at t116 asked for a potato alert. Three scripted probes in eight ticks with a smoking fryer make the cook look oblivious.
  • A medium, t108-t116. The composite fires the probe/proactive sub-cases back-to-back with no regard for the kitchen state; the proactive "tell me when the potatoes are done" arrived 40 ticks after they were done.
  • F medium, t109/t114. Answer capture is off by one: q_order (t108) was "answered" by the smoke warning at t109 (ungradable); the real answer "Fish does not need chopping" (t114) was captured as the answer to q_happened (t111), whose real answer "Yes, you went to the trash earlier" came at t117.
  • E high, t109-t115. After the cook's replace [counter (5,3), put down, fish source 1, pick up, pan 1, put down] (t103) the body did put down (t108) then go to the fryer with the fish [rejected], turn on [failed], go to the fryer with the potato ×2, turn on [done] (t109-t115) — none of these are in the cook's plan; they match the canonical recipe plan. Likewise ACTION t121: go to the plates [done] before the cook's t117 plan (fish source, pick up, pan, put down, turn on) began at t126.

Episode: classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2.txt

Outcome: won at t351, score 0.6963. Steak Frites (steak, potato, potato). 7 planned sub-cases.

(1) Narrative

mis_prep (onion for steak) was caught at pick-up (t7, early_correct) and the cook trashed the onion (t28). From then on the assistant spoon-fed every step. The same false rule was repeated for the fryers (t275 "fryers turn on automatically"), which the cook disproved by trying to scoop (t281 waiting). Two hallucinated false alarms (t89 "That is the onion crate", t329 "You added pepper to the plate") and a search false alarm caused by the body walking to a cutting board (t22). The dish was served correctly at t351.

Cost: steak raw t64→t246 (182 ticks), fryers idle t131/t168→t298/t311.
  • D high, t329. add_foreign -> false_alarm: "You added pepper to the plate instead of fries." — plate was steak + potato; no pepper exists in this recipe.
  • D medium, t89. mis_prep -> false_alarm: "That is the onion crate. Grab a potato from the crate next to it" — ACTION t89: go to potato source 1 [done], pick up t91 yielded a potato that was chopped and fried.
  • E/D medium, t19-t22. Cook's plan ["go to trash 1","trash"] (t11), body ACTION t19: go to the cutting board [done] (canonical next step); assistant flagged search -> false_alarm "That's a cutting board, not the trash can." The harness leak produced an assistant penalty.

(after pick up [done] t260); t315 "Pick up the plate ..." (after pick up [done] t315); t319 "Scoop the fries" while the cook is walking to do it; t234 "Turn on the burner ... where the meat is sitting" then t238 "That pan is empty" (cook was at pan 3 on the assistant's own t206 instruction).

  • C medium, throughout. Cook acknowledges every instruction with a near-identical line ("Got it, ...", "Sounds good, ...", "On it, ...", "Yep, ..." — 30+ times). Persona-consistent but reads as scripted.
  • C low, t218. "it says once it's in a station it has to cook" — the cook quotes engine feedback text as "it says".

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. Accept without repair / residual queue keeps executing after a release or an accept. Class E (harness) + B. Severity high. Count 8.
  • file 10 t65-t68: correct alias_in_window flag, move=accept, plan_update drop [], wrong_serve 3 ticks later.
  • file 1 t158-t185: accept "Let me trash this plate", body goes to the rice pot and waits 24 ticks.
  • file 2 t46, file 6 t50, file 11 t199: accept + replace [trash, trash], body still executes go to the serving pass (in-flight command survives the replace).
  • file 9 t109-t115, t121; file 11 t124, t274-t275; file 12 t19: canonical-plan commands the cook never authored run ahead of the cook's new plan (go to the fryer with the fish [rejected], turn on [failed], go to the plates, go to the cutting board).
  • file 3 t43 / file 7 t43-t45: the takeover block re-issues itself once after release (second cheese plated in file 7).

    • Diagnosis: harness rule. plan_update {"op":"drop","actions":[]} is a no-op, so an "accept" that describes a repair in words but leaves the queue alone lets the pre-error/takeover queue run; and a replace does not cancel the command already in flight. Fix candidates: flush the takeover queue on release; when the cook emits accept with no plan change, block the queue until a plan arrives (this is the "prompt-only slip" already on record as the bug).

  1. Both LLM loops go dark mid-episode. Class E. Severity high. Count 3.
  • file 2: last poll t101, last cook turn t88, idle t103-t133, timeout.
  • file 4: last poll t105, no ACTION lines for the bun/potato scoops or the serve, wrong_serve t130.
  • file 5: last poll t160→t164, last cook turn t164, idle t182-t212, timeout.

    • Diagnosis: harness (an exception or rate-limit that silently stops both callers while the engine clock runs; the sampled "stalled" category is at least partly this, not cook livelock). Both engine revisions are affected.

  1. Schedule dies after the first deviation: "after N clean commands" never satisfied, anchors never recur. Class A. Severity high. Count 5 (plus 2 legitimately schedule-limited).
  • file 12: q_ready_in_28 armed t8, never injected in 343 ticks; five sub-cases blocked.
  • file 2: q_past_done_5 armed t18, never injected in 116 ticks.
  • file 11: q_assistant_said_30 armed t272, never injected in 160 ticks.
  • file 1 (add_foreign anchor), file 9 (leave_unattended anchor), file 5 (leave_unattended anchor): anchors defined on the clean plate/prep trajectory cannot recur once a plate has been trashed and rebuilt.
  • files 3, 7, 10: q_happened_9 / q_order_9 armed with < 8 ticks of dish left — over-packed easy cases.

    • Diagnosis: case design + harness counter. The clean-command counter and the anchor matcher are keyed to the canonical trajectory; every ruinous error forces a rebuild that leaves that trajectory for good, so the composite effectively reduces to its first error. The mis_prep/serve_raw slot-0 cases are the worst: they guarantee a rebuild before anything else can fire.

  1. Assistant asserts false kitchen physics ("stations turn on automatically") and hallucinates station state. Class D. Severity high. Count 2 episodes, 9 utterances.
  • file 12 t71, t199, t206, t275; file 11 t366, t392, t411, t421, t429.
  • Related "done/cooking" hallucinations: file 1 t139 (rice done, pot off), file 5 t87 (fish ready, half-cooked), file 11 t178/t182/t214 (cooked fish in an unlit pan).

    • Diagnosis: model. It does not read the burner state from the frames and fills in with a prior; it also repeats the claim under direct contradiction from the cook (file 11 t407-t421). This single belief produced one stall (file 11) and ~250 wasted ticks (file 12).

  1. Silence through long takeovers and while food smokes. Class D. Severity high. Count 6.
  • Takeovers: file 8 t0-t109 (36 silent polls), file 11 t0-t73, file 9 t0-t70, file 6 t0-t38, file 1 t49-t69, file 2 t0-t17.
  • Smoke: file 1 t162-t218 (13 polls), file 6 t179-t251 (23 polls, fire), file 9 t117-t143 (fire).

    • Diagnosis: model, aggravated by case design — mis_prep with a recipe ingredient as the "wrong" item (potato for fish, lettuce for tomato) and serve_raw are absences until commit. But the smoke cases are pure detection failures: the assistant had flagged the same pan smoking earlier (file 1 t153) and went quiet.

  1. Hallucinated objects and false alarms. Class D. Severity high/medium. Count 7 flagged fps.
  • file 1 t26 (tortilla-before-rice with no plate), t36 (raw steak on a non-existent plate); file 11 t144 ("That's the meat crate" — it was fish); file 12 t89 ("That is the onion crate" — it was potato), t329 ("You added pepper"); file 10 t29/t39 (announced lettuce detour flagged twice); file 12 t22 (search fp caused by the harness leak in issue 1).

    • Diagnosis: model (object identity from small sprites) plus one harness-induced fp.

  1. Grading credits/verdicts contradict the timeline. Class F. Severity high. Count 9.
  • Proactive notice given but graded no_answer: file 4 t99, file 5 t96.
  • convinced=False/prevented=False after early_correct + accept: file 1 t158, file 12 t7 (vs file 11 t271 prevented=True).
  • Same flag with two verdicts: file 8 t132 (early_alias in summary, fp_preinjection on timeline).
  • Correct, still-preventive flag graded late while the sub-case is open: file 6 t256.
  • Correct diagnosis graded false_alarm instead of late/true_observation: file 2 t19, t44; file 7 t45; file 5 t126; file 1 t78.
  • Question answer capture takes the next utterance regardless of topic: file 5 t92, file 9 t109/t114; 9 of 10 answered questions graded ungradable (files 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11), so the QA dimension is effectively unscored in this sample.

    • Diagnosis: harness/grader. The max_hold_plate_committed archive-on-commit rule makes every post-commit correct flag uncredited for serve_raw/add_foreign; the proactive grader and the QA capture need topic matching.

  1. Takeover holds too long or beyond commit. Class A. Severity high. Count 3.
  • file 8: omit_subgoal held through plate commit (t142) to serve (t153), never released.
  • file 8/11: mis_prep held 109 ticks (three potatoes) — "deviated block" spans the whole prep of a recipe ingredient.
  • file 6: leave_unattended (loops=2) held ~90 ticks with the cook wandering until fire; never released.

  1. Engine resolution / rejection quirks. Class E. Severity medium. Count 4.
  • file 1 t132 go to pot 1 executed as go to the pan with the steak (rice never started).
  • file 1 t91 go to the empty cutting board [rejected] while board 3 accepted a put-down.
  • file 2 t68 go to the meat source / go to the empty pan rejected; explicit ids succeed 9 ticks later.
  • Duplicate consecutive go to X [done] lines in files 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12 (cosmetic, but they inflate visit counts used by count:visit questions).
  1. Latency spikes. Class D/E. Severity medium. Count 12 calls ≥ 7 s. file 5 (28.7 s, 24.5 s, 8.9 s), file 6 (17.6 s), file 11 (13.6, 9.4, 9.1, 8.8, 8.5, 8.3, 7.9 s), file 12 (11.4 s), file 10 (9.8 s), file 1 (7.9 s). In file 5 the spike swallowed an entire takeover window. Diagnosis: API side; the harness should log a "missed window" marker when a poll straddles an injection.
  1. Cook denies or contradicts its own feedback under takeover; novice argues like an expert. Class B/C. Severity medium. Count 5. file 1 t153 (not smoking), file 6 t122/t256/t263, file 8 t143-t151, file 11 t79-t107, file 9 t109-t116 (scripted probes over smoke). Diagnosis: harness/prompt — the takeover voice template overrides persona; the composite fires probes without checking kitchen hazards.
  1. Assistant narrates every step / repeats stale instructions. Class D/C. Severity low-medium. Count 3 episodes (files 12, 11, 10) — unprompted micro-steps (file 10 t3/t15), post-hoc repeats (file 12 t212, t260, t315, t319), stale-by-latency instructions (file 3 t53, file 7 t71, file 11 t113). Diagnosis: model style + prompt (it answers the novice's every "what next?" but also volunteers).
  1. Mixed engine revisions in one sweep. Class E. Severity medium. 7 episodes on 4d15175f2d, 5 on 4d23961171 (files 4, 5, 8, 10, 11). Any comparison across the sweep is confounded until the diff is confirmed harmless.

What this assistant does well

  • Short, actionable lines. Almost every utterance is one or two sentences with a concrete station ("trash can by the top right", "pan along the top counter"). No lectures, no lists.
  • Fast when healthy. Typical latency 2-4 s (2-4 ticks); it can react inside a plating window (file 12 t7 at pick-up, file 11 t271 before the peas were scooped).
  • Correct plating-order diagnoses whenever the plate is visible. file 1 t158, file 2 t44, file 5 t121, file 6 t49, file 7 t45, file 9 t79, file 10 t65, file 11 t194 — every one names the actual plate contents correctly; the failures there are grading windows, not detection.
  • Saves the serve when the body heads to the pass. file 2 t49, file 6 t52, file 8 t147/t151, file 11 t194 — "Don't serve that!" was on time four times.
  • Retracts when challenged. file 5 t92 "My mistake, you're right—it's still cooking." then a correct notice at t96.
  • Good recall answers. file 3 t63, file 9 t117 ("Yes, you went to the trash earlier"), file 10 t55 board count.
  • Pragmatic hazard triage. file 11 t190 "Scoop the smoking potato to save the kitchen, but remember you will need to trash that plate afterward" balances fire and order correctly.
  • Stays quiet during announced detours (files 3, 7 side quests) — except file 10 where it did not.

GPT-5.4 mini — auditor 报告（英文）

CookSim sweep v4 audit — assistant gpt-5.4-mini

Twelve sampled episodes (three per sampler category, four personas' worth). All ticks below are live ticks from the transcripts; every quotation is verbatim. Engine revisions in the sample are mixed: ten episodes carry 4d15175f2d, two (classic_expert__burned__b3_master_nops_hard_map_5, classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3) carry 4d23961171.

Anomaly classes: A-scheduling, B-cook, C-conversation, D-assistant, E-engine/physics, F-grading. Severity: high / medium / low as defined in the brief.

A note on one empirical constant used below: in the four burned episodes the FIRE came 70-85 ticks after the station's COOKED event (ep2 potatoes ~t86 -> t158; ep5 steak ~t142 -> t226; ep6 steak ~t145 -> t230; ep9 potato ~t134 -> t204). Ep9's other potato was scooped after 68 ticks on heat and survived. I therefore treat "on heat > ~30 ticks after COOKED" as genuinely smoking when judging leave_unattended flags.

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_4.txt

Case b3_master_nops_medium_map_4, persona pv2_baseline, burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce), outcome wrong_serve at t249, eval: blank, failed_at=injection_skipped.

(1) Narrative. The cook prepped rice, steak, tomato and lettuce cleanly (t7-t96), asked at t113 for a shout when the steak was done (never given), and was taken over for a search at t119-t138 (two "wander/idle (thinking)" looks, never flagged). It plated tortilla (t150) and rice (t161); the wrong_order takeover (lettuce before steak) began at t161 and was correctly flagged at t170, accepted and prevented. Immediately after the accept, however, the body executed a stale block: it walked to the tortilla crate and scooped a second tortilla (t177), then tried to scoop from the now-empty rice pot (t178, rejected). Steak (t188) and tomato (t197) went on, giving a five-item plate tortilla, rice, tortilla, steak, tomato. The cook then stood idle for 44 ticks (t201-t245) saying it was adding the lettuce, its lettuce scoop failed at t243, and it served the incomplete plate at t249 while the assistant chanted "Serve it now at the pass".

(2) Category mechanics (anchor_never_reached). Sub-case [3] omit_subgoal(assemble(tomato)) got its turn when wrong_order was archived at t171 (window [161,193]); its anchor is the clean-tick-150 plate tortilla, rice, steak. That plate never existed: at t177 the spurious second tortilla made the plate tortilla, rice, tortilla, and every later state carried the duplicate. The anchor was made unreachable by a stale queued block executed after the accept, not by the assistant and not by the schedule; the resulting five-item plate is also the most plausible reason the t243 lettuce scoop [failed] (recipe length reached), which produced the wrong serve.

(3) Anomalies.

  • E-engine, high, t175-t178. After COOK t170 [move=accept] with plan_update: drop, the body ran ACTION t175: go to the tortilla source [done], ACTION t177: scoop [done] -> PLATE: +tortilla (raw), ACTION t178: go to the pot with the rice [rejected], scoop [orphan-skipped]. Nobody ordered a tortilla: the wrong_order takeover was at the lettuce board, the cook's voice at t175 asked about the steak. This looks like the search sub-case's resume block (before: assemble(tortilla)) or the assembly plan from the anchor being replayed on release; it ruined the plate and the episode.
  • A-scheduling, high, t171-t249. omit_subgoal: injected_at=None ... archived_at=249 although the dish progressed through steak and tomato; anchor unreachable for the reason above. The episode has no eval score at all.
  • B-cook, high, t201-t245. ~~ IDLE t201-t245 (44 ticks, pos (5, 4) holding {... tortilla, rice, tortilla, steak, tomato ...}) ~~ while the voice said at t201 "grabbing the chopped lettuce now", t206 "Adding the lettuce now and taking it straight over to the pass", t213 "Taking it over to the serving pass right now". Three consecutive empty [move=accept close] turns (t226, t233, t238).
  • B-cook / E-engine, high, t243-t249. ACTION t243: scoop [failed]; COOK t245: "Wait, my scoop on the chopped lettuce failed, so it's not actually on the plate yet." — and the episode ends wrong_serve at tick 249: the body served four ticks after the voice said the plate was incomplete. The scoop failure itself is unexplained in the transcript (lettuce had been chopped at t96 and the cook was at "the cutting board with the lettuce" since t202).
  • D-assistant, high, t170-t187. The correct flag carried a wrong instruction: t170 "Stop there — the next item is the tomato, not the lettuce" (steak was next); t181 "Yes — steak goes before tomato ... Put the tomato on next, then the lettuce, then serve" (self-contradictory); t187 "Stop — tomato comes before lettuce" in reply to COOK t183: "The steak in the pan is smoking, so I have to scoop that out first". Had the cook obeyed, the plate would have been out of order; the smoke report was ignored.
  • D-assistant, high, t213-t245. "Serve it հիմա — take the finished burrito straight to the pass" (t213), "Serve it now at the pass." (t226, t233, t238, t245), "At the pass now — serve the finished burrito." (t242) — the lettuce was never plated and the cook was idle at (5,4), not at the pass. The assistant urged the wrong serve that ended the episode. The non-English token "հիմա" (Armenian "now") recurs in two other episodes.
  • D-assistant, medium, t113-t126. Proactive request "Could you give me a heads-up the exact moment the steak is done cooking?" (t113). HEAT [3, 2]: ['steak'] COOKED logged before t126, yet ASSISTANT t126: "Steak is still cooking in the pan." Live grade no_answer. The steak then sat on heat until t188 (~64 ticks) without a single warning.
  • D-assistant, medium, t39/t76/t100. "Looks like you've got the plate" (cook holds raw steak: COOK t39: "I'm holding a raw steak right now, not a plate"); "You've got the tomato on the plate now" (tomato on a board: COOK t76: "the tomato isn't on a plate yet"); "Lettuce looks chopped now — scoop it onto the plate next" (no plate; lettuce is last: COOK t100: "lettuce goes on last").
  • D-assistant, medium, t119-t138. search takeover (two looks: ACTION t128: wander/idle (thinking), t137) never flagged; the assistant endorsed the movement at t133 ("Yes — ... go straight to the tortilla crate").
  • D-assistant, low, t150-t157. Three near-verbatim messages in seven ticks ("rice next from the pot, then steak, tomato, and lettuce in that exact order").
  • F-grading, low, t162. Header lists (162, 'leave_unattended', 'true_observation') and the timeline prints [flag verdict] leave_unattended -> true_observation, but the spoken line is stamped ASSISTANT t162 [flag=fp_preinjection]. Two different verdict labels for one flag. (On the world state the flag was true: rice had been on heat since ~t124 and the frame was captured at t158, before the t161 scoop.)
  • C-conversation, low. Empty cook utterances at t119 (under the search injection), t153, t157, t226, t233, t238.

Worked well. The wrong_order flag at t170 was correct, accepted ("Oh, you're right, I completely mixed up the sequence") and the lettuce-before-steak mistake was prevented. The cook's perception corrections at t39, t76, t100, t126, t162 were all accurate.
