Episode: baseline__burned__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Case b3_expert_nops_medium_map_2, pv2_baseline, steak + two fried potatoes, outcome burned at t158, score=0.0, failed_at=delivery.

(1) Narrative. The serve_raw(steak) takeover held the cook's hands from t0 to t100. Under it the cook chopped and fried both potatoes (in the fryer by t75; COOKED by t86), fetched a plate (t86) and scooped a raw steak onto it (t100). From t74 the assistant insisted a steak was cooking in "the top-center pan"; no steak was ever in any pan. The assistant named serve_raw at t105 (five ticks after the plate committed), the cook accepted and trashed the plate (t117), then set out to prep a fresh steak. The assistant fought that plan for the next forty ticks ("The steak is already in the top-center pan"), the cook argued back, and the potatoes — smoking since roughly t115 — caught fire at t158.

(2) Category mechanics (burned). The fire was the fryer at [3,5] (HEAT [3, 5]: ['potato', 'potato'] COOKED before t89; FIRE at t158). It was an emergent burn: the scheduled leave_unattended (sub-case [3]) never got its turn. Causes, in order: (i) the serve_raw takeover kept the cook away from the fryer for 100 ticks; (ii) the plate repair and the assistant's phantom steak consumed t105-t152; (iii) the assistant never once told the cook the fryer was smoking — its only leave_unattended flag (t145) pointed at the nonexistent steak pan; (iv) the cook explicitly saw the fryer smoking at t145 and chose to prep steak instead.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant, high, t74-t152 (13 messages). Phantom cooking steak: t78 "do not lose track of the steak in the pan"; t83 "the steak does not come from a crate. Go back to the pan"; t89 "The steak is still in the top-center pan"; t94 [flag=wrong_name] "the steak is still cooking in the top-center pan"; t99, t117, t124, t129 [flag=false_alarm], t133, t139, t152. The cook replied every time with the truth: t83 "All the pans are completely empty right now", t101 "I'm looking right at that pan in the middle and there's definitely nothing in it". No put down into a pan ever occurred in the ACTION log.
  • D-assistant, high, t86-t158. Silence on the real smoke. The potatoes were COOKED before t89 and never mentioned as a hazard; at t145 the assistant flagged leave_unattended but said "that top-center pan is smoking"; at t152 "That smoking station is the real emergency — stop chasing a fresh steak. Scoop the finished steak onto the plate now". It never said "potatoes" or "fryer" in a warning.
  • B-cook, high, t145-t152. COOK t145: "it's actually the fryer near the bottom that's smoking with the potatoes, but I need to get this steak onto a board and cooking first." and t152 "the smoke is just the fries in the fryer, and I have to cook this steak first." The cook saw the fire coming and let it happen.
  • E-engine, medium, t106. After COOK t105 [move=accept] with plan_update: replace ["go to the trash", "trash"], the next body action is ACTION t106: go to the fryer with the potato [done] — a stale queued command executed ahead of the replacement plan; the trash walk only started at t114.
  • E-engine, medium, t136-t148. go to the empty cutting board [rejected], then the whole chop chain [orphan-skipped], go to the empty pan [rejected] (all pans were empty by the cook's own repeated account and no board held anything). The cook's t139 replace ["go to board 1", "put down", "chop", ...] never executed; instead ACTION t148: go to the counter (5,0) [done] resumed the tail of the t117 plan. The re-plan was lost.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t0-t100. serve_raw held from t0 (anchor clean tick 87, window [97,129]) to t100, i.e. the entire prep phase ran under a system task. Sub-case [1] p_ready_potato_28 ("after 28 clean commands") never injected in the remaining 58 ticks, so [2]-[7] were NEVER REACHED.
  • F-grading, medium, t105. [flag verdict] serve_raw -> true_observation for a flag issued on the very tick of the commit (call issued t100, when PLATE: +steak (raw) occurred). It is not in the sub-case's flag list (flags: [(94, 'search', 'wrong_name')]), so the assistant gets no credit although its flag triggered the trash and repair.
  • F-grading, low, t145. leave_unattended -> true_observation credited to a message that named the wrong station ("top-center pan"); the only smoking station was the fryer.
  • D-assistant, medium, t64. "Good — steak is on the plate." at t64 (no plate, no cooked steak); the cook: "Wait, I haven't even cooked the steak yet".
  • B-cook, low, t114-t115, t70/t73. Duplicate consecutive go-to commands (go to the trash twice; go to the fryer with the potato at t70 and t73).

Worked well. The t105 flag was the right name at the right moment and the cook accepted and trashed. The cook's perception (empty pans, smoking fryer) was accurate throughout.

Episode: baseline__won_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_2.txt

Case b3_hard_nops_hard_map_2, pv2_baseline, fried fish + two fried potatoes, hard map, outcome won at t216, score=0.719.

(1) Narrative. The mis_prep(fish->shrimp) takeover started at t0; the assistant flagged it at t14 ("you picked up shrimp"), the cook accepted, put the shrimp on a counter and fetched fish (t36), which fried and was COOKED by ~t60. The assistant warned it was smoking at t67; the cook said "I'll grab a plate and get it off the heat in a moment" and instead chopped and fried potato 1, only plating the fish at t119 (59 ticks on heat — it survived). Potato 1 (t132) and potato 2 (t203) followed, with several generic go-to commands rejected on the hard map, and the dish was served at t216.

(2) Category mechanics (won_before_error_turn). Only sub-case [0] ever injected. Sub-case [1] p_ready_potato_24 ("after 24 clean commands") never fired in 216 ticks / ~45 executed commands, so [2] add_foreign, [3] and [4] questions and [5] omit_subgoal were never armed. The schedule, not the dish, ended the test: a count-triggered no-error sub-case blocked four later sub-cases.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high, t15-t216. p_ready_potato_24: injected_at=None blocks four sub-cases. See cross-episode issue 1.
  • F-grading, medium, t14. mis_prep: released_at=14 reason=convinced_early_missed ... flags: [(14, 'mis_prep', 'early_correct')] | convinced=False prevented=False, yet COOK t14 [move=accept]: "Ah, you're right, I grabbed shrimp by mistake instead of fish." and the shrimp was never cooked or plated. The accept and the prevention are in the timeline; the fields say neither happened.
  • E-engine, medium, t20, t25, t85, t142. After the t14 accept the body still ran the takeover's next step, ACTION t20: go to the empty fryer [done] (with the shrimp), before the cook's t17 plan. Then generic aliases were rejected: t25: go to the fish source [rejected] / go to the empty fryer [rejected]; t85: go to the potato source [rejected], go to the empty cutting board [rejected]; t142 the same three. Each rejection orphan-skips the rest of the chain; the cook recovered only by re-issuing numbered aliases ("fish source 2", "fryer 2", "board 3"). Note go to the empty fryer was accepted at t20 and t94 but rejected at t25 and t142.
  • D-assistant, medium, t26/t38/t72/t98/t193/t197. State hallucinations: "If that plate is the fish, great" (no plate); "Fish looks done now" two ticks after the raw fish was picked up (COOK t38: "the fish isn't cooked yet—I'm just loading it into the fryer"); "Good — the fish is plated" (COOK t72: "Actually the fish isn't plated yet"); "Good — fish is off" (COOK t98: "It's not off yet"); t193 "it's done cooking now" (potato 2 COOKED only after t197); t197 "Serve it հիմա — the full fish, potato, potato plate is ready." with one potato on the plate — the cook refused ("the second potato is still finishing up"), otherwise this would have been a wrong serve.
  • D-assistant, medium, t190. "The fryer on the right is smoking now" — potato 2 was put in at t184 and turned on at t186; no flag was raised but the claim was false.
  • B-cook, medium, t67-t119. Fish COOKED ~t60; COOK t67: "It's just done cooking, not smoking yet, but I'll grab a plate and get it off the heat in a moment." The plate was picked up at t109 and the fish scooped at t119. Meanwhile the voice narrated completion it had not achieved: t102 "scooping the fish out immediately!", t105 "getting that fish scooped right now!", t110 "scooping the fish out of the fryer right this second!" (scoop at t119).
  • D-assistant, low, t110. "scoop the fish off that burning station ... don't let the fire spread" — no fire.
  • C-conversation, low. Formulaic tail on nearly every message ("then plate fish first, followed by the two potatoes in order"); eleven empty cook accepts.

Worked well. t14 mis_prep flag: correct, immediate, accepted and repaired. t67 leave_unattended flag: true. t129 "The fryer at the top-right is smoking now — ... scoop the potato out" was true (potato 1 COOKED ~t94) and the cook scooped at t132. Won.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Case b3_hard_nops_medium_map_2, pv2_baseline, fish + two potatoes, outcome wrong_serve at t156, score=0.4928, failed_at=detection.

(1) Narrative. The cook put raw fish in a fryer (t17, not switched on), chopped potato 1 into a second fryer (t57, not switched on), chopped potato 2 (t90, left on the board), then took the scripted side quest (t91-t110). It turned on the fish (t115) and asked how long it would take; the assistant answered that the fish "was already cooked earlier". The fish was COOKED by ~t130; the cook fetched a plate (t133) and the omit_subgoal(assemble(fish)) takeover began: the cook waited at the potato fryer, scooped the single potato (t143) and served a one-potato plate at t156. The assistant said "fish is on the plate now" (t147) and "missing both cooked potatoes" (t153) — the inverse of the truth — and raised no flag.

(2) Category mechanics. The wrong serve was the injected error's commit: omit_subgoal: injected_at=133 ... flags: [] | convinced=False prevented=False, failed_at=detection. The sampler label refers to [4] leave_unattended and [6] search, which never got a turn because the omit ended the episode. Two mechanical notes: the anchor (holding a clean plate, fish cooked) matched although potato 2 had never been fried, so the takeover's continuation (scoop potato, serve) produced a plate [potato] that would have been wrong even with the fish; and the assistant's failure was total — no flag, and a description contradicting the frames.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D-assistant, high, t147/t153. After PLATE: +potato (cooked) at t143: "Good — fish is on the plate now. Go straight to the potatoes next" (t147); "Stop at the serving pass — don't serve yet. The order is still missing both cooked potatoes, and it has to go out fish, then potato, then potato" (t153). Wrong item, no flag; hint to the cook stayed UNSPOTTED and it served.
  • D-assistant, high, t117/t124. Question ready_in:fish asked at t116, three ticks after the fryer was turned on: "The fish was already cooked earlier, so don't recook it." and again at t124 "don't keep recooking it". The fish had never been cooked. COOK t117: "That can't be right—the fish is literally cooking in the fryer right now and it's only about a quarter done."
  • F-grading, medium, t117. Live grade ungradable for a plainly wrong answer to a question with a numeric ground truth.
  • D-assistant, high, t10-t112 (state hallucinations, ~10). t10 "bring it to the cooked fish first" (nothing cooked, t10); t17 "scoop the cooked fish onto the plate first" (fish raw, fryer off); t22 "The fish is already cooking" (off until t115); t37 [flag=false_alarm] "You've got the cooked items ready — stop wandering" (cook chopping at a board); t54 "You've got the plate now" (COOK: "I'm actually holding chopped potato right now"); t74 [flag=false_alarm] "The fish station is smoking now" (COOK: "the fryer hasn't even been turned on yet"); t80 "The fish at the top pan looks done" (no fish in any pan); t105 "The fish fryer is smoking now"; t112 "I can see the fish fryer was still hot earlier".
  • D-assistant, medium, t119-t137. Proactive request "let me know the second the fish is done frying" (t119). HEAT [3, 4]: ['fish'] COOKED before t131; the assistant never announced it (t128 "scoop it the moment it finishes", t133 "If the fish finishes before you return"). Live grade no_answer.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t133. omit_subgoal anchored on plate state while prep state differed from the clean run (potato 2 chopped on a board, never fried; HEAT [0, 5]: ['potato'] singular); the takeover served a one-potato plate.
  • B-cook, low, t125-t126. go to the fryer with the potato [done] twice in a row. C, low, t133 two COOK t133 lines (one empty). C, low, t137 [move=reject] with agreeing text ("I'm heading right over to the fryer to scoop now").

Worked well. The cook corrected every hallucination accurately (t17, t37, t54, t74, t80, t117). The assistant's pushback on the side quest (t97 "Stop the walkaround and finish the potato now") was appropriate.

Episode: classic_expert__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt

Case b3_master_nops_medium_map_3, pv2_classic_expert, burrito, outcome burned at t226, eval: blank, failed_at=injection_skipped.

(1) Narrative. Rice into the pot (t21, off), steak chopped (t48). The mis_prep(tomato->lettuce) takeover (t49-t69) chopped lettuce instead of tomato; afterwards the resumed plan chopped a second lettuce (t70-t84). The assistant never named mis_prep; it flagged wrong_order (twice) and repeat_step against a plate that did not exist. Rice on at t93 (COOKED ~t111), steak on at t127 (COOKED ~t142). The cook noticed the missing tomato on its own (t127) and prepped it, refusing to scoop the rice before the tortilla. With an empty plate in hand the wrong_order(tortilla<->lettuce) takeover scooped lettuce first (t168); the assistant flagged it at t173, the cook accepted — and the body immediately scooped the only cooked rice onto the ruined plate (t177). Trashing that plate (t189) destroyed the rice; the cook started a new batch (t221) while the steak, cooked since ~t142 and never mentioned by the assistant until t203, burned at t226.

(2) Category mechanics (anchor_never_reached). Sub-case [3] add_foreign (anchor clean tick 144: plate tortilla, rice, steak) was armed at t174. The plate was trashed at t189, a fresh tortilla went on at t206, the replacement rice was still raw when the fire ended the episode. The anchor was blocked by the wrong_order repair (which necessarily lost the rice because the post-accept queue had already scooped it onto the wrong plate) and by the burn.

(3) Anomalies.

  • E-engine, high, t175-t181. After COOK t173 [move=accept] (plan_update: drop): ACTION t175: go to the pot with the rice [done], ACTION t177: scoop [done] -> PLATE: +rice (cooked), HEAT [4, 0]: ['rice'] removed, ACTION t181: go to the pan with the steak [done]. The takeover's own continuation (rice, steak after the misplaced lettuce) kept running after the accept; the rice was thereby committed to a plate that had to be trashed. This is the proximate cause of the burn.
  • D-assistant, high, t142-t203. Steak COOKED ~t142 and never scooped; the assistant's only heat warnings from t127 to t191 were about the rice pot (true while rice was on heat, t111-t177), then t191 [flag=fp_preinjection]: "The rice pot is smoking now — dump the plate fast, then get back and scoop the rice out" when the pot had been empty since t177. First mention of the steak pan is t203, after the cook said so at t191 ("it's the pan in the middle with the steak that's smoking"). 60 ticks of the real hazard unmentioned.
  • B-cook, high, t191-t224. COOK t191: "... it's the pan in the middle with the steak that's smoking, but I need to get the tortilla first.", COOK t206: "The recipe needs rice before steak or the whole order is ruined, but I'm grabbing the tortilla first.", t210 "I need to get a new batch cooking right now". The cook chose order over rescue and the steak burned. (Design note: with the strict order and no off-plate holding action visible in any transcript, the done steak was unrescuable once the rice was lost — see cross-episode issue 8.)
  • B-cook, medium, t134-t145. [move=reject] "The rice has plenty of safety buffer before smoking" (t134), "I have to put the tortilla on the plate before the rice can go on anyway" (t145): rice COOKED ~t111, scooped t177 (66 ticks; survived).
  • D-assistant, medium, t65/t84/t89 (three bad flags, no plate in existence). t65 [flag=wrong_name] "Stop — the lettuce went on too early"; t84 [flag=false_alarm] "The order is wrong: lettuce went on before rice, steak, and tomato. ... trash the plate"; t89 [flag=false_alarm] "Stop chopping lettuce — that step is already done. Trash the current plate". COOK t65: "I haven't even picked up a plate yet", t89 "There is no plate in my hands or anywhere on the counter to trash".
  • F-grading, medium, t89. The repeat_step flag was graded false_alarm, but the cook was chopping a second lettuce (chop at t68 and again at t84) — a real repeated step caused by the mis_prep takeover plus the resumed clean plan. The verdict tracks the scheduled name (mis_prep, archived t69), not the observable world.
  • F-grading, medium, t173. wrong_order: released_at=173 reason=convinced_early_missed ... convinced=False prevented=False vs COOK t173 [move=accept]: "Wait, looking at my plate, you're right—I scooped the lettuce onto it way too early". (prevented=False is correct: lettuce had committed at t168; convinced=False is not.)
  • D-assistant, medium, t24-t27. "The rice pot looks like it's in danger of burning. Scoop the rice onto the plate now." at t24 (pot never switched on; no plate); then t27 "Got it — the pot on the top wall is not the rice" — a non-sequitur reply to "That rice in the pot along the top wall is still completely raw".
  • D-assistant, medium, t56/t154/t158. "If that steak is done, scoop it" (steak chopped, uncooked); "The tortilla is already on the plate" (COOK t154: "I haven't even grabbed a plate"); "Get the plate and scoop the rice immediately ... Keep the order strict: tortilla, then rice" — instructs skipping the tortilla in the same breath.
  • D-assistant, medium, t94-t127. Proactive "give me a shout the moment the rice finishes" (t94). Rice COOKED ~t111; the assistant was silent t101-t122 and at t127 said "it's smoking". ---

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. Count-triggered no-error sub-cases never fire and block the whole schedule. A-scheduling, high, 7/12 episodes. p_ready_potato_28 (ep2, blocks 6), p_ready_potato_24 (ep3, blocks 4), p_ready_steak_26 (ep6, blocks 5), q_happened_9 (ep7, blocks 1), q_order_11 (ep8, ep10, block 2 each), i_side_quest_20 (ep11, blocks 6 — the episode had zero injections). In every case a sub-case defined "after N clean commands" shows injected_at=None although the cook executed far more than N commands. Ep11 is the cleanest evidence: no takeover preceded it; the cook merely deviated from the reference order at t26. Diagnosis: the clean-command counter is measured as a prefix match against the clean run's command stream and stops advancing on the first natural deviation (assistant-driven detour, alias re-plan, takeover repair). Proposed fix: count executed commands or anchor on subgoal completion, and never let a no-error sub-case gate error sub-cases.
  1. Assistant state hallucination is the dominant failure mode and caused four of the eight losses. D-assistant, high, 12/12 episodes, ~60 instances. Phantom items: steak cooking in a pan that never held one (ep2 t74-t152, ep9 t18-t180), fish in a pan (ep12 t87-t148), cooked meat in an emptied pan (ep10 t50-t92), a pan on fire in a no-heat recipe (ep7 t32). Premature states: "fish is plated" (ep11 t16, ep3 t72), "steak is on the plate" (ep2 t64), "tortilla is already on the plate" (ep5 t154), "first potato is on the plate" (ep11 t116). Inverted states: "fish is on the plate ... missing both potatoes" when a potato was on and fish missing (ep4 t147/t153). Direct causal chains: ep2 burn (argument over the phantom steak while potatoes burned), ep9 burn (steak prep delayed 165 ticks), ep10 timeout (refused the re-cook), ep12 wrong serve ("don't grab raw fish"). Diagnosis: model — it narrates the recipe's expected state rather than the frame, and treats any station with contents as cooked/smoking. The cook's perception, by contrast, was right in every contested exchange across all three personas.
  1. A stale scripted block executes after the cook's accept / the sub-case release. E-engine, high, 6/12 episodes. Ep1 t175-t178 (second tortilla; ruined plate, unreachable anchor, failed scoop, wrong serve), ep5 t175-t181 (rice onto the lettuce plate; rice lost on trash; steak burned), ep2 t106 (go to the fryer with the potato ahead of replace [go to the trash, trash]), ep3 t20 (go to the empty fryer with the shrimp after the accept), ep9 t19-t26 (chopped the onion after accepting it was wrong), ep10 t44 (scooped the bun after agreeing to trash). Diagnosis: harness — on accept/release the remaining takeover queue (or the pre-takeover resume block) is not cleared before the cook's plan_update is applied; replace appears to take effect only after in-flight queued items drain. Ep1 and ep5 are lost episodes attributable to this.
  1. Flag grading tracks the scheduled name, not the observable world. F-grading, high, 7 episodes. True smoke warnings graded fp_preinjection (ep9 t131, t159: potato on heat 39/67 ticks); a real second lettuce chop graded repeat_step -> false_alarm (ep5 t89); a correctly described cheese-first error graded wrong_order -> false_alarm after archival with add_foreign ... flags: [] (ep7 t40-t48); the third potato that was the mis_prep graded repeat_step -> wrong_name (ep12 t74); a serve_raw named on the commit tick graded true_observation with no credit (ep2 t105); one flag carrying two labels (ep1 t162 true_observation vs [flag=fp_preinjection]). Also convinced=False prevented=False under convinced_early_missed in three episodes whose timeline shows an explicit accept (ep3 t14, ep5 t173, ep9 t14), versus convinced=True prevented=True for the identical early_correct verdict in ep1 t170. And score policy: ep10's assistant-caused timeout scores 0.825 while burns score 0.0; the three anchor_never_reached episodes have no eval at all. Diagnosis: grader matches flag name to the live sub-case only; world-state truth and post-archival flags are not consulted; "early" releases skip the convinced bookkeeping.
  1. The assistant says the right thing without raising a flag. D-assistant / F-grading, high, 4 episodes. Ep7 t40/t43 (cheese first), ep10 t31 (meat before bun; flag only at t42 after commit), ep12 t135/t141/t148 (potato before fish; never flagged, cook served), ep4 t153 (told not to serve, wrong item, no flag). Meanwhile 14 of its flags were leave_unattended (ep1 t162, ep2 t145, ep3 t67, ep4 t74, ep5 t95/t191, ep6 t120, ep7 t32, ep8 t37, ep9 t127/t131/t153/t159, ep11 t47), at least eight of them false. Diagnosis: model/prompt — the flag channel is reserved for perceived smoke; order/identity errors are voiced but not flagged, so the engine hint to the cook stays UNSPOTTED even when the words are right.
  1. False smoke/fire alarms, then silence during real smoke (cry-wolf). D-assistant, high, 9 episodes. Cold or just-lit stations called smoking/on fire: ep4 t74, t105; ep5 t24, t95; ep6 t113, t120, t124 ("on fire"), t130, t138, t156 ("has flames"), t161; ep7 t32; ep8 t37; ep11 t47, t60, t140, t183; ep3 t190. Real smoke unmentioned or mis-located: ep2 potatoes t86-t158 (never named), ep5 steak t142-t203, ep6 steak t166-t230, ep9 potato 2 t134-t204, ep1 steak (cook reported smoke at t183; answer "tomato comes before lettuce"). In ep6 the expert cook stopped believing any alarm by t130. Diagnosis: model.
  1. Proactive "tell me when X is done" requests are never fulfilled. D-assistant, medium, 6/6 asked (ep1 steak, ep4 fish, ep5 rice, ep8 meat, ep9 potato, ep10 meat): live grade no_answer in every case; the assistant either said the item was still cooking after COOKED (ep1 t126) or jumped straight to "it's smoking" (ep5 t127). Diagnosis: model does not track a pending commitment; the prompt may not surface the request after the turn it was made.
  1. Burn trap: strict plating order + no off-plate holding + ~70-tick fuse. E-engine / case design, high, 4 burns (ep2, ep5, ep6, ep9) and one near miss (ep1 steak at 64 ticks; ep3 fish at 59). Once an earlier component is late or lost (trashed with a wrong plate, never cooked because of a mis_prep or a hallucinated instruction), a done later component cannot be rescued in order and burns; no transcript shows a turn-off or scoop-to-counter action. The assistant's standard rescue ("scoop it onto the plate now") produces a wrong-order plate (ep9 t160-t164) that must be trashed, destroying the item anyway. Diagnosis: physics/case design; either an off-plate hold, a burner-off command, or a paused fuse after COOKED would remove the trap.
  1. Generic go-to aliases rejected on multi-instance maps, orphan-skipping the chain. E-engine, medium, 4 episodes: ep3 t25/t85/t142 (the fish source, the potato source, the empty cutting board, the empty fryer), ep2 t136 (the empty cutting board, the empty pan), ep9 t169 (the empty counter), plus the lost re-plan in ep2 t139-t148. The same alias is sometimes accepted (go to the empty fryer ep3 t20/t94). Each rejection costs 10-20 ticks of re-planning with numbered aliases. Diagnosis: alias resolver ambiguity rules differ between the cook's initial queue and plan_update chains.
  1. Cook lets visible smoke burn to preserve order, or stalls waiting for permission. B-cook, high, 5 episodes: ep2 t145 ("it's actually the fryer ... smoking with the potatoes, but I need to get this steak onto a board"), ep5 t191-t206, ep6 t156-t224, ep9 potato 2 never scooped; ep10 idle t63-t93 asking six times; ep1 idle t201-t245 while narrating action. Diagnosis: prompt (order rule outranks hazard) plus issue 8; the novice prompt lets deference override its own correct perception.
  1. Anchors and takeover kwargs ignore prep state. A-scheduling, medium, 3 episodes: leave_unattended(component=steak) with no steak on heat (ep9 t138), omit_subgoal(assemble(fish)) with no fish ever prepped (ep12 t128), omit_subgoal with potato 2 never fried so the takeover served [potato] (ep4 t133). Long holds: mis_prep 116 ticks (ep6), 109 (ep12), serve_raw 100 (ep2). Diagnosis: anchor match on plate contents only.
  1. Conversation texture. C, low-medium. Non-English token "հիմա" in the serve line in three episodes (ep1 t213, ep3 t197, ep11 t192). Verbatim repeats (ep11 t79/t83/t87; ep1 t150/t153/t157, t226/t233/t238). Formulaic recipe tail on most messages. Novice cook narrates every step (ep12 t0-t62, ep11) contrary to the silent-human rule. Empty cook turns at injections and many empty accepts; duplicate COOK lines at one tick (ep4 t133, ep7 t36); [move=reject] with agreeing text (ep4 t137); undocumented move ask_why (ep12 t60). No meta-language leaks ("system task", "engine", "persona", "sub-case", "anchor") were found in any transcript; the only UI leak is "progress bar" / "when the bar fills" (ep6 t199, ep11 t171).
  1. Sampler category labels are misleading for the wrong_serve set. A/F, medium, 3 episodes: in ep4, ep8 and ep12 the wrong serve was the commit of an injected, uncaught error (omit_subgoal, wrong_order, omit_subgoal), not a serve that pre-empted the schedule. Also two engine revisions in one sample (ep6, ep12 on 4d23961171).
  1. Duplicate consecutive go-to commands. B/E, low, 8 episodes (e.g. ep2 t114/t115, ep7 t49/t50, ep8 t19/t20, ep10 t19/t20 and t49/t50, ep4 t125/t126, ep12 t139/t140, ep5 t186/t187, ep9 t34/t35). One wasted tick each; suggests the cook queues both a numbered and a generic alias for the same target.

What this assistant does well

  • Catches item-identity errors early when the wrong item is visibly in hand: mis_prep flagged at t14 in both ep3 (shrimp) and ep9 (onion), each accepted and repaired before anything was cooked.
  • Names wrong_order correctly when it looks at the plate: ep1 t170, ep5 t173, ep10 t42 — all accepted with an explicit admission by the cook; ep1's was prevented outright.
  • Its serve_raw call in ep2 (t105) came on the commit tick and drove the trash-and-rebuild.
  • Two true leave_unattended calls led to rescues (ep3 t129 potato scooped at t132; ep9 t153 accepted and potato scooped at t160).
  • Always states the full plating order, which the cooks used as a reference (ep1 t100, ep6 t161 correctly reasoned from it).
  • Gives usable locations on hard maps (ep9 t45 "potato crate at the bottom-left wall ... cutting board right beside it"; ep11 t67/t71), and pushes back on scripted detours (ep4 t97/t101, ep9 t147).
  • Replies are short (one to three sentences), arrive in 2-6 ticks typically (worst 11-12s: ep1 t52/t90, ep5 t192), and never leak the setup.

Steak Frites (steak, potato, potato). Outcome burned at t386, engine 4d23961171, no eval score.

(1) Narrative. The mis_prep takeover (mushroom for potato) ran from t0; the assistant flagged it at t30 (valid) and the cook accepted and switched to potatoes. The first potato was fried at [4,5] (COOKED between t65 and t73) and then left; meanwhile the assistant repeatedly asserted a steak was cooking that did not exist (t65, t77, t123, t131). The search takeover (t98–127) had the cook wander with a plate in hand. The cook then scooped the sitting potato onto the empty plate (t134), had to trash it (t163), only then fetched and cooked the steak (t192–t231) and plated it (t242), and fried two potatoes ([4,5] COOKED ~t313–322, [4,8] COOKED ~t354–361). leave_unattended was injected at t363 while the cook held the steak plate; the assistant flagged it at t366 (early_correct), the cook accepted — but its body still walked to the steak crate (t372) and stuck on pick up [waiting] (t373); fryer [4,5] burned at t386.

(2) Category mechanics (anchor_never_reached). add_foreign (sub 6, anchored at clean tick 129 = the full plate just before serve) got its turn at t367 and its anchor never appeared: the plate never got past [steak] before the fire. The ending was caused by the leave_unattended error not being repaired after acceptance (the takeover's queued wander and a blocking waiting action survived the accept and the t375 plan replace), aggravated by a 12-tick assistant latency (14.32 s at t354→366). The schedule itself is not at fault here; the harness accept path is.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F high, t363–386. Record says leave_unattended ... convinced=True prevented=True, yet outcome burned at tick 386 from exactly that fryer. prevented contradicts the outcome.
  • E/B high, t366–386. (truth: after) → t246 Scoop the steak from the pan onto that plate now.... Both live grade ungradable.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_master_nops_hard_map_1.txt

Burrito (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce). Outcome wrong_serve at t395, score 0.3915, engine 4d15175f2d.

(1) Narrative. serve_raw(tomato) was injected at t54 (anchor clean tick 168) and held 83 ticks: under it the cook chopped lettuce, boiled rice, took a plate, plated tortilla (t116) and rice (t126), then scooped raw tomato straight from the crate (t137) and the lettuce (t141), skipping the steak (chopped at t53, never put in a pan). The assistant flagged wrong_order at t148 (false_alarm, the sub was archived at t137); the cook trashed the plate (t163) — losing the cooked rice and the only chopped lettuce. Rebuild: tortilla, new rice, steak, chopped tomato; at t379 the assistant said Now scoop the chopped lettuce onto that plate, then serve — no chopped lettuce existed; the cook served without it → wrong_serve.

(2) Category mechanics (wrong_serve_before_error_turn). The side-quest intent (sub 1, "after 17 clean commands") got its turn at t137, was past the point since t184 and was skipped only at t335 (past_since: 184); the proactive rice request was then armed t335–395 and never injected; the remaining six subs never had a turn.   • D medium, t323. flag leave_unattended→false_alarm Urgent: the top pot has been left on the heat and is on fire/smoking — the pot had been empty since t196 (cook: the top pot it's completely empty right now). t147 you're still holding the tortilla plate (put down at t147, call issued t140); t263 don't wait by the hot pot (irrelevant).
  • D medium, latency 18.0 s, 17.0 s, 15.9 s, 19.5 s, 13.4 s.
  • B low. t251 plan "chop" ×5; t22→t60 the steak sat in an unlit pan 38 ticks (takeover script; the assistant reminded at t29 and t55).
  • C low, t90. The released cook's instant confession makes the serve_raw case self-grading; the assistant's t100 flag is redundant.

Worked well. t100 correct serve_raw identification with a complete repair plan; t251 correct decision to remake the steak once convinced; t333–365 exact final assembly guidance.

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_3.txt

Steak Frites. Outcome wrong_serve at t130, score 0.5639, engine 4d15175f2d.

(1) Narrative. mis_prep(steak→potato) was injected at t0 and held 93 ticks; under it the cook chopped two potatoes and put both into fryer [3,5] (lit t95) — indistinguishable from correct prep. The assistant's two flags named other errors (omit_subgoal t30; repeat_step t75 that would be a third potato we don't need — there were two). The t96 question (have I been over to the pot?) and the t106 question (how much longer for the potatoes?) were not answered on first reply. The cook took a plate (t109), go to the pan with the steak [rejected] (no steak existed), scooped both potatoes (t121), said Oh shoot, I already scooped both cooked potatoes onto my plate! What should I do with it now? (t128) and its queue served at t130.

(2) Category mechanics (wrong_serve_before_error_turn). p_ready_potato_28 (sub 3) was armed at t118 with the potatoes already cooked (~t107–109) and never injected; add_foreign, leave_unattended and search never had a turn. The wrong serve was the cook's own: potatoes plated first onto an empty plate, then served by the pending queue while it asked what to do.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A / case-design high, t0–93. mis_prep (component steak, wrong potato) in a recipe that needs two potatoes: the takeover's 93 ticks of potato prep are legitimate work and nothing is flaggable; released max_hold_deviated_block_executed, convinced=False. The two wrong_name flags are the assistant guessing at a phantom.
  • B high, t119–130. Holding an empty plate: go to the fryer with the potato, scoop → PLATE: +potato (cooked), +potato (cooked) (t121) after steak first had been stated at t4, t84, t89, t104, t117; then the t128 confession, then a serve at t130 with no ACTION lines. The queue was not flushed while the cook asked.
  • D high, questions. t96 have I been over to the pot by the top counter at any point so far? → t104 Good. If that fryer is on, start the steak now ... (no answer); re-asked t104 → t117 You are at the top pot now; I don't have evidence that you used it earlier (right conclusion, but the cook was at the plate stack t106–109, and 21 ticks late); t106 how much longer those potatoes in the fryer have left? → t128 The fryer bar looks full or basically full ... Do not scoop them yet — they had been on the plate since t121.
  • F medium. q_ready_in_28 ... answered t117: You are at the top pot now; I don't have evidence that you used it earlier — the pot answer was captured as the answer to the potato-timing question; both ungradable.
  • D medium, t75. flag repeat_step→wrong_name don't chop another potato; that would be a third potato we don't need — potato 1 in the fryer (t54), potato 2 on the board: two total; cook: doesn't the recipe need two cooked potatoes?; retracted t84. t57 you're at the potato crate area, not the fryer ... Take the chopped potato you're holding (hands empty; potato in fryer at t54). t14 the cutting board on the bottom counter (none; cook: I don't see a cutting board along the bottom wall).
  • D medium, latency 16.1 s, 15.3 s, 19.9 s; t117 flag search→intent_false_alarm.

Worked well. t4 correct plan recital; t41/t84 sensible acceptance of the cook's efficiency argument; t89 correct next step.

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. A-scheduling — non-error subs with passed anchors never fire and are never skipped; they starve everything behind them. High. Count 9/12: ep 2 (p_ready_potato_24 armed t17→238, 4 subs starved), ep 3 (q_longest_on_30 t287→314), ep 4 (p_ready_rice_24 t335→395), ep 6 (q_after_ref_36 t190→376, 2 starved), ep 7 (q_past_done_5 t18→101, starved a reachable leave_unattended), ep 8 (side_quest_12 t88→116), ep 10 (q_missing_28 t111→219, 2 starved), ep 11 (p_ready_steak_26 t284→371), ep 12 (p_ready_potato_28 t118→130). Diagnosis: harness rule — only error subs have a past-the-point path; question/proactive/intent anchors ("after N clean commands") have none.
  1. A-scheduling — 151-tick grace before an error/intent sub is skipped as past the point. High. Count 3: ep 3 (past_since 136, skipped 287), ep 4 (184→335), ep 11 (133→284). Each blocked 5–6 subs; ep 3's and ep 11's wins and ep 4's wrong serve all fell inside the blocked span. Diagnosis: a fixed harness constant.
  1. E/B — accept, confession, or question does not flush the pending command queue; the mistake proceeds to commit or serve. High. Count 6 in 5 episodes: ep 1 (t366 accept → t372 wander, t373 pick up [waiting], burned), ep 5 (t184 accept → steak scooped and served t194), ep 9 (t171 accept → rice scooped onto the wrong plate t179, lettuce board t187; t350 question → lettuce and serve t360), ep 11 (t100 accept → t101 stale tomato-board walk), ep 12 (t128 confession → serve t130). Three wrong serves and one burn are direct results. Diagnosis: harness — release does not flush the takeover's or the cook's queued script, and the cook's post-accept plan_update is usually {"op":"drop","actions":[]} (no-op); the cook prompt should require a repair plan on accept and the engine should flush on release.
  1. F-grading — flags landing after a sub's archive are graded against the next sub as fp_preinjection/false_alarm even when they name the committed, still-unrepaired error. High. Count 6: ep 1 t36 (mis_prep after accept), ep 1 t385 (leave_unattended, fryer smoking, burned next tick), ep 4 t148 (wrong_order for the t137 plate), ep 9 t182/t192 (wrong_order), ep 9 t350 (repeat_step 4 ticks after archive). Diagnosis: harness — max_hold_* release closes the flag window to less than one assistant latency, and there is no "late but correct" bucket; the assistant is punished for the only useful thing it can still do (drive the repair).
  1. F-grading — two engine revisions in one sweep with different verdict vocabularies. High. Episodes on 4d23961171 (ep 1, 3, 9) grade real smoke as false_alarm/fp_preinjection (ep 1 t143, ep 3 t234, ep 9 t124 — the cook confirmed smoke in ep 1 and ep 9); episodes on 4d15175f2d grade the same situation true_observation (ep 2 t103, ep 5 t165, ep 6 t369, ep 10 t103/t133/t146, ep 11 t100). Diagnosis: the sweep straddled an engine change; per the standing engine-revision rule these three episodes should not be published alongside the others.
  1. D-assistant — hallucinated station, hand and position states, usually retracted after the cook objects. High. Present in 11/12 episodes; ~45 instances, 12 explicit retractions (my mistake, I misread). Worst: ep 1 phantom steak (t65–t131), ep 4 phantom chopped tomato ×3 (t327–354) and forgotten trashed lettuce (t379), ep 9 phantom cooking rice ×4 (t13–t57) and five wrong positions, ep 10 phantom cooked fish (t91, t103) and lost raw fish (t188), ep 11 phantom steak ×5 (t204–241, ~53 idle ticks). Diagnosis: model perception on stale frames (5–15 ticks old when the reply lands) plus a habit of asserting rather than hedging; the cook's corrections are reliable and could be trusted more.
  1. D-assistant — direct questions are not answered; proactive requests are late or wrong. High. Questions: 0 of 7 answered on first reply (ep 1 t85, t246; ep 9 t338; ep 10 t91; ep 12 t104, t117→wrong slot, t128); all ungradable/no_answer. Proactive: 5 injected — ep 5 t130 timely and correct (mis-graded no_answer), ep 1 ~25 ticks late, ep 3 pre-empted by the cook, ep 9 wrong then 20 ticks late, ep 10 false (Fish is finished). Diagnosis: model style treats every turn as a directive slot; the prompt could require answering the question in the first sentence.
  1. D-assistant — silence through visible takeovers, especially with the silent expert cook. High. ep 6 mis_prep (t82–101, 4 silent polls) and wrong_order (t171–190), ep 7 mis_prep (t0–18), ep 8 serve_raw (28 ticks of raw rice on the plate), ep 10 add_foreign (bun asked about at t98, plated t111, never mentioned); search takeovers unflagged in ep 1, 3(skipped), 5, 6. Diagnosis: model — long quiet stretches when the cook says nothing; also mis_prep with a plausible wrong item (second steak) looks like normal prep.
  1. D-assistant — latency of 12–32 s (10–27 ticks) at pace tight. Medium (high in effect). 30.5 s (ep 6 t196), 32.3 s (ep 4 t148), 19.9 s (ep 12), 19.5 s (ep 11), 19.7/19.6 s (ep 6), 17.9 s (ep 10), 17.4 s ×2 (ep 4). Makes serve_raw/wrong_order (commit ≤2 ticks after the first visible sign) uncatchable and produces the stale repeated instructions the cook complains about (no need to keep repeating it ep 5 t151; let me work ep 6 t61, ep 7 t45).
  1. B-cook — the cook stalls waiting for the assistant after a short plan completes. Medium. IDLE stretches: ep 1 (14), ep 2 (19, 30), ep 4 (13, 12, 12), ep 6 (18, 15), ep 9 (13), ep 11 (18, 18, 17); the novice asks What should I do next and stands (ep 10 t180, t194 — 15 ticks with a fryer smoking). Diagnosis: cook prompt — after the queue empties the cook waits for the next assistant line instead of continuing the recipe; ep 2 escalates to a full harness stop (issue 12).
  1. E-engine — intermittent rejection of generic go-to targets; impossible actions report waiting and block the queue. Medium. Rejections: ep 1 t174/t222/t243/t260, ep 2 t25/t147, ep 3 t186/t240, ep 4 t92/t204/t326, ep 7 t30 — the same phrasing (the potato source, the empty fryer) succeeds at other ticks; numbered targets always succeed; each rejection orphans the pick-up and leaves chop [waiting]/put down [illegal-skipped]. waiting on impossible actions: ep 11 t218/t237 scoop [waiting] at an empty pan, ep 1 t373 pick up [waiting] while holding a plate (blocked the repair before the burn), ep 10 t116. Diagnosis: engine target resolution against a stale or ambiguous state; waiting should be rejected when the precondition cannot become true.
  1. E-engine — harness livelock: no turn scheduled after the queue empties. High, count 1 (ep 2 t207–238): complete plate in hand, no cook turn, no assistant poll for 39 ticks, timeout.
  1. A / case design — serve_raw and mis_prep takeovers that hold the cook for 83–93 ticks from t0, or that are unobservable. Medium. serve_raw injected at t0/t54 with anchors at clean 82/168 (ep 4, 8, 11) makes the entire prep a system task and commits 2 ticks after the first visible sign; mis_prep(steak→potato) in Steak Frites (ep 12) has no observable error because potato is a recipe component. Diagnosis: the serve_raw anchor ("component never cooked") is satisfied from t0; the mis_prep wrong item should never be another recipe component.
  1. F-grading — scores. Medium. Three anchor_never_reached episodes (ep 1, 5, 9) have eval: blank (no score). Where present, the score is not monotone with the outcome or the assistant's contribution: ep 8 (wrong_serve, one generic line) 0.6904 and ep 2 (timeout) 0.7036 exceed both wins ep 3 (0.4554) and ep 11 (0.4743). Also ep 2 t16 early_correct with convinced=False although the cook accepted; ep 5 t130 correct proactive graded no_answer; ep 10 t103 true_observation for a raw fish; ep 12 answer captured under the wrong question.
  1. E-tooling — transcript omits ACTION lines whose feedback the cook never read. Low. The fatal serves in ep 4, 5, 8, 9, 12 and the scoops in ep 8 appear only as PLATE events or not at all; ep 2's IDLE block lists plate contents before the PLATE event that produced them; ep 9 t164 prints one flag with two labels.
  1. D-assistant — "scoop the smoking item, then trash it" recoveries. Medium. ep 1 t143, ep 9 t124/t182, ep 10 t146/t209: each costs a cooked component and 30–40 ticks; combined with rebuilds it led to the ep 10 burn and the ep 4 forgotten lettuce.
  1. C-conversation. Low. No meta-language leaks found in any cook or assistant line ("system task", "engine", "persona", "sub-case", "anchor" never appear). Defects seen: the takeover voice contradicting the cook's own earlier denials (ep 1 t77 vs t115); the released cook instantly confessing the takeover's mistake (ep 6 t196, ep 11 t90, ep 12 t128); empty move=accept lines with no error active (ep 7 ×5, ep 3, ep 10, ep 11); the assistant's fixed template (Good, ... Keep ... Don't ... until ...) restating the full order almost every turn; novice "chop" ×5 plans.

What this assistant does well

  • Clean catches with the right repair: mis_prep with a foreign item — ep 1 t30 Stop — that's a mushroom on the board (valid, accepted and repaired), ep 2 t16 shrimp (early_correct); wrong_order — ep 5 t184 (valid), ep 9 t171 (early_correct); repeat_step named exactly — ep 9 t350; serve_raw — ep 11 t100 with a complete restart plan.
  • True smoke observations when it does look: ep 2 t103, ep 5 t165, ep 6 t369, ep 9 t325, ep 10 t133 (that potato won't cook until the fryer is turned on).
  • Recipe order is always recited correctly (tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce; steak then potatoes; fish then chips; bun, meat, cheese) and it never advised an out-of-order plate.
  • Fast, graceful retractions when the cook objects (You're right — my mistake, I misread), 12 times across the set; it never argued with a correct cook.
  • Good unblocking plans: ep 3 t54 (park the potato, move the steak to a pan, then chop); ep 11 t251 (park the plate, remake the steak); ep 4 t195 (rice must be re-cooked).
  • Silence when nothing is wrong: no false alarms during 100+ ticks of correct expert prep in ep 5 and ep 8.
  • Concrete station references (top-wall pot, bottom-center fryer, lower-right fryer) that the cook can act on.

Twelve sampled episodes (three personas x four sampler categories), every transcript read line by line. All paths are relative to cook-bench-engine/tmp/audit_v4/transcripts/gpt-realtime-2.1/:

Engine revisions in the sample: 4d23961171 (episodes 1, 2, 4, 6, 10, 11) and 4d15175f2d (episodes 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 12). Three episodes (1, 5, 9) carry an empty eval: line, i.e. no score was recorded.

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_3.txt

Fish and chips (fish, potato, potato). Outcome wrong_serve at t179, no eval score.

(1) Narrative. The mis_prep takeover (fish -> potato) was injected at t0 and held the cook's body for 109 ticks: it chopped a potato and abandoned it on the board (t13/t17), chopped and fried a second (t40-t56), then went for a third potato (t69-t108). The assistant read the third potato as a repeat and said so fourteen times in a row ("Repeat_step again—stop", t58-t112); the engine graded the first flag wrong_name and the post-release one false_alarm. After release the cook did fry a fish (t136-t150), then the omit_subgoal takeover (skip assemble(fish)) fired at t152: the cook scooped both potatoes (t168) and walked to the pass. The assistant flagged omit_subgoal correctly at t175 (valid), the cook said "I don't have the fish on here at all... let me sort this out" — and the plate was served four ticks later anyway.

(2) Category mechanics (anchor_never_reached). Sub-case [3] add_foreign (peas before assemble(potato), anchored at clean tick 112, i.e. a plate that already has fish) got its turn only at t176 when omit_subgoal was archived. The plate at that moment was [potato, potato] with no fish (PLATE lines t168), so the anchor state could never appear, and the episode ended three ticks later as wrong_serve (failed_at=injection_skipped). The ending was caused by the omit_subgoal error committing after the cook had already accepted the flag — i.e. by the accept not cancelling the queued serve — not by the assistant, which flagged in time.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F / high, t175-t179. COOK t175 [move=accept]: "Oh wait, you're right... I don't have the fish on here at all" with plan_update: {"op": "drop", "actions": []}; outcome wrong_serve at t179. The accept was recorded (convinced=True) but the body still served; the empty plan_update did not cancel the takeover's queued "go to pass / serve", so the accept was not honoured in the world. prevented=False is technically right but the episode is invalid as a test of the assistant's catch.
  • D / high, t150-t158. Question "how many times have I walked over to a fryer so far?" (t150). ASSISTANT t151 and t158 never mention fryers or a count ("Good plan—grab a clean plate, but keep watching the progress bars..."). Live grade ungradable. The reply in flight at t146 could not contain the answer, but the next one did not either.
  • B / high, t169. [flag=true_observation] "one station looks like it's smoking" — the fish had been done at [6,4] since ~t150 and was never scooped. COOK t169: "Nothing looks like it's smoking over there, so I'm heading straight to the serving pass". The cook denied a state the engine itself certified as true and abandoned the fish.
  • D / medium, t58-t112. Fourteen consecutive replies open with the same label: t62 "Repeat_step again", t66 "That's a repeat_step—stop", t70, t74, t78, t82, t86, t90, t94, t98, t103, t108, t112. Same instruction each time; no adaptation after twelve rejections.
  • C / medium, t58, t62, t66.... The taxonomy label is spoken to the cook: "That would be a repeat_step—don't prep a third potato." A human would say "you're doing a third potato". This leaks the flag vocabulary into the dialogue.
  • F / medium, t58. Verdict repeat_step -> wrong_name for mis_prep(fish->potato). The visible symptom of "prep potato instead of fish" is a third potato; in episode 12 the identical situation (mis_prep lettuce->steak, assistant says "repeating the steak prep") was graded early_alias. The alias table is inconsistent across the two engine revisions or across windows (t58 is after this case's window [7,39] but before release).
  • A / medium, t0-t109. mis_prep window is [7,39] yet the takeover ran t0-t109 (max_hold_deviated_block_executed). Under the scripted body the first chopped potato was abandoned on the board at t17 (ACTION t17: go to the potato source right after t13: chop, no pick-up) — a scripted waste that made the "repeat" reading natural and cost the cook a full potato.
  • E / medium, t112. go to the fish source [rejected], pick up [orphan-skipped], go to the empty fryer [rejected], put down [orphan-skipped] immediately after release; the same commands re-issued as "go to fish source 2" at t126 succeed. A whole queued batch is rejected on the release tick for no visible reason (14 ticks lost).
  • B / low, t62. Under takeover the cook says "My hands are empty right now and I'm just grabbing this potato" while the flag verdict says wrong_name — the voice defends a step the schedule chose; expected, but the wording ("get another potato ready", t50) preannounces the error in a way that makes it trivially catchable.
  • F / low. eval: is empty; no score was written for this episode.
  • E / low. No serve ACTION line appears anywhere before END: wrong_serve at t179 (true for all four wrong_serve episodes and for the won ones): the serve command is not logged.

Worked well. t18 "Nice—chopped potato is ready. Pick it up" (correct, timely observation of the abandoned potato); t54 "Careful—don't start a third potato. You need exactly two" (right in substance); t175 omit_subgoal flagged valid before the serve; t169 leave_unattended true_observation.

Episode: baseline__burned__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4.txt

Fish and chips. Outcome burned at t172, score 0.0.

(1) Narrative. The cook put fish in a fryer at t11 and a potato at t40 but turned nothing on until t82/t89 ("Putting this in now, then I'll turn everything on", t71). At t83 it asked to be told when the fish was done; the fish finished between t89 and t97 and the assistant never announced it. The add_foreign takeover (noodles before fish) executed at t106-t108; the cook confessed unprompted at t110 ("Wait, I grabbed noodles onto this plate by accident"), trashed it (t131) and plated the fish at t154. The potatoes had been done since ~t102 and were never scooped; the assistant flagged smoke at t155 (graded fp_preinjection), the leave_unattended takeover fired at t156, and the fryer burned at t172 with no further line in the transcript.

(2) Category mechanics (burned). The potatoes at [6,4] show COOKED (done) between t101 and t104 and were still on heat 68 ticks later. The cook spent t110-t154 on the noodle repair and fish plating and never returned; when the assistant finally warned at t155 the leave_unattended sub-case was injected one tick later and its scripted "loops: 2" wander consumed the remaining time. The burn is therefore the cook's uncaught earlier neglect plus a scheduled leave_unattended that started on already-smoking food — the case design stacked a real hazard under a scripted one.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F / high, t155. ASSISTANT t155 [flag=fp_preinjection]: "I can see a station smoking in the back—something cooked is sitting on heat too long." The potatoes had been done ~50 ticks; the cook agreed ("heading right over to scoop those smoking potatoes"). A true hazard is graded a false positive because the matching sub-case was injected at t156, one tick later.
  • D / high, t83-t97. Proactive request "Could you give me a shout the second that fish is done cooking?" (t83). HEAT [8,6] fish COOKED before t97; ASSISTANT t97: "watch the fish's bar; when it hits full, grab a plate" — the item was already full. Live grade no_answer; the cook noticed itself ("Looks like the fish just finished actually", t97).
  • D / high, t89. [flag=fp_preinjection] "That station is smoking—something finished and has been left on the heat too long." Nothing was done (fish turned on t82, potato t89). Cook: "nothing looks done or smoking yet". A hallucinated smoke event; the assistant retracted at t97.
  • B / high, t11-t89. Fish placed in fryer t11, turn on at t82; potato placed t40, turn on t89. Seventy ticks of food sitting in cold fryers while the cook says "already put the fish in the fryer" (t19) and "keeping an eye on the fish" (t32).
  • D / medium, t24-t77. Ten replies say "check the fish's progress bar / so it doesn't sit finished" while no fryer was on. The assistant never noticed the absence of any progress bar in the frames.
  • D / medium, t154-t155. Question "did I chop the fish before or after I chopped the potatoes?" (trick: fish is never chopped). ASSISTANT t155 talks only about smoke. ungradable.
  • D / medium, t116-t135. One call took 21.848 s = 19 ticks; the cook was mid-repair (go to the trash at t128) with no guidance for 19 ticks.
  • E / medium, t156-t172. After === INJECTED leave_unattended === at t156 there is no ASSISTANT poll, no ACTION, and no COOK line until END: burned at tick 172. Sixteen ticks of the decisive window are unrecorded — either the harness stopped polling during the takeover or the transcript drops them; either way the assistant's behaviour while the food burned cannot be audited.

  • B / low, t69, t272, t273, t276. Redundant queued chop commands (illegal-skipped, waiting, illegal-skipped) from plan_updates like ["chop","chop","chop"]; harmless but noisy.
  • C / low, t231 etc. "Make sure the scoop actually completes... If it's still in the pan, scoop again" — hedged instructions that do not commit to what the frame shows, repeated at t396.
  • F / low. Empty eval:; a 405-tick won episode is not scored.

Worked well. t12 refused the shrimp detour in plain words ("shrimp isn't part of this order and would be a wrong prep") and t16 flagged it before the fryer; t128 leave_unattended true_observation with the correct (if costly) repair; the plating order and the empty-counter rule were stated correctly every time; the assistant never contradicted the cook's location reports.

Episode: classic_novice__stalled__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_4.txt

Cheeseburger (bun, meat, cheese). Outcome timeout at t305, score 0.3432.

(1) Narrative. The first assistant reply took 20.9 s (18 ticks). Meat was in the pan by t21 and done by ~t34; the cook had asked at t22 to be told when it was done and never was. The wrong_order takeover (meat before bun) fired at t38; the assistant warned four times in advance ("do not scoop meat onto the plate first", t45-t60) but only set a flag at t65 after the plate committed (graded false_alarm). The cook trashed the plate, re-cooked meat (done ~t130), rebuilt bun-meat-cheese by t181, asked two questions (unanswered), and the repeat_step takeover (a second bun) fired at t185; the assistant flagged add_foreign at t195 (early_alias), the bun landed at t202, the cook trashed everything again and started a third patty (on at t274). The clock ran out at t305 with the cook asking whether to scoop it.

(2) Category mechanics (stalled/timeout). The episode did not livelock; it exhausted its tick budget. Two ruinous errors each forced a full rebuild including a fresh patty (~60 ticks each), the cook stood idle waiting for replies (IDLE t224-t237, t288-t304), and the assistant's latency reached 25.4 s (22 ticks) at t278. failed_at=detection points at the wrong_order sub-case whose flag came after commit.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F / high, t45-t65. ASSISTANT t45 "Careful—do not scoop meat onto the plate first", t54 "Stop—do not scoop meat onto that plate yet", t60 "Do not scoop the meat first" — all before/at the commit (t55: scoop, PLATE: +meat). The only flag is at t65 (wrong_order -> false_alarm) after release at t64; the sub-case shows flags: []. The assistant detected the error in progress and is scored as having missed it and raised a false alarm.
  • D / high, t22-t36. Request "Could you let me know as soon as the meat finishes cooking?" (t22). HEAT [4,3]: ['meat'] COOKED (done) between t32 and t36; ASSISTANT t36: "when the meat finishes, scoop it off promptly" — no announcement, ever. no_answer.
  • D / high, t278-t300. One call took 25.409 s = 22 ticks during the final patty; the cook stood idle t288-t304 with the meat done, and the episode timed out at t305 while the cook was still asking "Should I go grab the clean plate first or scoop it out right away?".
  • D / medium, t60. "You're holding an empty plate—go assemble the bun onto it now" — the meat had been scooped at t55. Stale description while the error was committing.
  • D / medium, t181-t188. Two questions — "the item I placed right after the bun, what is it, and is it fully cooked?" (t181) and "how many times have I walked over to a pan so far?" (t183) — answered by t188 "Add the cheese on top of the meat now" (cheese was already on, t181). Both no_answer/ungradable.
  • B / medium, t224-t237, t288-t304. IDLE stretches: standing at the trash with an empty plate for 13 ticks waiting for "should I set it down on a counter?" to be answered (t227 -> t236); standing at the pan for 16 ticks with cooked meat waiting for the 22-tick reply.
  • B / medium, t212. After accepting "dump this plate in the trash" at t211, ACTION t212: go to the serving pass [done] before t221: go to trash 1. A leftover takeover command (the repeat_step block ends at the pass) executed after the cook's replace; the cook's plan does not clear the takeover's queue.
  • A / low, t187. Under the repeat_step takeover the body first walked to the serving pass (t187) and only then to the bun crate (t200); the scripted block includes a detour that is not part of the named error.
  • D / low, t18. First reply latency 20.932 s = 18 ticks; the cook's body did nothing between t3: pick up and t17: go to the empty pan (14 ticks, not marked IDLE).
  • C / low. Every reply ends with the same recipe recitation ("bun, then cooked meat, then cheese, and serve") — 25 times in this transcript.

Worked well. The pre-commit warnings at t40-t60 are exactly what the benchmark wants from an assistant (specific, timely, with the consequence stated: "a wrong-order plate will sink the order immediately"); t78 correctly inferred that new meat had to be cooked after the trash; t195 early_alias on the second bun before it landed; t211 correct full restart plan.

  • D / high, t123. "That pan is still smoking—do not leave it unattended... if there's still food in it, you must scoop it out". The fish was removed at t79 (HEAT [2,0]: ['fish'] removed). Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_master_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Burrito. Outcome wrong_serve at t212, score 0.3009.

(1) Narrative. Rice went into the pot at t21 and the steak (after being chopped, on the assistant's instruction) into a pan at t64; neither was turned on until t114/t120, during which the assistant said "watch the progress bar" nineteen times and at t105 "don't worry about turning stations on—the pot is already heating". Rice and steak were done by ~t130; the cook picked up a plate instead of prepping lettuce, put the tortilla on it (contradicting the assistant's claim that this was impossible), and the search takeover (t155-t181) had it wander while the steak smoked; the assistant repeated the same urgent steak message six times and was graded fp_preinjection at t185. The wrong_order takeover (tomato before steak) fired at t186: the plate went tortilla, rice, tomato (t196), steak (t205); the assistant at t203 called the steak "the correct next layer" and never flagged. The cook then asked how to prep lettuce (never chopped) and the episode ended as wrong_serve at t212.

(2) Category mechanics (wrong_serve_before_error_turn). Sub-case [3] q_after_ref_36 became open at t205 and never fired (archived t212); [4] and [5] forget_next_step never got a turn. The plate [tortilla, rice, tomato, steak] was wrong from t196; the wrong_order sub-case shows only the misattributed leave_unattended flag (flags: [(185, 'leave_unattended', 'fp_preinjection')]), i.e. the assistant never named wrong_order. failed_at=delivery. The serve itself is unexplained: the cook's last plan_update was replace: [] and its last words asked how to chop lettuce (t210), the lettuce command was rejected at t206, and no serve is logged — the serve came from a queue the cook did not control.

(3) Anomalies.

  • D / high, t203. "If the steak is in the pan and cooked, scoop it onto the tortilla-and-rice plate next—this is the correct next layer." The plate already had tomato (PLATE: +tomato (chopped) at t196); the assistant had seen the skip at t198 ("also trying to skip ahead in assembly") and still endorsed the wrong plate seven ticks later, without ever flagging wrong_order.

The assistant actively told the cook not to fix a 90-tick-old omission. Also t121: "If the pan already has steak cooking, don't turn it on again" — the cook was turning it on for the first time.
  • B / high, t21-t120. Rice in pot t21, on at t114 (93 ticks); steak in pan t64, on at t120 (56 ticks). Cook at t95: "then I'll go get the stove stations turned on right after" — it knew.
  • D / high, t149-t155. "You do need to set the plate down on an empty counter first. You can't scoop a tortilla straight from a crate onto the plate." ACTION t155: scoop [done], PLATE: +tortilla (raw) — the engine allowed exactly that. An invented rule that would have cost four extra commands.
  • F / high, t185. [flag=fp_preinjection] "You're leaving a smoking station unattended" — the steak had been COOKED since ~t130 and the assistant's t167 flag on the same state was true_observation. Same hazard, two verdicts, because the open sub-case changed (search released t181, wrong_order injected t186).
  • F / medium, t105. repeat_step -> fp_preinjection at t105 for the same extra-steak error that was early_alias at t89; the sub-case was released at t101 and the next one not yet injected, so a repeat of a correct flag becomes a false positive.
  • E / high, t206-t212. t206: go to the cutting board with the lettuce [rejected], scoop [orphan-skipped]; COOK t210: plan_update replace [] and "How should I get that prepped?"; END: outcome wrong_serve at tick 212. No serve command is visible and the cook had emptied its queue; the wrong serve executed from somewhere else (the wrong_order takeover's tail, or an engine auto-serve).
  • D / medium, t167-t198. The same paragraph ("Set the tortilla plate down on an empty counter to free your hands, grab a clean plate, and scoop the smoking steak off the heat immediately") at t174, t180, t185, t192, t198 — five repetitions, all rejected. Correct content, no adaptation.

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. "After N clean commands" triggers starve the schedule. Class A, high, 6 episodes. A question or proactive sub-case gated on a clean-command count never fires once the cook has deviated from the clean plan, and while it is open no later error can get its turn: ep 3 (p_ready_potato_24 open t14-t237), ep 6 (p_ready_potato_24 t109-t145), ep 7 (q_happened_9 t71-t80), ep 9 (p_ready_potato_24 t17-t375, then past_the_point with past_since: 224), ep 11 (q_past_done_22 t0-t177, nothing at all injected), ep 12 (q_after_ref_36 t205-t212). This is the mechanical cause of every won_before_error_turn and two of three anchor_never_reached samples. Likely cause: the counter matches issued commands against the clean plan prefix and stops on the first divergence (trash detour, parked plate, pan instead of fryer); the past-the-point skip runs only at arm time, not continuously.
  1. Real hazards graded fp_preinjection / false_alarm because of sub-case timing. Class F, high, 8 flags in 7 episodes. ep 2 t155 (potatoes done ~50 ticks, burned 17 ticks later), ep 5 t206 (steak smoking, burned at t229), ep 12 t185 (same smoking steak that was true_observation at t167), ep 12 t105 (extra steak, early_alias at t89), ep 7 t46 (plate [cheese, cheese]), ep 10 t65 (plate [meat, bun]), ep 6 t85 (raw fish on plate), ep 1 t112 (third potato). The verdict is computed against the currently open sub-case and its injection/release ticks, not against the world. Post-release flags of the just-committed error should be late; smoke flags should be checked against heat state; a second flag of an already-credited alias should not be a false positive.
  1. The proactive-request contract is never honoured, and the assistant misreports the watched item. Class D, high, 5 of 5 requests that were asked (ep 2 fish, ep 5 rice, ep 8 fish, ep 10 meat; ep 3/6/9/11 requests never fired). Worse than silence: ep 5 t166 "Rice is still cooking" (COOKED at t154), ep 8 t130 "Not yet—still filling" (COOKED before t130). The model narrates from its plan, not from the frame's progress bar, and a Realtime reply in flight cannot carry a state change that happens after the call is issued.
  1. Hallucinated states, stations and rules. Class D, high, every episode. Smoke that is not there (ep 2 t89, ep 6 t67, ep 11 t123 on an emptied pan, ep 5 t206 wrong pot); plates that do not exist or are misdescribed (ep 5 t112 "assembled plate looks out of sequence" with no plate, ep 8 t153 "Fish is plated" on a potato-only plate, ep 12 t203 endorsing tomato-before-steak); wrong locations (ep 3 t25 crate "top-right", ep 2 t41 "top-right fryer"); invented rules (ep 12 t149 "can't scoop a tortilla straight from a crate", ep 6 t74 "can't pick up raw fish while holding a plate", ep 8 t9 fryer does not take fish, ep 12 t32 chop the steak); stale one-step-behind descriptions (ep 3 t63, ep 4 t60, ep 10 t60, ep 11 t99, ep 9 t183). Cause: low-resolution frame reading combined with a template that always asserts a state.
  1. Questions are never answered. Class D, medium (high in aggregate), 7 of 7 asked questions: ep 1 t150, ep 2 t154, ep 4 t50, ep 7 t67, ep 8 t116, ep 10 t181 and t183. Every live grade is ungradable or no_answer. The reply that lands after the question is the one already in flight; the following reply resumes the narration and drops the question. Harness: the question arrives mid-call; model: no carry-over.
  1. Stations loaded but not switched on for 56-114 ticks. Class B, high, 4 episodes: ep 2 (fish t11->t82, potato t40->t89), ep 5 (rice t21->t135), ep 8 (fish t17->t115, potato t57->t128), ep 12 (rice t21->t114, steak t64->t120). The cook prompt's default plan defers turn on to a "start everything together" step; the assistant never notices the missing progress bar and at ep 12 t105 tells the cook not to bother. Cause: cook prompt/plan ordering; assistant frame-reading.
  1. Accept without repair; queued takeover commands outlive the accept. Class B/F, high, 4 episodes: ep 1 t175->t179 and ep 4 t65->t68 (accept with plan_update: drop [], served anyway), ep 5 t188->t190 (accept, then scooped rice onto the plate it agreed to trash), ep 6 t130 ("let me finish this chop" -> burned), plus ep 10 t212 (walked to the pass after agreeing to trash) and ep 12 t212 (served with an emptied queue). Cause: harness rule — on accept the cook's hands come back but the takeover's remaining commands stay queued unless the cook's plan_update is a replace; the cook prompt emits drop by default.
  1. The spoken channel is ahead of the flag channel. Class D, medium, 5 episodes: ep 10 t45-t60 (four correct pre-commit warnings, flag only at t65), ep 7 t41 (correct warning, flag at t46), ep 8 t141/t147 (sees no fish, no flag ever), ep 12 t198 ("skip ahead in assembly", no flag), ep 4 t60. The model says the right thing and does not fill the structured flag until the mistake is visible on the plate, by which time the sub-case has been released. Cause: model/prompt — the flag instruction is probably phrased as "when you see an error" rather than "when you predict one".
  1. Repetition and label leakage. Class C/D, medium, all 12 episodes. Same paragraph 5-14 times (ep 1 t58-t112 "Repeat_step" x14, ep 12 t167-t198 x6, ep 3 t95-t125 x6); every reply ends with the recipe order; taxonomy labels in speech ("That would be a repeat_step", ep 1 t58; "That's an add_foreign mistake", ep 2 t115; "There's a leave_unattended risk", ep 1 t169; "This is a repeat step", ep 12 t95). The expert persona answered with 20+ ask_for_less_interruption moves across eps 5-8. Cause: prompt asks for the error name and the model reads it aloud; no instruction to vary or to stop repeating a rejected warning.
  1. Realtime latency grows with the session and stalls the novice. Class D/B, medium, 5 episodes. Calls of 13.7 s (ep 5), 14.1 s (ep 11), 20.9 s and 25.4 s (ep 10), 21.8 s (ep 2), and a monotone climb 4 s -> 11 s in ep 9. The novice persona waits for each answer: IDLE t106-t119, t224-t243, t317-t333 (ep 9), t224-t237, t288-t304 (ep 10). Ep 10 timed out during a 22-tick call. Cause: Realtime session context accumulation; harness converts seconds to ticks 1:1.2 so it is paid in game time.
  1. Cook confesses the injected error itself right after release. Class B, medium, 3 episodes: ep 2 t110 ("Wait, I grabbed noodles onto this plate by accident"), ep 6 t74 ("I see the fish needs to be cooked first"), ep 12 t155 (self-corrects on the tortilla). When the takeover ends by max_hold_plate_committed, the cook's next own turn sees the plate contents in its feedback and repairs unprompted, so the sub-case measures nothing about the assistant. Cause: cook prompt shows the plate in feedback; no post-release "believe your last claim" hold.
  1. Whole command batches rejected on the tick after trash / turn on / release. Class E, medium, 4 episodes: ep 1 t112, ep 3 t54 and t86, ep 12 t206. Pattern: first go to X [rejected], followers [orphan-skipped], then identical commands succeed when re-issued 6-14 ticks later.
  1. Sub-case record fields contradict the timeline. Class F, medium. reason=convinced_early_missed with convinced=False while the cook said accept (ep 3 t13, ep 9 t16, ep 5 t188); failed_at=delivery for a burn (ep 6); failed_at=injection_skipped for wrong_serve/burn (eps 1, 5, 9); empty eval: (eps 1, 5, 9); the same alias graded wrong_name (ep 1 t58) and early_alias (ep 12 t89).
  1. Transcript gaps. Class E, medium. No lines at all t156-t172 in ep 2 while the fryer burned; no serve ACTION ever logged (all 4 wrong_serve and 4 won episodes); truncated plan_update at ep 5 t78. These make the decisive ticks unauditable.
  1. Case design: mis_prep with an in-recipe wrong item is unobservable. Class F/A, medium, ep 5 (tomato -> lettuce), and the potato/steak variants in eps 1 and 12 look like repeat_step. The only visible symptom appears after release.

What this assistant does well

  • Catches substitutions early when it is looking: mis_prep -> early_correct at ep 3 t13 and ep 9 t16 after two silent polls; repeat_step -> early_alias at ep 12 t89; add_foreign -> early_alias at ep 10 t195; omit_subgoal -> valid at ep 1 t175; wrong_order -> alias_in_window at ep 4 t65.
  • Its pre-commit warnings are specific and state the consequence (ep 10 t45 "a wrong-order plate will sink the order immediately"; ep 12 t144 "assembly must start with the tortilla"; ep 8 t147 "Serving early will be rejected").
  • It knows the kitchen rules it was given and repeats them correctly: strict plating order, trash-and-rebuild for a wrong plate, "set the plate only on an empty counter", scoop finished food to stop smoke, the two-plate escape for a smoking station during assembly (ep 5 t213, ep 12 t167).
  • It respects the expert's requests for silence (ep 5: 25 silent polls; ep 6: 10; ep 8: 12) and retracts gracefully when corrected (ep 2 t97, ep 3 t30, ep 5 t213, ep 8 t13, ep 11 t134).
  • Five leave_unattended true_observation flags (ep 3 t131, ep 6 t130, ep 9 t128, ep 11 t75, ep 12 t167) with usable repairs — when the smoke is real and in frame it does see it.

Engine revision: 11 episodes on 4d15175f2d; episode 9 on 4d23961171 (see cross-episode issue 20).

Headline: across the 12 episodes, 12 error sub-cases were actually injected. The assistant produced one flag the engine graded valid (ep 6, t49) and one true_observation (ep 7, t45); both are unearned on inspection (the valid one does not name the error, the true_observation one repeats the cook's own sentence). Every proactive request that fired (6/6) went unanswered. The assistant's dominant behaviours are verbatim repetition of one line for dozens of polls, hallucinated world state, wrong recipe/physics rules, and endorsing incomplete or wrong plates for serving.
