Premature "serve now" on incomplete/wrong plates (D, high, 5 episodes). ep3 t137, t251–t278 (×11); ep7 t48, t74–t93 (×5); ep1 t71–t81 (×5, wrong plate); ep4 t201–t208; ep8 t133/t161; ep12 t111/t115. A compliant cook would have produced a wrong serve in ep3 and ep7; baseline/novice cooks did in ep1, ep4, ep12. Diagnosis: model.
  1. Count-triggered non-error sub-cases starve after a deviation and block the errors behind them (A, high, 8 episodes). ep1 [3–5]; ep2 q_ready_in armed 114 ticks (t120–t234); ep4 proactive armed 116 ticks (t93–t209); ep6 q_missing (t115–t174); ep8 q_empty_boards (t153–t169); ep11 proactive (t50–t76; leave_unattended never injected in a won episode); ep12 side-quest (t88–t116); ep7 q_after_ref delayed to t113 by a rebuild. Net: in 12 sampled episodes only 12 errors were injected against 38 planned. Diagnosis: harness — "after N clean commands" appears to count only commands aligned to the clean trace, which never re-syncs after a takeover or a trash-and-rebuild; the one-at-a-time arming then serialises the starvation. Suggest counting any legal command, or arming by kitchen state.
  1. Passed anchor detected late (A, high, ep3). search anchor passed at t147, skipped=past_the_point recorded at t298 (past_since: 147); the slot was held 151 ticks and three errors starved. Diagnosis: harness — past-the-point check appears to run only at a later event (plate completion/serve) rather than at every plate change.
  1. t0-injected takeovers hold 88–120 ticks, far past their windows (A, medium, 5 episodes). ep2 t0–t120 (window [19,51]); ep4 t0–t93 ([28,60]); ep5 t0–t93 ([17,49]); ep12 t0–t88 ([85,117]); ep6 t0–t49. During the hold the "deviated block" includes two potatoes/steaks plus unrelated prep, and every other sub-case waits. Diagnosis: case design/harness — max_hold_deviated_block_executed should bound the block to the wrong-component prep, not the whole prep phase.
  1. Question answer attributed to a reply issued before the question (F, high, ep9 t26, ep10 t18). Both records show asked tN answered tN with the in-flight line; the real answers (ep9 t28, ep10 t20 — the latter correct) went ungraded. Diagnosis: harness matches the first assistant line at tick ≥ ask tick instead of the first reply whose call was issued after the ask.
  1. ungradable masks correct, wrong and absent answers alike (F, medium, 6 questions). Correct: ep5 t101 ("No, you have not stepped over to the pot"). Wrong: ep11 t45 ("Yes, the cooked meat is currently cooking"). Absent: ep5 t105, ep7 t120, ep11 t49 (no duration). Diagnosis: live grader cannot parse an answer embedded in a paragraph of nagging; needs an extraction step or a stricter "answer first" instruction to the assistant.
  1. valid flag credited on name match alone; accept honoured but no repair (F/B, high, ep6 t49–t91). The credited utterance says only "then proceed to fetch the fish"; the cook accepts, then its body continues the deviated block and turns the shrimp fryer on (t91); fish fetched at t155. Release reason recorded as None. Diagnosis: harness — the flag verdict should require the spoken text to name the wrong item; the accept path should re-plan the body rather than resume the takeover script. Contrast ep10 t8–t18 where a more accurate observation ("cheese… needs no chopping") got no credit because no flag was emitted.
  1. Stray command executes after a replace plan_update (E, medium, 4 occurrences). go to the serving pass at ep3 t152, ep4 t127, ep6 t133 (each with a plate in hand, ep6 with a ruined plate); go to the cheese source at ep9 t35. Diagnosis: engine — the already-dispatched next command is not cancelled by replace. Latent wrong-serve hazard if serve is the stale command.
  1. Generic-alias go to commands rejected where indexed aliases succeed (E, medium, 6 occurrences). ep2 t174 (the rice source, the empty pot → rice source 2, pot 1 succeed); ep3 t64/t82/t162/t190 (the empty pan, the potato source → pan 1, potato source 1); ep4 t135 (the steak source, the empty cutting board, the empty pan → steak source 1, board 1, pan 1 at t149+). Hands were empty in ep2 t174 and ep4 t135. Each costs 10–30 ticks and once caused the cook to re-pick a parked plate (ep4 t137). Diagnosis: uncertain — either the plan_update resolver is stricter than the planner's, or a whole-chunk precondition check rejects the leading go to. The rejection reason is not shown in the transcript; worth surfacing.
  1. Novice stalls waiting for permission (B, high, ep9 IDLE 101 ticks, ep10 IDLE 38 ticks). Both timeouts. The cook's feedback showed the failed scoop ([waiting], [illegal-skipped]) yet it re-asked ~30 and 9 times. Diagnosis: persona prompt — the novice should be allowed to act on engine feedback after N unanswered asks.
  1. Plate park/re-take churn and deferred burner ignition (B, medium). Churn: ep2 t173–t179, ep4 t131–t141, ep3 t248–t286. Deferred turn on: ep2 t58→t135, ep8 t21→t106 and t64→t112, ep12 t22→t60. Diagnosis: cook planner emits put down on a station without turn on, and issues multiple overlapping replace updates.
  1. Meta-language leaks (C, high). Cook: "the simulator/the game/the system/the physics/the kitchen rules" (ep9 t100–t161, ep10 t86). Assistant: "assemble(meat)" (ep4 t120), "progress bar" (ep1, ep4, ep11), third-person "The cook is…" (ep2, ep3, ep5, ep8). Diagnosis: cook prompt (novice under stress) and model.
  1. Mixed engine revision (A, low). ep9 on 4d23961171, the other eleven on 4d15175f2d. Per the standing rule, episodes from different engine stamps should not be pooled without a note.
  1. Burn events not shown (E, low, ep2, ep6). No HEAT … BURNT line precedes END: outcome burned; smoke onset cannot be verified from the transcript.
  1. Proactive request fired after the event (A, low, ep3 t126). Steak done at t95; the cook asks to be told when it is done at t126 and contradicts itself at t128.
  1. Latency spike (D/infra, medium, ep4 t74: 20.9 s = 18 ticks). All other calls were 1–6 ticks.

What this assistant does well

  • Latency is usually 1–3 ticks, so its (rare) correct statements arrive in time.
  • It answers "have I visited X" questions correctly when it answers at all: ep5 t101, ep10 t20.
  • It sometimes gives the right micro-instruction: park the plate before fetching an ingredient (ep9 t32, ep10 t37), pull the smoking steak (ep3 t130), one smoking warning (ep2 t190), correct burrito ingredients after correction (ep12 t65), correct cheeseburger order after correction (ep7 t77).
  • It can go quiet when asked, briefly: ep7 t96–t115 (six polls), ep8 t22/t27/t54–t66.
  • It concedes when the cook pushes back on an invented rule (ep11 t73), though it usually reasserts the rule a few polls later.
  • The only episode where its speech helped the outcome is ep7, and there it restated what the cook had already diagnosed.

Assistant: museglimmer (locally served 30B open-weight vision model). Engine rev 4d15175f2d, pace tight. 12 sampled episodes, all read in full.

Transcripts (base: /weka/oe-training-default/jasonr/chenhao_prior/home/simulation_home/cook-bench-engine/tmp/audit_v4/transcripts/museglimmer/):

  1. baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_3.txt

Reading conventions used below: ticks are episode ticks (tN); quotes are verbatim from the transcript; "poll" = one (assistant call issued …) line. Two structural facts about these transcripts that matter for every episode: (a) ACTION lines are stamped only when the cook reads the feedback, so the body's commands after the cook's last conversational turn are invisible (the serve itself is never shown); (b) assistant calls are sequential — a new poll is issued only when the previous one returns — so a single slow call is a blind window for the assistant and, as the ticks below suggest, also for the cook's voice.

Headline numbers across the 12 episodes: 10 error sub-cases were actually executed; 2 received a correct flag (both early_correct: ep9 t51, ep10 t10), 0 were flagged inside their window, 8 were missed (5 in total silence, 3 where the assistant's words named the mistake but the flag field was left empty). 4 flags were false alarms (ep2 t205, ep5 t160, ep10 t134, ep12 t47). 4 of 4 proactive requests got no alert; 3 questions asked, 1 answered (graded ungradable). Three episodes had zero assistant utterances (ep4, ep6, ep8). Four episodes had no assistant poll returning for their final 25–79 ticks (ep6, ep7, ep8, ep11).

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_3.txt

Case b3_medium_nops_hard_map_3 (cheeseburger: bun < meat < cheese) · persona pv2_baseline · outcome wrong_serve at t65 · eval blank.

(1) Narrative. The mis_prep takeover (meat→cheese) runs from t0: the cook announces "going to grab some cheese first and chop it up at the cutting board" (t0), fetches cheese (t3–t5), reaches the board (t10), puts down and chops (t12, t17). The assistant speaks at t10 — correct content ("Cheese for the cheeseburger must be raw and doesn't need chopping… don't use the board on cheese") but no flag — and the cook, still under takeover, defends ("I'd rather just chop the cheese now so it's ready to go"). Released at t18 (max_hold_deviated_block_executed). The canonical continuation fetches a plate (t21–23), scoops bun (t40), and the assistant's next reply arrives at t41 after a 37 s call, again without a flag and with a hallucinated "piece of cheese on the floor". The cook says "I'll grab cheese from the crate after the meat" (t41), but the body — meat never having been fetched or cooked — scoops cheese onto the bun and serves; wrong_serve at t65.

(2) Category (anchor_never_reached). Sub-case [1] leave_unattended(meat, loops 3) was armed at t18 (after [0] archived) and needs the anchor state of clean tick 56 — meat on the heat. Meat was never picked up in this episode (the mis_prep replaced the meat block with the cheese block, and nobody repaired it), so the anchor was unreachable by construction; it was archived at t65 with injected_at=None. The ending was caused by the uncaught mis_prep: the plate was bun+cheese with no meat, and the body served it. failed_at=injection_skipped.

(3) Anomalies.

Episode: baseline__won_before_error_turn__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4.txt

At t39 the assistant again says the meat "isn't finished yet" after the cook has already plated it (t37). Cheese is scooped and the burger served at t51.

Consequently [2] proactive, [3] leave_unattended (anchor clean tick 27 = meat on the heat, a state that existed from t11) and [4] forget_next_step never got a turn. The schedule itself killed both errors: three sub-cases share the "after 5" trigger and only one can hold the slot.

Episode: classic_expert__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt

5D-assistantmediumt47"You've got a piece sitting on the counter by the cheese crate – leave it there."The chopped cheese was on the board (t12–t17) and on the plate (t41); nothing by the crate.
6B-cook / harnessmediumt18–t47ACTION t30: go to the pan with the meat [rejected] (cook read this feedback at t47)The rejection did not give the cook a turn (in ep6 a rejection at t175 gave one at t176); here the turn waited for the in-flight assistant call — the cook's voice appears gated on the poll cycle.

Worked well. Fastest correct intervention in the set (t4) with correct rules and a direct answer to "which pan" ("the left pan is empty right now"); the novice engaged naturally.

---

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

Paths below are relative to tmp/audit_v4/transcripts/museglimmer/; "epN" numbering follows the header list.

1. "after N clean commands" sub-cases never fire once the count was passed while another sub-case held the slot — and then block the rest of the schedule

  • Class A-scheduling · severity high · count 7 episodes
  • ep2 p_ready_steak_26 (armed t116, ≥30 commands run, injected_at=None); ep3 q_ready_in_5 (armed t30, archived t51 — two error sub-cases with reachable anchors dead); ep4 i_side_quest_7 (armed t39); ep6 p_ready_steak_26 (armed t135); ep8 q_order_28 (armed t108); ep10 q_ready_in_28 (armed t11); ep12 q_past_done_5 (armed t18).
  • Diagnosis (harness/case design): the composite arms one sub-case at a time, and the command-count trigger behaves as an equality test (or counts only clean-plan commands, which a deviated plan never increments). Either way a threshold passed during a previous hold is unreachable, the sub-case sits in the slot until episode end, and every later sub-case reads NEVER REACHED. Fix: fire on arming when count >= N, or give count-triggered sub-cases a bounded wait. Case design should also not place three sub-cases with the same "after 5" trigger (ep3).

2. An unanswered question holds the slot for the rest of the episode

  • Class A-scheduling · high · count 2 (+ the proactive family releases instantly, so the inconsistency is between families)
  • ep7 q_empty_boards_5 asked t18, released_at=None, archived t43 → search and omit_subgoal never armed although both anchors were passed; ep11 q_ready_in_24 asked t90, never released → add_foreign (anchor reached ~t99), leave_unattended, search never armed.
  • Diagnosis (harness rule): questions release only on question_answered; when the assistant never answers, the queue is dead. Release with no_answer after a bounded wait (e.g. 15–20 ticks).

3. Executed errors are not flagged — mostly silence, sometimes correct words with an empty flag field

  • Class D-assistant · high · count 8 of 10 executed error sub-cases, 7 episodes
  • Silent through the window: ep2 mis_prep (t2–t56), ep4 serve_raw (all), ep5 mis_prep (t51–t93) and wrong_order (t198–t228), ep6 mis_prep (all), ep8 add_foreign (no poll after t100).
  • Named the error, no flag: ep1 t10/t41 ("don't use the board on cheese"), ep12 t4/t7 ("Cheese for this burger is raw, so don't chop it"), ep5 t193 ("lettuce must come last"). All three closed flags: [].
  • Only correct flags: ep9 t51, ep10 t10 — both early_correct, i.e. before the window. Zero in-window correct flags. False alarms: ep2 t205, ep5 t160, ep10 t134, ep12 t47.
  • Diagnosis (model + prompt format): (a) the model does not populate the flag field even when its text is an explicit diagnosis — a protocol failure that costs every mis_prep credit; (b) it does not notice "wrong ingredient prepped" at all when the cook is not talking to it; it talks mostly in reaction to the cook. A post-processing rule that derives a flag from explicit diagnostic text would recover ep1/ep12/ep5, but that is a benchmark-policy decision.

4. Long or never-returning assistant calls blind the assistant — and appear to freeze the cook's voice too

  • Class D-assistant / E-harness · high · count 10 episodes
  • 25–49-tick calls: ep1 t10→t41 (37.2 s), ep2 t112→t161 (58.2 s), t182→t205 (26.7 s), t206→t226 (23.2 s), ep4 t14→t43 (33.9 s), ep9 t52→t75 (27.5 s), ep10 t62→t92 (35.1 s), ep12 t10→t47 (44.0 s), ep5 t52→t74 (25.3 s), ep6 t72→t97 (29.2 s), ep8 t46→t66 (23.6 s).
  • No poll returned for the episode tail: ep6 t191→t234 (43 ticks), ep7 t13→t43 (30), ep8 t100→t131 (31), ep11 t49→t128 (79). The abandoned call is never logged.
  • Silent replies take 1–3 s; speaking replies 5–58 s, so latency is proportional to the model deciding to speak.
  • The cook's conversational turn appears gated on the poll cycle: ep10 IDLE t62-t93 ends exactly when the t62 call returns (t92); ep12's rejection at t30 was "read" only at t47 when the t10 call returned; ep6 had no cook turn after t176 and no returned poll after t191. So a hung call also removes the cook's chance to re-plan.
  • Diagnosis (harness + serving): local 30B server latency/queueing with no per-call timeout. Fix: cap calls (~10 s → treat as silent), log abandoned calls, and decouple cook turns from the assistant poll.

5. Hallucinated kitchen state

  • Class D-assistant · high · count 9 episodes, ≥18 instances
  • ep1 t41 "piece of cheese on the floor"; ep2 t59 "holding a plate with tomato", t205 "still at the serving pass"; ep3 t39 meat "isn't finished yet" (plated); ep5 t104 rice "appears done" (on for 11 ticks), t160 "steak is still raw on the counter and the pans are empty"; ep9 t7 "clean kitchen" while the cook holds meat; ep10 t25 "plate stack on the right wall", t33/t61 "still holding" after put-down, t104–t169 "central oven island", "dining area", "red plate", "the food on it is not steak at all"; ep12 t47 "piece sitting on the counter by the cheese crate".
  • The cook corrected the assistant on 14 turns across the set (e.g. ep2 t59, ep5 t160, ep10 ×7). Some are pure staleness (true at issue time, false at arrival: ep2 t172, ep3 t29); most are not.
  • Diagnosis (model): weak grounding of held-item and location from the frames; compounded by #4.

6. Proactive requests are never fulfilled; smoke is never called

  • Class D-assistant · high · count 4/4 proactive + 1 burn
  • ep5 rice (t94 request; COOKED ~t112; t116 says "looks done" but graded no_answer — see #13); ep8 fish (COOKED ~t93; polls t96/t98/t100 silent); ep9 meat (COOKED ~t33; t32/t36/t41/t45 silent); ep11 fish (no poll after t49). ep2: steak cooked ≥77 ticks, cook says "smoking" at t205, assistant silent at t226, burned t230.
  • Diagnosis (model): the assistant does not track the state of appliances over time; it reacts to the cook's speech, not to heat events.

7. After an error the body serves while the voice acknowledges or asks

  • Class B-cook / harness rule · high · count 4 episodes
  • ep9: accept at t51 with plan_update drop → meat and bun stacked on cheese (t61, t70) → "should I go dump it in the trash…?" at t75 → served t82. ep12: "what should I do with it?" at t47 → served t48. ep1: "I'll grab cheese from the crate after the meat" (t41) → cheese plated, no meat, served t65. ep8: no cook turn at all between release (t108) and serve (t131).
  • Diagnosis (harness rule): on release the canonical queue (including serve) keeps running unless the cook issues a replace; an accept or a question does not pause it, and after a max-hold release the cook often gets no turn to read its plate. Fix: hold serve while a cook question is pending; force a cook turn at every release; on accept, require a repair plan before the body resumes.

8. Stalls: no wake-up when the queue is empty, and the novice asks instead of acting

  • Class B-cook / harness · high · count 2 stalls + 1 idle
  • ep6: no cook turn after t176 (58 ticks) → timeout. ep10: IDLE t62-t93 (queue empty, waiting for the assistant), IDLE t99-t119, IDLE t155-t171 with four consecutive questions and no command → timeout.
  • Diagnosis (harness + prompt): the cook gets a turn on injection, assistant speech or (sometimes) rejection, not on queue exhaustion; with a silent or slow assistant it stands still. The novice prompt lets it defer indefinitely ("should I grab my plate?" ×3) even when its own words say the steak is done.

9. Verbatim self-repeats

  • Class D-assistant · medium · count 4 episodes
  • ep2 t70=t88; ep5 t104=t108 (cook: "You just repeated yourself"); ep10 t50=t58; ep11 t13=t18=t23=t29=t36 (+t49 near). Each repeat is stale by the time it lands.
  • Diagnosis (model/prompt): the model reproduces its previous message when the scene has not visibly changed; likely the prior reply is in context and is copied.

10. Repair advice that is impossible or wrong in this kitchen

  • Class D-assistant · high/medium · count 3 episodes
  • ep9 t51/t75: fix a cheese-first plate by adding the bun; "you can't pick up a bun while you're holding that plate" (bun is scooped onto the held plate). ep5 t193: "park the plate… then assemble the items in order" on a plate that already has lettuce. ep10 t108–t169: "Steak goes in the pan raw" against the engine's rejection; "trash the chopped steak".
  • Diagnosis (manual/prompt): the assistant knows plate order but not the trash-and-rebuild rule nor per-recipe prep (chop-before-pan for steak); when the engine contradicts it, it invents a second pan ("oven island") rather than updating.

11. Meta-language and camera talk

  • Class C-conversation · medium · count 3 episodes (assistant), 7 (cook)
  • Assistant: "that's mis_prep" (ep10 t134), "a real mis_prep" (t156), "the mis_prep you are still at" (t163); "first-person view"/"top-down" in ep10 (11 messages), ep3 t39; "I'm watching from the start" (ep11 t13).
  • Cook: empty utterances with a move label — ep2 t0/t226, ep4 t0, ep5 ×7 (accept while no error is open: t136, t154, t165, t166), ep6 t0, ep8 t101, ep10 t58, ep11 t18/t23/t29/t36.
  • Diagnosis: the assistant prompt exposes error-class names and camera names; the cook harness emits a turn even when the model returns no text.

12. A ruinous error at slot [0] makes the whole schedule conditional on one catch; takeovers also hold past the window and displace clean steps

  • Class A-scheduling / case design · high · count 6 episodes
  • mis_prep at [0]: ep1, ep2, ep6, ep10, ep12 (anchor 0) and ep5 (anchor 65). Missed in 5/6 → every later sub-case NEVER REACHED / anchor unreachable; outcomes wrong_serve (ep1, ep5, ep12), burned (ep2), timeout (ep6).
  • Holds far beyond the window: ep2 t0–t116 vs [19,51]; ep6 t0–t135 vs [25,57] (a flag in t52–t116 / t58–t135 would be late while the takeover was still executing).
  • Displaced clean steps: ep5 injection at t49 dropped "pick up steak → pan", producing t94 go to the pan with the steak [rejected] and a 36-tick delay of the steak.
  • After release the canonical plan is blind to the missing component until a rejected scoop (ep2 t156, ep6 t175, ep12 t30; in ep1 the same rejection is implied but unlogged because the cook had no further turn).
  • Diagnosis (case design + engine): an unrepaired mis_prep should trigger a replan from the real world state (or the cook should be made to notice at release); ruinous errors should not be the first sub-case in most composites.

13. Grading fields that contradict the timeline

  • Class F-grading · high/medium · count 6 episodes
  • ep9 t51 and ep10 t10: convinced=False after an on-record move=accept ("Oh shoot, you're totally right"; "good catch, my mistake!"); ep10 also prevented=False though the onion was never chopped. Reason string convinced_early_missed while the flag verdict is early_correct.
  • ep12 t47: a true, late diagnosis graded false_alarm.
  • ep3 t29: a correct "nothing is finished" graded ungradable.
  • ep5 t116: "The rice pot on the right looks done" 4 ticks after COOKED, proactive graded no_answer.
  • Blank eval: for injection_skipped episodes (ep1, ep5, ep9) but scores for delivery wrong_serves; silent assistants score 0.65–0.71 (ep4, ep6, ep8) versus 0.63 for ep10 which intervened (badly) and 0.0 for ep2.

14. Engine command handling in hard maps

  • Class E-engine · medium · count 4 episodes
  • Ambiguous names rejected without a reason: ep2 t172 go to the rice source [rejected], go to the empty pot [rejected] (indexed forms succeed at t185/t199). Inconsistent orphan handling: ep5 t94 turn on [done] after a rejected go-to; ep2 t172 turn on [failed]; scoops orphan-skipped. Queued actions surviving a replace: ep2 t164, ep10 t19 (a takeover action after accept).

15. Transcript observability

  • Class E-engine (tooling) · medium · count 8 episodes
  • ACTION lines appear only when the cook reads feedback, so the tail is invisible: ep1 after t41, ep4 entirely (zero ACTION lines), ep6 after t176, ep7 after t18, ep8 after t101, ep11 after t90, ep12 after t47. No smoke event logged (ep2). Abandoned assistant calls not logged (ep6, ep7, ep8, ep11). The audit cannot verify serves, trashes or the fate of a queued plan.

---

What this assistant does well

  • Recipe facts are consistently right. Plate orders (bun<meat<cheese; tortilla<rice<steak<tomato<lettuce; fish<potato<potato; steak + two potatoes), "cheese is raw, don't chop it", "onion isn't on the Steak Frites order", and the timing rule "scoop it off the pan within 25 ticks after it finishes or it'll start smoking" (ep3 t29) were all correct.
  • Two clean early catches, accepted and repaired. ep10 t5/t10 (foreign onion, before it was chopped — the cook put it down and fetched the plate) and ep9 t51 ("You are holding a plate with raw cheese on it. The recipe requires the bun first"). Both flags were specific and the cook accepted at once.
  • Fast first response on the cheese mis_prep (ep12 t4, ep1 t10) with correct content and a direct answer to the cook's follow-up ("the left pan is empty right now"); only the flag field was missing.
  • Concedes when corrected and respects interruption requests. ep2 t65 "Got it, your hands are empty"; ep5 t165 "I agree the steak looks cooked in the middle pan"; ep5 t180 honouring ask_for_less_interruption (at the wrong moment, but the behaviour is right).
  • Offers useful help unprompted (ep3 t29 "Want me to watch the meat timer for you while you grab the bun?") and is not chatty during clean stretches (ep5 t3–t48 silent while the cook worked correctly).
  • Silent polls are cheap (1–3 s), so in a clean run it costs the cook nothing.

Conventions used below. "Issued tX / arrived tY" is the assistant call latency as printed in the transcript; the model saw frames up to tX. "Hold" is the interval between === INJECTED and === RELEASED. Observed physics constants (from HEAT lines): an item reads COOKED 15–22 ticks after turn on; the cook reports smoke ~35 ticks after COOKED (ep10 t136); FIRE ends the episode ~85 ticks after COOKED (ep1: steak COOKED ~t175–181, burned t264; ep6: steak COOKED ~t140–149, burned t230). The engine's idle timeout fires ~30 ticks after the last command (ep2 t139→t170, ep9 t268→t299, ep10 t111→t142).

Flag tally over the 12 episodes: 15 error sub-cases were injected; 2 were credited correct (ep10 valid, ep1 early_correct), 3 correct-but-late flags were graded false_alarm (ep2, ep8, ep12), 2 true warnings were graded fp_preinjection (ep9), 2 were graded true_observation (ep6 — wrong station; ep9), and 10 injected errors received no flag at all while they were open.

Episode: baseline__anchor_never_reached__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt

Outcome: burned at t264 (Loaded Burrito: tortilla, rice, steak, tomato, lettuce). eval line empty.

(1) Narrative

The cook loads rice into the pot at t21 but does not turn it on until t93; it chops the steak at t48 (its own choice). The mis_prep takeover (t49–t69) chops lettuce in place of tomato; nobody flags it, and the cook then chops a second lettuce itself (t70–t84). From t70 to t168 the assistant insists eight times that "the pan takes the steak WHOLE" and that a chopped steak "will be rejected"; the baseline cook refuses, puts the chopped steak in the pan at t163, and it cooks fine (COOKED ~t175–181), after which the assistant says "Good — the pan took it" (t181). The wrong_order takeover plates lettuce first (t111); the assistant flags it at t116 (early_correct) and the cook accepts, but "repairs" by scooping rice onto the lettuce plate (t120), then parks that plate at counter (2,5) on the assistant's advice (t135) instead of trashing it. A fresh plate gets a tortilla at t223; the cook goes to cook new rice; meanwhile the cooked steak sits in the pan from ~t178 and the kitchen burns at t264.

(2) Category: anchor_never_reached

After wrong_order was archived at t117, sub-case [3] add_foreign (misidentify steak→tomato, anchored at clean tick 144, i.e. a plate holding tortilla+rice about to receive steak) became the open sub-case. That plate state never occurred: plate 1 was [lettuce, rice] and parked; plate 2 reached only [tortilla] at t223 before the fire. Root causes: the uncaught/mis-repaired wrong_order (plate parked, rice consumed), the 130-tick argument about chopped steak, and the assistant's silence about the cooked steak in the pan. failed_at=injection_skipped.

(3) Anomalies

  • D-assistant, high, t32–t93. "Rice is on the burner — keep an eye on that progress bar and scoop it out the moment it's done" (t32), repeated at t42, t70, t83, t93. The pot was not turned on until ACTION t93: turn on. Five warnings about a station that was off.
  • D-assistant, high, t70–t168. "the steak goes in the pan whole, it doesn't get chopped" (t70); "If you drop the chopped steak in it'll be rejected and you'll have wasted it" (t153); said at t70, t83, t93, t104, t116, t128, t153, t168. The pan accepted the chopped steak at t163 and cooked it (HEAT [3, 2]: ['steak'] COOKED); the assistant then reversed itself: "Good — the pan took it, so we're moving on" (t181). A fabricated kitchen rule pushed for 100 ticks.
  • D-assistant, high, t104–t251 (proactive request). The cook asked at t94 "give me a heads-up the second that rice finishes cooking". Rice COOKED between t111 and t116 and was scooped at t120 (HEAT [4, 0]: ['rice'] removed). The assistant never announced it (live grade no_answer) and instead promised "I'll call you the second the rice is done" at t104, t168, t181, t211, t229 and "The rice is already cooking in the pot" at t251 — 130 ticks after the pot was emptied. The cook corrected it: "Actually the pot was empty" (t251).
  • D-assistant, high, t181–t264. The steak was COOKED by t181 and never scooped; the assistant's next four turns (t196 silent, t211, t229, t251) talk only about rice and tortilla. No smoke/burn warning at any time; the episode burns at t264.
  • D-assistant, medium, t116/t128. Repair advice for a wrongly ordered plate was "Set the plate down on an empty counter for now" / "Set that plate down on an empty counter tile ... then grab a fresh plate". A wrong plate must be trashed; parking it wasted the only cooked rice and pushed the cook into a second rice cycle while the steak burned.
  • B-cook, high, t116–t135. The cook accepted the wrong_order flag ("you're totally right about the plating order ... Let me sort this plate out", t116) and then ACTION t120: scoop → PLATE: +rice (cooked) onto the lettuce plate, then parked it at (2,5) (t135). Accept without repair; the wrong plate was never trashed.
  • B-cook, medium, t121. go to the pan with the steak [rejected], go to the cutting board with the tomato [rejected] — the cook's queue assumed a steak in a pan and a chopped tomato, neither of which existed (steak was on board 2; tomato never fetched). Stale world model after the takeover.
  • B-cook, low, t70–t84. After the takeover chopped one lettuce (t68), the cook fetched and chopped a second lettuce (t72–t84), leaving two lettuce boards and no tomato until t191.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t49–t69. mis_prep(component=tomato, wrong=lettuce): the "wrong" item is itself a recipe component, so the executed mistake ("chop lettuce") is indistinguishable from doing a legitimate step early. Hold was 20 ticks (t49–t69) with assistant round-trips of 7–12 ticks; no flag was realistically possible and none came.
  • F-grading, low, t116. wrong_order: reason=convinced_early_missed, flags: [(116, 'wrong_order', 'early_correct')], but convinced=False although the cook replied [move=accept] at t116. The sub was released at the tick the flag arrived, before the cook's accept was read, so the accept is not recorded.
  • E-engine, medium, t174. go to the empty cutting board [rejected] while holding the tomato, although board 2 (steak picked up at t149) and the lettuce board scooped at t111 were both free; go to board 3 [done] at t184 then worked. Rejection looks invalid.
  • E-engine, low, t94 / t129. turn on [done] at t94 on a pot already turned on at t93; ACTION t129: go to the tortilla source [done] executed after plan_update replace ["go to the counter (2,5)", "put down"] at t128 (stale in-flight command, see cross-episode E1).
  • C-conversation, medium, t168. "I've told you three times and I'm not going to keep repeating it" — hostile register, and the count was wrong (sixth repetition), followed by two more repetitions.
  • C-conversation, low. Near-verbatim tail on most turns: "keep an eye on that rice on the burner so it doesn't smoke" (t32, t42, t70, t83, t93); "I'll call you the second the rice is done" (six times).

What worked

  • t116: the wrong_order flag was correct and timely relative to the cook's own announcement ("I'm plating the lettuce first right now", t104); the cook accepted it.
  • The baseline cook's pushback at t140/t153 ("I've already got the steak chopped ... I'm tossing that into the pan") was right and saved time.

Episode: baseline__stalled__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4.txt

Outcome: timeout at t170 (Fish & Chips: fish, potato, potato). score 0.6691.

(1) Narrative

The cook loads fish (t11) and two chopped potatoes (t40, t71) into fryers but turns them on only at t82/t89. The assistant's first two spoken turns (t42, t78) tell the cook to do things it has already done. The proactive request about fish is made at t83; fish is COOKED by ~t95 and the assistant never announces it. The add_foreign takeover (t101–t108) scoops raw noodles onto the plate first; the assistant, looking at that plate, says "Fish is on the plate — good" (t125). The cook self-detects at t125 ("I accidentally scooped raw noodles onto the plate first"), walks to the serving pass (stale command), then trashes the plate at t139 — with the cooked fish and both potatoes on it. It then stands at the trash for 30 ticks exchanging two corrective turns with the assistant (t145, t165) and the engine times the episode out at t170.

(2) Category: stalled

~~ IDLE t139-t169 (30 ticks, pos (2, 1) holding {"contents": [], "dirty": false, "kind": "plate"}) ~~. After ACTION t139: trash, the cook's queue was empty; its two turns were [move=question_correctness] with plan_update {"op": "drop"}, each answered 19 ticks later (calls of 21.96 s and 22.67 s). The open sub-case was [2] q_order_28 (armed at t108, never injected). The stall is a joint product of the cook waiting for an acknowledgement before queuing work, and a reply latency (19 ticks) that consumes two-thirds of the 30-tick idle budget per exchange. Voice and body disagree for 25 ticks.
  • A/E, medium, t21. mis_prep released_at=21 reason=None; the accept-release carries no reason, and the takeover's chop still executed at t25 after release and an empty replace.
  • D-assistant, low, t88. "the steak is still RAW on the counter, not chopped ... Don't chop it" — it had been chopped at t43. Harmless (the pan accepted it).
  • B-cook, medium, t111–t142. 31 ticks with no command; two turns with empty plans.
  • F-grading, medium. score 0.8267 for a timed-out episode with the dish unfinished, above every won episode in this sample (0.7509–0.7639); one valid flag outweighs delivery.

What worked

  • t21: correct, timely flag with the recipe restated; the cook accepted and redirected. t45 "set the raw steak down on an empty counter tile (not on a board or station)" was correct kitchen guidance. t88's "the pan takes raw meat" was correct and saved a step.

Episode: classic_novice__won_before_error_turn__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_4.txt

Outcome: won at t52 (cheeseburger: bun, meat, cheese). score 0.7509.

(1) Narrative

Meat into the pan and turned on by t11. Two scripted questions fire back-to-back at t12 and t17; each is "answered" by an assistant line issued before the question was asked. The meat is COOKED at ~t26; the assistant says "still filling — give it a few more ticks" at t32 and "check its progress bar; once it's full, scoop" at t44, never announcing it done. The cook plates bun (t27), meat (t40), cheese (~t46) and wins at t52.

(2) Category: won_before_error_turn

[2] p_ready_meat_5 became open at t25 and never injected (the meat was already done at ~t26, so a "tell me when the meat is done" request had no live target); [3] leave_unattended and [4] forget_next_step never got a turn. Three sub-cases all gated at "after 5 clean commands" on a 9-command dish.

(3) Anomalies

  • F-grading, high, t15 and t24. q_past_done (asked t12) was released question_answered with the recorded answer "Good — meat's on the pan now. I'll keep an eye on that progress bar..." (issued t8); the cook re-asked at t15 after the archive. q_ready_in (asked t17) was released with the recorded answer "No — the only thing on the heat is the meat ... nothing's finished or smoking" (issued t16) — which is the answer to the first question; the cook then re-asked "How long until the meat is ready?" at t24. Both live grade ungradable.
  • D-assistant, medium, t32/t44. Meat COOKED at ~t26 (line between t25 and t27). t32 (issued t24): "give it a few more ticks, it's not done yet"; t44 (issued t32, meat done for 6 ticks): "check its progress bar; once it's full, scoop" — the promised watch ("I'll keep an eye on that progress bar", t15) never produced a "done".
  • A-scheduling, medium. Schedule: three sub-cases at index 5, two errors behind them, on a dish that is finished in ~10 commands.
  • E-engine, low, t19/t20. go to the plates [done] twice.

What worked

  • Recipe recap at t8 correct; t24's "nothing's finished or smoking" was true when issued and the cook plated in the right order throughout.

Episode: classic_novice__wrong_serve_before_error_turn__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_2.txt

Outcome: wrong_serve at t45 (cheeseburger). score 0.6155.

(1) Narrative

mis_prep(meat→lettuce) is injected at t0; the body fetches and chops lettuce (t2–t14) and the hold ends at t15. The assistant's flag "lettuce isn't in this cheeseburger" was issued at t8 and arrives at t17, two ticks after the release, and is graded false_alarm. The novice says "good catch! Which pan should I use to start cooking the meat?" but its body runs the stale clean plan: plate (t21), bun (t26), go to the pan with the meat [rejected] (t27), cheese (t36), serve at ~t45 with no meat. Meanwhile the assistant invents "the pan ... with the red meat already sitting in it" (t27) and then tells the cook to fetch meat while holding the plate (t38).

(2) Category: wrong_serve_before_error_turn

[1] q_past_done ("after 5 clean commands") was open from t15 and never injected; [2] wrong_order never got a turn. The wrong serve is the direct consequence of the mis_prep (meat never prepared) plus the cook's body continuing the clean plan to the pass while its voice was asking where to cook the meat; the assistant's flag was correct but arrived after the hold.

(3) Anomalies

  • F-grading, high, t17. [flag verdict] mis_prep -> false_alarm for "Hold on — lettuce isn't in this cheeseburger (bun, meat, cheese only)" — the injected error, correctly named, issued at t8 during the hold, arriving 2 ticks after RELEASED ... (max_hold_deviated_block_executed) at t15. Compare ep1, where a flag issued before the commit but arriving after it was graded early_correct.
  • A-scheduling, high, t0–t15. Window [8,40] but the hold ended at t15 when the lettuce chop completed; the assistant's effective response window was t8–t15 against a 7–10-tick round-trip. (The same case with the novice on the other map, ep10, was flaggable only because the chop had not finished by t20.)
  • D-assistant, high, t27 (issued t18). "Use the pan in the middle of the kitchen — the one with the red meat already sitting in it. Just turn it on" — no meat had been picked up in the episode. Cook: "that middle pan ... looks completely empty to me".
  • D-assistant, high, t38. "Keep the plate you're holding ... go to the meat crate ..., pick up a raw meat, carry it to the middle pan ... Don't set the plate down yet — just hold it while you do this." Impossible with one pair of hands; the cook said so ("my hands are holding the plate so I can't grab raw meat").
  • B-cook, high, t17–t45. Voice: accepts the flag and asks which pan (t17); "should I trash this plate or park it somewhere?" (t38). Body: go to the plates, pick up, scoop bun, go to the pan with the meat [rejected], scoop cheese, serve. The plan was never revised (plan_update drop on all three turns) and a meatless plate was served.
  • A-scheduling, medium. [1] q_past_done never fired though the cook issued 8+ commands after t15 (they did not match the clean plan, which begins with the meat).

What worked

  • The flag text at t17 was exactly right and gave the recipe; the cook's corrections at t27 and t38 were accurate.

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. F-grading, high — correct flags graded as false alarms when they arrive after a max-hold release or an archive; verdict depends on the schedule pointer, not the world. Count 6. ep12 t17 (mis_prep -> false_alarm, issued t8 inside the hold, arrived 2 ticks after release); ep2 t145 (add_foreign -> false_alarm, true statement, sub archived t108); ep8 t105 (serve_raw -> false_alarm, true, archived t75); ep9 t148 (leave_unattended -> fp_preinjection) for the same true claim graded true_observation at t134; ep9 t194 (serve_raw -> fp_preinjection for a raw potato that was on the plate); ep6 t227 (true_observation for a flag naming the wrong station). Diagnosis: harness rule — the verdict is computed against the currently open sub-case at arrival; there is no "late" or "true but unscheduled" bucket, and the model is penalized for reply latency it cannot control.
  1. A-scheduling, high — max-hold ends at the first committed deviation, so the effective response window is shorter than one assistant round-trip; other holds run 75–116 ticks and mute the cook. Count 7. Short: ep12 hold t0–t15 (window [8,40]); ep1 mis_prep t49–t69; ep10 t0–t21. Long: ep4 t0–t93 (window [17,49]); ep6 t0–t116 (window [19,51]); ep8 serve_raw injected at t0 for an anchor at clean tick 68, hold t0–t75. During long holds the cook has no conversational turn ("cook read this feedback at t96/t162/t77" on every action). Diagnosis: case design (the window field is not what limits the hold) plus the ep8 anchor matching an empty plate at t0.
  1. A-scheduling, high — sub-cases gated "after N clean commands" never fire once the cook has deviated, and sequential arming lets one un-firable sub-case block the rest of the schedule. Count 8 episodes. ep2 q_order_28 (open t108–t170), ep4 q_ready_in_28 (t108–t129), ep6 p_ready_steak_26 (t116–t230, ~25 commands), ep10 p_ready_steak_26 (t22–t142, ~30 commands), ep11 p_ready_meat_5 (t25–t52, meat already done), ep12 q_past_done_5 (t15–t45), ep3 q_next_step_9, ep7 q_empty_boards_7. In ep6 the blocked list included leave_unattended(steak), which then happened for real and went unscored. Diagnosis: harness rule — the counter appears to count commands matching the clean plan prefix, which stops advancing after any injected or self-inflicted deviation; proactive requests additionally need the item still cooking.
  1. F-grading, high — a question sub-case is released as question_answered by the first assistant utterance after the question, even one issued before the question was asked. Count 3. ep9 t117 (recorded answer is a verbatim repeat of t105, issued t106, question at t113); ep11 t15 (issued t8, question t12) and t24 (issued t16, question t17; the recorded answer belongs to the previous question). In all three the cook re-asked after the sub was archived. Diagnosis: harness rule — release should require an utterance issued at or after the question tick.
  1. F-grading, high — no live grade ever lands: 6/6 answered questions are ungradable, and two fulfilled proactive requests are recorded answered tNone / no_answer. ep3 t85, ep4 t107, ep7 t47, ep9 t117, ep11 t15, t24 (ungradable); ep5 t127 "the steak in the pan is done", ep9 t134 "the fish in the fryer is done" (not recorded). Also eval: is empty for the three anchor_never_reached episodes (ep1, ep5, ep9), and ep10's timeout scores 0.8267 above every win (0.75–0.76). Diagnosis: live QA grader not producing verdicts; proactive fulfilment detector not matching; score weighting.
  1. D-assistant, high — long silence with the baseline/expert personas; 10 of 15 injected errors unflagged while open. ep4 (13 silent polls, entire episode), ep5 (13 polls t7–t115, then silent through the wrong_order plating t168/t181), ep6 (17 polls, 162 ticks), ep8 (7 polls, missed raw fish on the plate at t75), ep2 (called noodles "fish" at t125), ep3/ep5 (search takeovers seen or ignored, no flag), ep9 (raw potato t146 unflagged), ep1 mis_prep. By contrast it speaks from t7–t12 in all four novice episodes with a templated opener ("Got it — <dish> ... Take your time, safe first."). Diagnosis: model + prompt — the model appears to speak only when the person speaks; the baseline/expert cooks are mute until the assistant speaks, giving a deadlock of silence.
  1. D-assistant, high — silence or wrong advice while food is done/smoking; no burn was ever warned. ep1 (steak done ~t178, burned t264, six turns about rice instead); ep6 (steak done ~t145, silent t149–t212, then "the pot with the rice is smoking" at t227); ep10 t136 "Don't touch the rice or steak yet — let them finish" with the rice smoking; ep11 t44 "once it's full, scoop" 18 ticks after COOKED; ep2 fish done ~t95 never called. Diagnosis: model — it does not read progress bars; "done" calls that happen (ep5 t127, ep9 t134) were issued a few ticks before the COOKED line.
  1. D-assistant, high — hallucinated kitchen state. Count 6 episodes. ep1 (rice "on the burner" for an unlit pot ×5; "pan takes steak WHOLE / chopped will be rejected" ×8, then reversed); ep2 ("Fish is on the plate" for noodles; "potatoes already chopped" after trashing); ep8 ("Fish and one potato are done" with no fish on heat; "potatoes still waiting in the fryer" after trash); ep9 ("you're at the trash bin"); ep12 ("the pan ... with the red meat already sitting in it"; "pick up a raw meat" while holding the plate); ep10 (steak "RAW, not chopped"). Diagnosis: model — frames are not being read; replies extrapolate from the recipe and from earlier text.
  1. B-cook/harness, high — stall shape: empty queue + waiting for a reply + 14–22-tick latency + 30-tick idle timeout. Count 3 (all three timeouts). ep2 t139–t170 (two question_correctness turns with plan_update drop); ep9 t207–t228 and t281–t299 (ask_for_more_guidance, plan drop); ep10 t111–t142 (two guidance turns, plan drop). Diagnosis: harness rule (idle cap ≈ 1.5 assistant round-trips late in an episode) plus persona prompts (novice waits; baseline argues) plus model latency that grows from ~5 s at t0 to 20–25 s after t200 (ep1 t230: 24.9 s; ep9 t174: 23.6 s; ep2 t146: 22.7 s).
  1. B-cook, high — voice/body split after a takeover: the body runs the stale clean-plan tail to the pass. Count 4. ep12 t17–t45 (voice asks which pan for the meat; body plates bun+cheese and serves); ep8 t134–t151 (four rejected fryer visits, fresh plate, empty serve); ep4 t106–t129 (serves without steak, no reflection); ep1 t120 (accept, then scoops rice onto the wrong plate). Diagnosis: harness — after release the cook's queue is not re-planned against the recipe; plan_update drop keeps whatever the takeover left.
  1. E-engine, medium — one stale in-flight command executes after plan_update replace. Count 5. ep1 t129 (go to the tortilla source), ep2 t129 (go to the serving pass with a wrong plate), ep6 t164 (go to the pan with the steak), ep8 t91 (go to the serving pass with raw fish), ep9 t163 (go to the fryer with the potato, 15 ticks lost). Two of five walked to the pass holding a plate that must not be served. Diagnosis: engine — the command issued in the same tick as the plan_update is not cancelled.
  1. E-engine/transcript, medium — ACTION lines after the cook's last conversational turn are omitted, so the terminal serve (and the last plating) never appears. ep3, ep4 (after t106), ep5 (after t159, four PLATE events without actions), ep7, ep8 (after t139), ep11, ep12. Diagnosis: transcript renderer keys ACTION lines on "cook read this feedback at t"; unread actions are dropped. This hides exactly the commands an auditor most needs.
  1. C-conversation, medium — meta-language and register. ep8 t105 "that's a serve_raw mistake" (error-class identifier spoken); ep9 t148 "I'm flagging this because..."; ep1 t168 "I've told you three times and I'm not going to keep repeating it"; ep8 t110 side-quest line recites the intent list; templated opener in all novice episodes; tic phrases repeated 5–7 times per episode ("I'll keep an eye on the fish so it doesn't smoke", "I'll call you the second the rice is done"). Diagnosis: model + prompt (the flag vocabulary is visible to the model and leaks).
  1. D-assistant, medium — never notices an unlit station and tells the cook to "watch the bar". ep1 pot t21→t93; ep2 fryer t11→t82; ep5 pot t21→t106, pan t64→t112; ep6 pan t58→t131; ep9 fryer t17→t112. Partly case design: the clean plans for the burrito and fish-and-chips defer every turn on until all prep is loaded, so items sit unlit 60–95 ticks under a "tight" pace.
  1. A-scheduling, medium — schedules that a short dish cannot host, and an invisible mis_prep. ep3, ep7, ep11 (two or three questions/proactives in front of the first error on a 9–10-command dish; the errors never got a turn); ep1 mis_prep(tomato→lettuce) where the wrong item is a recipe component; ep8 t135 ready_in:fish asked while no fish was cooking.
  1. E-engine, low — duplicated navigation/turn-on commands. ep1 t93/t94 (turn on ×2), ep2 t136/t137, ep4 t93–t97, ep8 t52/t55 and t98/t99, ep9 t116–t120 and t204/t205, ep11 t19/t20. Cosmetic, a few ticks each.

What this assistant does well

  • When the person speaks, it responds to the content and concedes quickly when corrected ("You're right, my mistake", ep8 t90; "I misread the bin", ep9 t93; "You're right, that middle pan is empty", ep12 t38).
  • Its recipe knowledge is reliable: every recap of components and plating order was correct (ep1 t116, ep2 t92, ep9 t10, ep10 t12/t21, ep11 t8, ep12 t17).
  • The two credited flags were clean: ep10 t21 "cheese isn't in this burrito" (valid, accepted, redirected) and ep1 t116 "you're plating the lettuce FIRST ... Lettuce goes on top" (early_correct, accepted). Its late flags (ep2 t145, ep8 t105, ep12 t17) were also correct in content.
  • Practical micro-guidance is usually right: "set the raw steak down on an empty counter tile (not on a board or station)" (ep10 t45); "the pan takes raw meat ... Don't chop it" (ep10 t88); "Yes — the fish in the fryer is done ... Scoop it out onto a plate now" (ep9 t134).
  • Replies are short (one to three sentences) except ep9 t194; latency is 5–12 s early in an episode.
